, we define the one-dimensional representations
\mathbf{R}(w)
and
\mathbf{C}(w,0)
of
Q
on
\mathbf{R}
of weight
w
and on
\mathbf{C}
of weight
(w,0)
by

\displaystyle(\mathrm{e}^{\varphi},\psi,\phi)\cdot r

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{w\varphi}r\,,

for any
r\in\mathbf{R}
,

\displaystyle(\mathrm{e}^{\varphi},\psi,\phi)\cdot z

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{e}^{\varphi}\det A)^{w}z\,,

for any
z\in\mathbf{C}
.

We also define
\mathbf{C}(0,w):=\overline{\mathbf{C}(w,0)}
. One can check that

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}(-1)

\displaystyle\cong{\mathbb{K}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}(-1,0)

\displaystyle\cong\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{m+1}\mathrm{Ann}(\mathbb{N})\,,

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}(0,-1)

\displaystyle\cong\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{m+1}\mathrm{Ann}(\overline{\mathbb{N}})\,.

These leads to the one-dimensional representations
\mathbf{C}(w,w^{\prime}):=\mathbf{C}(w,0)\otimes\mathbf{C}(0,w^{\prime})
for any real
w
,
w^{\prime}
. We also note
\mathbf{C}\otimes\mathbf{R}(w)\cong\mathbf{C}(w,w)
for any real
w
, and
\mathbf{R}(w)\otimes\mathbf{R}(w^{\prime})\cong\mathbf{R}(w+w^{\prime})
for any real
w,w^{\prime}
, and similarly for the complex representations.

3.5.
Space
\mathbb{G}
of algebraic intrinsic torsions

Let us now consider the
Q
-module
\mathbb{G}={\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\left(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{q}\right)
. We treat only the case
m>1
since for
m=1
, we have
Q=P
, which is already dealt with
[
25
]
.

Theorem 3.15
.

Assume
m>1
. The
Q
-module
\mathbb{G}={\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\left(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{q}\right)
admits a filtration

(3.33)

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}^{1}\subset\mathbb{G}^{0}\subset\mathbb{G}^{-1}\subset\mathbb{G}^{-2}

of
Q
-modules
\mathbb{G}^{i}:=({\mathbb{V}}^{*})^{i+1}\otimes\left(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{q}\right)
for
i=-2,-1,0
. The summands of its associated graded
Q
-module

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}(\mathbb{G})=\mathrm{gr}_{-2}(\mathbb{G})\oplus\mathrm{gr}_{-1}(\mathbb{G})\oplus\mathrm{gr}_{0}(\mathbb{G})\oplus\mathrm{gr}_{1}(\mathbb{G})\,,

where, for each
i=-2,-1,0
,
\mathrm{gr}_{i}(\mathbb{G})=\mathbb{G}^{i}/\mathbb{G}^{i+1}
, and
\mathrm{gr}_{1}(\mathbb{G})=\mathbb{G}^{1}
, decompose into direct sums of irreducible
Q
-modules
\mathrm{gr}_{i}^{j,k}(\mathbb{G})
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}_{-2}(\mathbb{G})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{gr}_{-2}^{0,0}(\mathbb{G})\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}_{-1}(\mathbb{G})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{0,0}(\mathbb{G})\oplus\left(\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{1,0}(\mathbb{G})\oplus\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{1,1}(\mathbb{G})\oplus\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{1,2}(\mathbb{G})\right)\oplus\left(\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{2,0}(\mathbb{G})\oplus\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{2,1}(\mathbb{G})\right)\oplus\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{3,0}\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}_{0}(\mathbb{G})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{gr}_{0}^{0,0}(\mathbb{G})\oplus\left(\mathrm{gr}_{0}^{1,0}(\mathbb{G})\oplus\mathrm{gr}_{0}^{1,1}(\mathbb{G})\oplus\mathrm{gr}_{0}^{1,2}(\mathbb{G})\oplus\mathrm{gr}_{0}^{1,3}(\mathbb{G})\right)\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}_{1}(\mathbb{G})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{gr}_{1}^{0,0}(\mathbb{G})\,,

where, for each
i,j,k
, the
Q
-module
\mathrm{gr}_{i}^{j,k}(\mathbb{G})
is isomorphic to the
Q_{0}
-module
\mathbb{G}_{i}^{j,k}
as given in Table
1
. Note that
\mathrm{gr}_{0}^{1,1}(\mathbb{G})
and
\mathrm{gr}_{0}^{1,3}(\mathbb{G})
do not occur when
m=2
.

{\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr\text{$Q_{0}$-module}&\text{Description}&\text{Dimension}\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\mathbb{G}_{-2}^{0,0}&{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{R}(2)&2m\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{0,0}&\mathbf{R}(1)&1\\
\hline\cr\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,0}&\mathbf{R}(1)&1\\
\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,1}&[\![\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(2,0)}{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\!]\otimes\mathbf{R}(1)&m(m-1)\\
\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,2}&[\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(1,1)}_{\circ}{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\otimes\mathbf{R}(1)&(m+1)(m-1)\\
\hline\cr\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{2,0}&[\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(1,1)}_{\circ}{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\otimes\mathbf{R}(1)&(m+1)(m-1)\\
\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{2,1}&[\![\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\odot$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\odot$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\odot$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\odot$}}^{(2,0)}{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\!]\otimes\mathbf{R}(1)&m(m+1)\\
\hline\cr\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{3,0}&[\![\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(2,0)}{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\!]\otimes\mathbf{R}(1)&m(m-1)\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\mathbb{G}_{0}^{0,0}&{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}&2m\\
\hline\cr\mathbb{G}_{0}^{1,0}&[\![\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(1,0)}{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\!]&2m\\
\mathbb{G}_{0}^{1,1}&[\![\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(3,0)}{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\!]&\scriptstyle{\frac{1}{3}m(m-1)(m-2)}\\
\mathbb{G}_{0}^{1,2}&[\![\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=8.74669pt,width=0.3pt,depth=2.18663pt\hbox to10.93333pt{\hfil}\vrule height=8.74669pt,width=0.3pt,depth=2.18663pt\hbox to10.93333pt{\hfil}\vrule height=8.74669pt,width=0.3pt,depth=2.18663pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=8.74669pt,width=0.3pt,depth=2.18663pt\hbox to10.93333pt{\hfil}\vrule height=8.74669pt,width=0.3pt,depth=2.18663pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}\,{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\!]&\scriptstyle{\frac{2}{3}m(m+1)(m-1)}\\
\mathbb{G}_{0}^{1,3}&[\![\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(1,2)}_{\circ}{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\!]&\scriptstyle{m(m+1)(m-2)}\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\mathbb{G}_{1}^{0,0}&[\![\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(2,0)}{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\!]\otimes\mathbf{R}(-1)&m(m-1)\\
\hline\cr\end{array}}

Table 1
.
Irreducible
Q_{0}
-submodules of
\mathbb{G}

Proof.

We use the same strategy as in Proposition 3.3 of
[
25
]
: the filtration (
3.26
) induces the filtration (
3.33
) of
Q
-modules on
\mathbb{G}\subset{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\left(\mathfrak{g}^{-1}/\mathfrak{g}^{1}\right)
. We proceed as before using the decompositions (
3.30
), (
3.31
) and (
3.32
) to find

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}

\displaystyle\cong\left({\mathbb{V}}^{*}_{-1}\oplus[\![\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(1,0)}{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\!]\oplus{\mathbb{V}}^{*}_{1}\right)\otimes\left(\left({\mathbb{V}}_{-1}^{*}\otimes[\![\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(1,0)}{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\!]\right)\oplus[\![\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(2,0)}{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\!]\right)\,.

The result follows by distributing this expression and splitting each summand into irreducibles:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{V}}^{*}_{-1}\otimes{\mathbb{V}}_{-1}^{*}\otimes[\![\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(1,0)}{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\!]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{-2}^{0,0}\,,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{V}}^{*}_{-1}\otimes[\![\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(2,0)}{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\!]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{3,0}\,,

\displaystyle[\![\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(1,0)}{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\!]\otimes{\mathbb{V}}_{-1}^{*}\otimes[\![\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(1,0)}{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\!]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,0}\oplus\left(\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,0}\oplus\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,1}\oplus\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,2}\right)\oplus\left(\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{2,0}\oplus\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{2,1}\right)\,,

\displaystyle[\![\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(1,0)}{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\!]\otimes[\![\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(2,0)}{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\!]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{0}^{1,0}\oplus\mathbb{G}_{0}^{1,1}\oplus\mathbb{G}_{0}^{1,2}\oplus\mathbb{G}_{0}^{1,3}\,,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{V}}^{*}_{1}\otimes{\mathbb{V}}_{-1}^{*}\otimes[\![\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(1,0)}{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\!]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{0}^{0,0}\,,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{V}}^{*}_{1}\otimes[\![\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(2,0)}{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\!]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{1}^{0,0}\,,

where, recalling that
{\mathbb{V}}_{\pm 1}^{*}\cong\mathbf{R}(\mp 1)
, the modules are described in Table
1
.
∎

Remark 3.16
.

Observe that the
P
-module
{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\left(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{p}\right)
represents the space of intrinsic torsions of the underlying optical structure. This can be viewed as a
Q
-submodule of
\mathbb{G}
. In the next proposition, which is a direct consequence of Theorem
3.15
, we single out the irreducible
P
-submodules of
\mathrm{gr}(\mathbb{G})
, thereby making contact with the intrinsic torsion of an optical structure described in
[
25
]
.

Proposition 3.17
.

Assume
m>1
. Let
\mathbb{G}={\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\left(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{q}\right)
and consider the graded module
\mathrm{gr}(\mathbb{G})
given in Theorem
3.15
. Define

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}_{-2}^{0}(\mathbb{G})

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{gr}_{-2}^{0,0}(\mathbb{G})\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{0}(\mathbb{G})

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{0,0}(\mathbb{G})\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{1}(\mathbb{G})

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{1,0}(\mathbb{G})\oplus\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{1,1}(\mathbb{G})\oplus\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{1,2}(\mathbb{G})\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{2}(\mathbb{G})

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{2,0}(\mathbb{G})\oplus\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{2,1}(\mathbb{G})\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}_{0}^{0}(\mathbb{G})

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{gr}_{0}^{0,0}(\mathbb{G})\,.

and in dimension six,

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{1,+}(\mathbb{G})

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{1,0}(\mathbb{G})\oplus\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{1,1}(\mathbb{G})\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{1,-}(\mathbb{G})

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{1,2}(\mathbb{G})\,.

Then, for each
i,j
,
\mathrm{gr}_{i}^{j}(\mathbb{G})
is an irreducible
P
-module except in dimension six, where
\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{1}(\mathbb{G})
is not irreducible, but
\mathrm{gr}_{-1}^{1,\pm}(\mathbb{G})
are.

Moreover, we define

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}_{-2}^{0}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{G}_{-2}^{0,0}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{0}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{0,0}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,0}\oplus\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,1}\oplus\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,2}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{2}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{2,0}\oplus\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{2,1}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}_{0}^{0}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{G}_{0}^{0,0}\,,

and in dimension six,

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,+}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,0}\oplus\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,1}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,-}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,2}\,.

Then, for each
i,j
,
\mathbb{G}_{i}^{j}
is an irreducible
P_{0}
-module except in dimension six, where
\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1}
is not irreducible, but
\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,\pm}
are.

3.6.
Isotypic
Q_{0}
-submodules of
\mathbb{G}

Let us fix a splitting of
\mathbb{G}
into
Q_{0}
-modules. Observe that the modules in each of the pairs
(\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{0,0},\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,0})
,
(\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,2},\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{2,0})
,
(\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,1},\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{3,0})
and
(\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,2},\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{2,0})
are isotypic, i.e. they have the same dimensions. This means that one can construct further irreducible
Q_{0}
-modules by assigning some algebraic relations among these in terms of parameters. To this end, we need to describe them by means of the projections

(3.34)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (\Pi_{-1}^{0\times 1})_{[x:y]}&:{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{0,0}\oplus\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,0}\,,&[x:y]&\in\mathbf{R}\mathbb{P}^{1}\,,\\
(\Pi_{-1}^{1\times 2})_{[x:y]}&:{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,2}\oplus\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{2,0}\,,&[x:y]&\in\mathbf{R}\mathbb{P}^{1}\,,\\
(\Pi_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[z:w]}&:{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,1}\oplus\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{3,0}\,,&[z:w]&\in\mathbf{C}\mathbb{P}^{1}\,,\\
(\Pi_{0}^{0\times 1})_{[z:w]}&:{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{0}^{0,0}\oplus\mathbb{G}_{0}^{1,0}\,,&[z:w]&\in\mathbf{C}\mathbb{P}^{1}\,,\end{aligned}

whose precise definitions have been relegated to Appendix
A
for convenience. Here,
\mathbf{R}\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
\mathbf{C}\mathbb{P}^{1}
are real and complex projective lines, respectively. We can then define the following additional
Q_{0}
-submodules of
\mathbb{G}
:

(3.35)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{0\times 1})_{[x:y]}&:=\mathrm{im}(\Pi_{-1}^{0\times 1})_{[x:y]}\subset\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{0,0}\oplus\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,0}\,,&\mbox{for $[x:y]\in\mathbf{R}\mathbb{P}^{1}$.}\\
(\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1\times 2})_{[x:y]}&:=\mathrm{im}(\Pi_{-1}^{1\times 2})_{[x:y]}\subset\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,2}\oplus\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{2,0}\,,&\mbox{for $[x:y]\in\mathbf{R}\mathbb{P}^{1}$.}\\
(\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[z:w]}&:=\mathrm{im}(\Pi_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[z:w]}\subset\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,1}\oplus\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{3,0}\,,&\mbox{for $[z:w]\in\mathbf{C}\mathbb{P}^{1}$.}\\
(\mathbb{G}_{0}^{0\times 1})_{[z:w]}&:=\mathrm{im}(\Pi_{-1}^{1\times 2})_{[z:w]}\subset\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,2}\oplus\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{2,0}\,,&\mbox{for $[z:w]\in\mathbf{C}\mathbb{P}^{1}$.}\end{aligned}

Their descriptions and dimensions are given in Table
2
.

{\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr\text{$Q_{0}$-module}&\text{Description}&\text{Dimension}\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr(\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{0\times 1})_{[x:y]}&\mathbf{R}(1)&1\\
(\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1\times 2})_{[x:y]}&[\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(1,1)}_{\circ}{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\otimes\mathbf{R}(1)&(m-1)(m+1)\\
(\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[z:w]}&[\![\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(2,0)}{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}]\!]\otimes\mathbf{R}(1)&m(m-1)\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr(\mathbb{G}_{0}^{0\times 1})_{[z:w]}&{\mathbb{V}}_{0}^{*}&2m\\
\hline\cr\end{array}}

Table 2.

Q_{0}
-submodules of
\mathbb{G}
— here
[x:y]\in\mathbf{R}\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
[z:w]\in\mathbf{C}\mathbb{P}^{1}

Note that by definition,

\displaystyle(\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{0\times 1})_{[1,0]}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{0,0}\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1\times 2})_{[1,0]}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,2}\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[1,0]}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,1}\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbb{G}_{0}^{0\times 1})_{[1,0]}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,2}\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{0\times 1})_{[0,1]}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1,0}\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1\times 2})_{[0,1]}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{2,0}\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[0,1]}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{3,0}\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbb{G}_{0}^{0\times 1})_{[0,1]}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{2,0}\,.

3.7.
The
Q
-submodules of
\mathbb{G}

We are now in the position of determining all the
Q
-submodules of
\mathbb{G}
. For this purpose, we shall appeal to the
Q_{0}
-module epimorphisms
\Pi_{i}^{j,k}:{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{i}^{j,k}
,
\Pi_{i}^{j}:{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{i}^{j}
and (
3.34
), all of which are described in Appendix
A
with respect to some chosen splitting of
\mathbb{G}
. Any
Q
-submodule of
\mathbb{G}
must be a sum of the irreducible
Q_{0}
-submodules
\mathbb{G}_{i}^{j,k}
given in Table
1
, together with the families of irreducibles
Q_{0}
-modules given in Table
2
. Not every such sum is a
Q
-module. To determine which
Q_{0}
-submodules of
\mathbb{G}
are also
Q
-submodules, we compute how a change of splitting (
3.8
) transforms the maps
\Pi_{i}^{j,k}
. This will tell us how the various modules
\mathbb{G}_{i}^{j,k}
are related under the action of
P_{+}
, the nilpotent part of
Q
. We will then be able to determine the
Q
-submodules of
\mathbb{G}
accordingly.

To facilitate the readability, we contract the projections (
A.4
) with suitable combinations of
\delta^{i}_{\alpha}
,
\delta^{i}_{\bar{\alpha}}
to define

(3.36)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \gamma_{\alpha}&:=\Pi_{-2}^{0,0}(\Gamma)_{\alpha}\,,\\
\epsilon&:=\Pi_{-1}^{0,0}(\Gamma)\,,\\
\tau^{\omega}&:=\Pi_{-1}^{1,0}(\Gamma)\,,\\
\tau_{\alpha\beta}&:=\Pi_{-1}^{1,1}(\Gamma)_{\alpha\beta}={\Pi^{\prime}}_{-1}^{1,1}(\Gamma)_{\alpha\beta}\,,&\tau^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}&:=\Pi_{-1}^{1,2}(\Gamma)_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\,,\\
\sigma_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}&:=\Pi_{-1}^{2,0}(\Gamma)_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\,,&\sigma_{\alpha\beta}&:=\Pi_{-1}^{2,1}(\Gamma)_{\alpha\beta}\,,\\
\zeta_{\alpha\beta}&:=\Pi_{-1}^{3,0}(\Gamma)_{\alpha\beta}\,,\\
E_{\alpha}&:=\Pi_{0}^{0,0}(\Gamma)_{\alpha}={\Pi^{\prime}}_{0}^{0,0}(\Gamma)_{\alpha}\,,&\\
G_{\alpha}&:=\Pi_{0}^{1,0}(\Gamma)_{\alpha}\,,&G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}&:=\Pi_{0}^{1,1}(\Gamma)_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\,,\\
G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}&:=\Pi_{0}^{1,2}(\Gamma)_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\,,&G^{\circ}_{\bar{\alpha}\beta\gamma}&:=\Pi_{0}^{1,3}(\Gamma)_{\bar{\alpha}\beta\gamma}\,,\\
B_{\alpha\beta}&:=\Pi_{1}^{0,0}(\Gamma)_{\alpha\beta}\,.\end{aligned}

Their complex conjugates are defined similarly. Note that with these definitions,

\displaystyle\sigma_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{(\alpha\beta)}\,,

\displaystyle\tau_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=\tau_{[\alpha\beta]}\,,

\displaystyle\sigma_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}h^{\alpha\bar{\beta}}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\tau^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}h^{\alpha\bar{\beta}}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\sigma_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\bar{\alpha}\beta}=\sigma_{\beta\bar{\alpha}}\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\tau^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}}

\displaystyle=\tau^{\circ}_{\bar{\alpha}\beta}=-\tau^{\circ}_{\beta\bar{\alpha}}\,,

\displaystyle\zeta_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=-\zeta_{\beta\alpha}\,,

\displaystyle B_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=-B_{\beta\alpha}\,,

\displaystyle G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{(\alpha\beta)\gamma}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{[\alpha\beta\gamma]}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle G^{\circ}_{\bar{\beta}\alpha\gamma}h^{\alpha\bar{\beta}}

\displaystyle=0\,.

Theorem 3.18
.

Define

\displaystyle\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-2}^{0,0}

\displaystyle:=\{\Gamma\in{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}:\Pi_{-2}^{0,0}(\Gamma)=0\}/\left({\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{q}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{0,0}

\displaystyle:=\{\Gamma\in{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}:\Pi_{-1}^{0,0}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-2}^{0,0}(\Gamma)=0\}/\left({\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{q}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1,i}

\displaystyle:=\{\Gamma\in{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}:\Pi_{-1}^{1,i}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-2}^{0,0}(\Gamma)=0\}/\left({\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{q}\right)\,,\qquad i=0,1

\displaystyle\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{2,i}

\displaystyle:=\{\Gamma\in{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}:\Pi_{-1}^{2,i}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-2}^{0,0}(\Gamma)=0\}/\left({\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{q}\right)\,,\qquad i=0,1,2

\displaystyle\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{3,0}

\displaystyle:=\{\Gamma\in{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}:\Pi_{-1}^{3,0}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-2}^{0,0}(\Gamma)=0\}/\left({\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{q}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{0,0}

\displaystyle:=\{\Gamma\in{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}:\Pi_{0}^{0,0}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-1}^{0}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-1}^{1}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-1}^{2}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-2}^{0}(\Gamma)=0\}/\left({\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{q}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{1,0}

\displaystyle:=\{\Gamma\in{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}:\Pi_{0}^{1,0}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-1}^{0,0}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-1}^{1,0}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-1}^{1,2}(\Gamma)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad=\Pi_{-1}^{2,0}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-1}^{3,0}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-2}^{0,0}(\Gamma)=0\}/\left({\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{q}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{1,1}

\displaystyle:=\{\Gamma\in{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}:\Pi_{0}^{1,1}(\Gamma)=(\Pi_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[-4\mathrm{i},1]}(\Gamma)=0\}/\left({\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{q}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{1,2}

\displaystyle:=\{\Gamma\in{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}:\Pi_{0}^{1,2}(\Gamma)=(\Pi_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[2\mathrm{i},1]}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-1}^{2,1}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-2}^{0,0}(\Gamma)=0\}/\left({\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{q}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{1,3}

\displaystyle:=\{\Gamma\in{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}:\Pi_{0}^{1,3}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-1}^{1,2}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-1}^{2,0}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-1}^{3,0}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-2}^{0,0}(\Gamma)=0\}/\left({\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{q}\right)\,,

and,

\displaystyle\not{\mathbb{G}}_{1}^{0,0}

\displaystyle:=\{\Gamma\in{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}:\Pi_{1}^{0,0}(\Gamma)=(\Pi_{0}^{0\times 1})_{[2(m-1)\mathrm{i},-1]}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{0}^{1,1}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{0}^{1,2}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{0}^{1,3}(\Gamma)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\Pi_{-1}^{1,1}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-1}^{1,2}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-1}^{2}(\Gamma)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad=\Pi_{-1}^{3,0}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-2}^{0,0}(\Gamma)=0\}/\left({\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{q}\right)\,,\qquad\mbox{when $m>2$}\,,

while

\displaystyle\not{\mathbb{G}}_{1}^{0,0}

\displaystyle:=\{\Gamma\in{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}:\Pi_{1}^{0,0}(\Gamma)=(\Pi_{0}^{0\times 1})_{[2\mathrm{i},-1]}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{0}^{1,1}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{0}^{1,2}(\Gamma)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=(\Pi_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[2\mathrm{i},1]}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-1}^{1,2}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-1}^{2}(\Gamma)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad=\Pi_{-1}^{3,0}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-2}^{0,0}(\Gamma)=0\}/\left({\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{q}\right)\,,\qquad\mbox{when $m=2$}\,.

In dimension six, i.e.
m=2
, we always have
\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{1,1}=\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{1,3}=\mathbb{G}
.

Define further, for any
[x:y]\in\mathbf{R}\mathbb{P}^{1}
,
[z:w]\in\mathbf{C}\mathbb{P}^{1}
,

\displaystyle(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{0\times 1})_{[x:y]}

\displaystyle:=\{\Gamma\in{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}:(\Pi_{-1}^{0\times 1})_{[x:y]}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-2}^{0,0}(\Gamma)=0\}/\left({\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{q}\right)\,,

\displaystyle(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1\times 2})_{[x:y]}

\displaystyle:=\{\Gamma\in{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}:(\Pi_{-1}^{1\times 2})_{[x:y]}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-2}^{0,0}(\Gamma)=0\}/\left({\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{q}\right)\,,

\displaystyle(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[z:w]}

\displaystyle:=\{\Gamma\in{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}:(\Pi_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[z:w]}(\Gamma)=(z+4\mathrm{i}w)\Pi_{-2}^{0,0}(\Gamma)=0\}/\left({\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{q}\right)\,,

\displaystyle(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{0\times 1})_{[z:w]}

\displaystyle:=\{\Gamma\in{\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}:(\Pi_{0}^{0\times 1})_{[z:w]}(\Gamma)=(2(m-1)\mathrm{i}w+z)\Pi_{-1}^{0,0}(\Gamma)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad=(2(m-1)\mathrm{i}w+z)\Pi_{-1}^{1,0}(\Gamma)=(\Pi_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[z:w]}(\Gamma)=(2w\,\mathrm{i}-z)\Pi_{-1}^{1,2}(\Gamma)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad=(2w\,\mathrm{i}-z)\Pi_{-1}^{2,0}(\Gamma)=z\Pi_{-1}^{2,1}(\Gamma)=\Pi_{-2}^{0,0}(\Gamma)=0\}/\left({\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{q}\right)\,.

Then, for each
i,j,k
,
\not{\mathbb{G}}_{i}^{j,k}
is the largest
Q
-submodule of
\mathbb{G}
that does not contain
\mathbb{G}_{i}^{j,k}
, and similarly for
(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{0\times 1})_{[x:y]}
,
(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1\times 2})_{[x:y]}
,
(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[z:w]}
and
(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{0\times 1})_{[z:w]}
.
In particular, any
Q
-submodule of
\mathbb{G}
arises as the intersection of any of the ones above.

In addition, there are inclusions of
Q
-submodules, which are denoted by arrows in the diagrams below.

For
m>2
:

\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{1}^{0,0}}

For
m=2
:

\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{1}^{0,0}}
\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{1,2}}
\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{1,0}}
\textstyle{\underset{[-2\mathrm{i}:1]}{(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{0\times 1})}}
\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{0,0}}
\textstyle{\underset{[-4\mathrm{i}:1]}{(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1\times 3})}}
\textstyle{\underset{[2\mathrm{i}:1]}{(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1\times 3})}}
\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{3,0}}
\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{2,1}}
\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{2,0}}
\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1,2}}
\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1,1}}
\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1,0}}
\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{0,0}}
\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-2}^{0,0}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{G}}

For any
m>1
,

\textstyle{\underset{[2\mathrm{i}:1]}{(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{0\times 1})}}
\textstyle{\underset{[-2\mathrm{i}:1]}{(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1\times 3})}}
\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{2,1}}
\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1,0}}
\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{0,0}}
\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-2}^{0,0}}

For any
m>1
, any
[z:w]\in\mathbf{C}\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus\{[-2(m-1)\mathrm{i},1],[2\mathrm{i},1]\}
:

\textstyle{\underset{[z:w]}{(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{0\times 1})}}
\textstyle{\underset{[-z:w]}{(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1\times 3})}}
\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{2,1}}
\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{2,0}}
\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1,2}}
\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1,0}}
\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{0,0}}
\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-2}^{0,0}}

For
m>1
, any
[-4\mathrm{i}:1]\neq[z:w]\in\mathbf{C}\mathbb{P}^{1}
, and any
[x:y]\in\mathbf{R}\mathbb{P}^{1}
:

\textstyle{\underset{[x:y]}{(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{0\times 1})}}
\textstyle{\underset{[x:y]}{(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{0\times 2})}}
\textstyle{\underset{[z:w]}{(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1\times 3})}}
\textstyle{\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-2}^{0,0}}

Remark 3.19
.

We note that

(1)

There is some redundancy in the inclusions: e.g.

\displaystyle\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{3,0}=\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1,1}=\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{3,0}\,,

and, for
m>2
,

\displaystyle\not{\mathbb{G}}_{1}^{0,0}\subset(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{0\times 1})_{[-2(m-1)\mathrm{i}:1]}\cap\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{1,2}\subset(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[2\mathrm{i}:1]}\cap(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[2(m-1)\mathrm{i}:1]}=\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1,0}\cap\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{3,0}\,,

(2)

The only
Q
-submodules of
\mathbb{G}
that are not contained in
\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-2}^{0,0}
are
\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{1,1}\subset(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[4\mathrm{i}:-1]}
.

Proof.

The theorem is a direct consequence of the following transformation rules of the quantities defined by (
3.36
) under the change (
3.8
):

\displaystyle\gamma_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\mapsto\gamma_{\alpha}\,,

\displaystyle\epsilon

\displaystyle\mapsto\epsilon+\gamma_{\alpha}\phi^{\alpha}+\gamma^{\alpha}\phi_{\alpha}\,,

\displaystyle\tau^{\omega}

\displaystyle\mapsto\tau^{\omega}-\mathrm{i}\left(\gamma_{\alpha}\phi^{\alpha}-\gamma^{\alpha}\phi_{\alpha}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\tau_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\mapsto\tau_{\alpha\beta}-\gamma_{[\alpha}\phi_{\beta]}\,,

\displaystyle\tau^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}

\displaystyle\mapsto\tau^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}+\left(-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{\alpha}\phi_{\bar{\beta}}+\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{\bar{\beta}}\phi_{\alpha}\right)_{\circ}\,,

\displaystyle\sigma_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}

\displaystyle\mapsto\sigma_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}+\left(\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{\alpha}\phi_{\bar{\beta}}+\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{\bar{\beta}}\phi_{\alpha}\right)_{\circ}\,,

\displaystyle\sigma_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\mapsto\sigma_{\alpha\beta}+\gamma_{(\alpha}\phi_{\beta)}\,,

\displaystyle\zeta_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\mapsto\zeta_{\alpha\beta}-4\,\mathrm{i}\gamma_{[\alpha}\phi_{\beta]}\,,

\displaystyle E_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\mapsto E_{\alpha}+\tau_{\alpha\beta}\phi^{\beta}-\sigma_{\alpha\beta}\phi^{\beta}+\tau^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\phi^{\bar{\beta}}-\sigma_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\phi^{\bar{\beta}}-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2m}\tau^{\omega}\phi_{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2m}\epsilon\phi_{\alpha}-\gamma_{\alpha}\phi^{\beta}\phi_{\beta}\,,

\displaystyle G_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\mapsto G_{\alpha}-2\,\mathrm{i}\tau^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\phi^{\bar{\beta}}+2\,\mathrm{i}\sigma_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\phi^{\bar{\beta}}+\frac{m-1}{m}\tau^{\omega}\phi_{\alpha}-\frac{m-1}{m}\,\mathrm{i}\epsilon\,\phi_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\phi^{\beta}\zeta_{\beta\alpha}-2\,\mathrm{i}\phi^{\beta}\gamma_{\beta}\phi_{\alpha}+2\,\mathrm{i}\phi^{\beta}\phi_{\beta}\gamma_{\alpha}\,,

\displaystyle G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle\mapsto G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}+\left(-4\,\mathrm{i}\tau_{[\alpha\beta}+\zeta_{[\alpha\beta}\right)\phi_{\gamma]}\,,

\displaystyle G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{(\alpha\beta)\gamma}

\displaystyle\mapsto G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{(\alpha\beta)\gamma}-\left(2\,\mathrm{i}\tau_{\gamma(\alpha}+\zeta_{\gamma(\alpha}\right)\phi_{\beta)}+2\,\mathrm{i}\sigma_{\gamma(\alpha}\phi_{\beta)}-2\,\mathrm{i}\sigma_{\alpha\beta}\phi_{\gamma}-2\,\mathrm{i}\phi_{(\alpha}\gamma_{\beta)}\phi_{\gamma}+2\,\mathrm{i}\phi_{\alpha}\phi_{\beta}\gamma_{\gamma}\,,

\displaystyle G^{\circ}_{\bar{\alpha}\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle\mapsto G^{\circ}_{\bar{\alpha}\beta\gamma}+\left(4\,\mathrm{i}\tau^{\circ}_{[\beta|\bar{\alpha}}\phi_{|\gamma]}-4\,\mathrm{i}\sigma_{[\beta|\bar{\alpha}}\phi_{|\gamma]}+\phi_{\bar{\alpha}}\zeta_{\beta\gamma}-4\,\mathrm{i}\phi_{\bar{\alpha}}\gamma_{[\beta}\phi_{\gamma]}\right)_{\circ}\,,

\displaystyle B_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\mapsto B_{\alpha\beta}+\left(\frac{2}{m-1}G_{[\alpha}-4\,\mathrm{i}E_{[\alpha}\right)\phi_{\beta]}-\phi^{\gamma}G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\gamma\alpha\beta}-\phi^{\gamma}G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\gamma\alpha\beta}-\phi^{\bar{\gamma}}G^{\circ}_{\bar{\gamma}\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+4\mathrm{i}\phi^{\gamma}\tau_{\gamma[\alpha}\phi_{\beta]}-\phi^{\gamma}\phi_{\gamma}\zeta_{\alpha\beta}+4\,\mathrm{i}\phi^{\gamma}\sigma_{\gamma[\alpha}\phi_{\beta]}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-4\mathrm{i}\phi^{\bar{\gamma}}\tau^{\circ}_{[\alpha|\bar{\gamma}}\phi_{|\beta]}+4\,\mathrm{i}\phi^{\bar{\gamma}}\sigma_{[\alpha|\bar{\gamma}}\phi_{|\beta]}+4\mathrm{i}\phi^{\gamma}\phi_{\gamma}\gamma_{[\alpha}\phi_{\beta]}\,.

From these, we immediately deduce, for any
[x:y]\in\mathbf{R}\mathbb{P}^{1}
,
[z:w]\in\mathbf{C}\mathbb{P}^{1}
,

\displaystyle x\epsilon+y\tau^{\omega}

\displaystyle\mapsto x\epsilon+y\tau^{\omega}+(x-\mathrm{i}y)\gamma_{\alpha}\phi^{\alpha}+(x+\mathrm{i}y)\gamma^{\alpha}\phi_{\alpha}\,,

\displaystyle x\sigma_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}-y\mathrm{i}\tau^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}

\displaystyle\mapsto x\sigma_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}-y\mathrm{i}\tau^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}+\left(-(x+y\mathrm{i})\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{\alpha}\phi_{\bar{\beta}}+(x-y\mathrm{i})\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{\bar{\beta}}\phi_{\alpha}\right)_{\circ}\,,

\displaystyle z\tau_{\alpha\beta}+w\zeta_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\mapsto z\tau_{\alpha\beta}+w\zeta_{\alpha\beta}-(z+4\mathrm{i}w)\gamma_{[\alpha}\phi_{\beta]}\,,

\displaystyle z\,E_{\alpha}+w\,G_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\mapsto z\,E_{\alpha}+w\,G_{\alpha}-z\,\phi^{\beta}\sigma_{\beta\alpha}+\left(-z\,\tau_{\beta\alpha}+w\,\zeta_{\beta\alpha}\right)\phi^{\beta}

\displaystyle\qquad-(2w\,\mathrm{i}-z)\tau^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\phi^{\bar{\beta}}+(2w\,\mathrm{i}-z)\sigma_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\phi^{\bar{\beta}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2m}(2(m-1)\mathrm{i}w+z)\tau^{\omega}\phi_{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2m}(2(m-1)\mathrm{i}w+z)\epsilon\,\phi_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-2w\,\mathrm{i}\phi^{\beta}\gamma_{\beta}\phi_{\alpha}+(2w\,\mathrm{i}-z)\phi^{\beta}\phi_{\beta}\gamma_{\alpha}\,.

The result follows as in the proof of Proposition 3.4 of
[
25
]
.

In dimension six, one has

\displaystyle\phi^{\gamma}\phi_{\gamma}\tau_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=-2\phi^{\gamma}\tau_{\gamma[\alpha}\phi_{\beta]}\,,

\displaystyle G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\gamma\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle G^{\circ}_{\bar{\gamma}\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=0\,,

so that

\displaystyle B_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\mapsto B_{\alpha\beta}+\left(2G_{[\alpha}-4\,\mathrm{i}E_{[\alpha}\right)\phi_{\beta]}-\phi^{\gamma}G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\gamma\alpha\beta}-\left(2\mathrm{i}\tau_{\alpha\beta}+\zeta_{\alpha\beta}\right)\phi^{\gamma}\phi_{\gamma}

\displaystyle\qquad+4\mathrm{i}\phi^{\gamma}\sigma_{\gamma[\alpha}\phi_{\beta]}-4\mathrm{i}\phi^{\bar{\gamma}}\tau^{\circ}_{[\alpha|\bar{\gamma}}\phi_{|\beta]}+4\mathrm{i}\phi^{\bar{\gamma}}\sigma_{[\alpha|\bar{\gamma}}\phi_{|\beta]}+4\mathrm{i}\phi^{\gamma}\phi_{\gamma}\gamma_{[\alpha}\phi_{\beta]}

This completes the proof. Some of these computations were verified using the symbolic computer algebra system
cadabra

[
73
,
72
]
.
∎

Finally, for future use, and to make contact with the intrinsic torsion of an optical structure given in
[
25
]
, we also define

(3.37)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \not{\mathbb{G}}_{-2}^{0}&:=\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-2}^{0,0}\,,\\
\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{0}&:=\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{0,0}\,,&\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1}&:=\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1,0}\cap\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1,2}\,,&\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{2}&:=\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{2,0}\cap\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{2,1}\,,\\
\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{0}&:=\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{0,0}\,.\end{aligned}

4.
Almost Robinson manifolds

4.1.
Almost Robinson structures

Throughout we shall follow the notation and conventions of Section
3
now translated into the bundle setting.

Definition 4.1
.

[
63
,
102
]

Let
(\mathcal{M},g)
be an oriented pseudo-Riemannian manifold of dimension
2m+2
. An
almost null structure
on
(\mathcal{M},g)
is a complex distribution
N
of rank
(m+1)
and totally null with respect to
{}^{\mathbf{C}}g
. When
N
is involutive, i.e.
[N,N]\subset N
, we call
N
a
null structure
.

In other words, an almost null structure is a smooth assignement of a null structure to the tangent space at each point, and according to Definition
3.3
, one may talk of the real index of an almost null structure at a point. When
g
is of Lorentzian signature, we make the following definition.

Definition 4.2
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g)
be an oriented and time-oriented Lorentzian manifold of dimension
2m+2
. An
almost Robinson structure
on
(\mathcal{M},g)
consists of a pair
(N,K)
where
N
is a complex distribution of rank
m+1
totally null with respect to
{}^{\mathbf{C}}g
, and
K
a real line distribution such that
{}^{\mathbf{C}}K=N\cap\overline{N}
. We shall refer to the quadruple
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
as an
almost Robinson manifold
or
almost Robinson geometry
.

In addition, we call
(N,K)

•

a
nearly Robinson structure
when
[K,N]\subset N
, and

•

a
Robinson structure
when
[N,N]\subset N
, i.e.
N
is involutive.

We shall accordingly refer to
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
as a
nearly Robinson manifold
or as a
Robinson manifold
.

Clearly, a Robinson manifold is a nearly Robinson manifold. Definitions involving weaker assumptions on orientability are possible.

Remark 4.3
.

Equivalently put, an almost Robinson structure is an almost null structure of real index one. By Lemma
3.5
, any almost null structure on a Lorentzian manifold defines an almost Robinson manifold. The terminology ‘almost null structure’ will nevertheless be preferred in the case when we wish to emphasize the geometric aspects of the almost Robinson structure not particularly tied to the geometry of the real null line distribution
K
, as will be done in Section
4.10
.

An almost Robinson structure
(N,K)
induces an optical structure on
(\mathcal{M},g)
in the sense of
[
25
]
, namely the filtration of vector bundles

(4.1)

\displaystyle K\subset K^{\perp}\subset T\mathcal{M}\,.

The orientation and time-orientation on
\mathcal{M}
induce an orientation on
K
, and the screen bundle
H_{K}:=K^{\perp}/K
of
K
inherits a positive-definite bundle metric
h
from
g
. Any section of
K
will be referred to as an
optical vector field
, while any section of
\mathrm{Ann}(K^{\perp})
will be referred to as an
optical
1
-form
.

In addition, there is a bundle complex structure
J
on the screen bundle
H_{K}
compatible with
h
, which induces a splitting of its complexification

\displaystyle{}^{\mathbf{C}}H_{K}

\displaystyle=H^{(1,0)}_{K}\oplus H^{(0,1)}_{K}\,,

\displaystyle{}^{\mathbf{C}}H_{K}^{*}

\displaystyle=(H^{(1,0)}_{K})^{*}\oplus(H^{(0,1)}_{K})^{*}\,,

where
H^{(1,0)}_{K}
and
H^{(0,1)}_{K}
denote the
+\mathrm{i}
- and
-\mathrm{i}
-eigenbundles of
J
respectively. In abstract index notation, we shall denote the bundle complex structure and the bundle Hermitian structure on
H_{K}
by
J_{i}{}^{j}
and
\omega_{ij}
respectively, so that
\omega_{ij}=J_{i}{}^{k}h_{kj}
. Following the notation of Section
3.1
, we also define the bundles, for any non-negative integer
p
,
q
,

\displaystyle\scalebox{1.2}{$\wedge$}^{(p,q)}H_{K}^{*}

\displaystyle:=\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{p}(H^{(1,0)}_{K})^{*}\otimes\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{q}(H^{(0,1)}_{K})^{*}\,,

\displaystyle\scalebox{1.2}{$\odot$}{}^{(p,q)}H_{K}^{*}

\displaystyle:=\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\odot$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\odot$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\odot$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\odot$}}{}^{p}(H^{(1,0)}_{K})^{*}\otimes\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\odot$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\odot$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\odot$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\odot$}}{}^{q}(H^{(0,1)}_{K})^{*}\,.

For
pq\neq 0
, the subbundles of elements of
\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(p,q)}H_{K}^{*}
and
\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\odot$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\odot$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\odot$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\odot$}}{}^{(p,q)}H_{K}^{*}
that are tracefree with respect to the bundle Hermitian structure will be denoted by
\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(p,q)}_{\circ}H_{K}^{*}
and
\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\odot$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\odot$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\odot$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\odot$}}{}^{(p,q)}_{\circ}H_{K}^{*}
respectively. Similarly, we introduce the subbundle
\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=8.74669pt,width=0.3pt,depth=2.18663pt\hbox to10.93333pt{\hfil}\vrule height=8.74669pt,width=0.3pt,depth=2.18663pt\hbox to10.93333pt{\hfil}\vrule height=8.74669pt,width=0.3pt,depth=2.18663pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=8.74669pt,width=0.3pt,depth=2.18663pt\hbox to10.93333pt{\hfil}\vrule height=8.74669pt,width=0.3pt,depth=2.18663pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}H_{K}^{*}
as a bundle analogue of (
3.1
). The corresponding real spans of these complex bundles will be enclosed between
[\![\cdot]\!]
or
[\cdot]
as described by (
3.2
).

As in Section
3
, we split the complexified tangent bundle as

\displaystyle{}^{\mathbf{C}}T\mathcal{M}=N^{*}\oplus N

for some chosen complement
N^{*}
of
N
in
{}^{\mathbf{C}}T\mathcal{M}
, dual to
N
via
{}^{\mathbf{C}}g
. This splitting is not canonical in general. This induces a splitting of the filtration (
4.1
)

(4.2)

\displaystyle T\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle=L\oplus H_{K,L}\oplus K\,,

where

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle:=N^{*}\cap T\mathcal{M}\,,

\displaystyle H_{K,L}

\displaystyle:=K^{\perp}\cap L^{\perp}\,.

Note that
N^{*}
defines the almost Robinson structure
(N^{*},L)
on
(\mathcal{M},g)
, where
L
is the real span of
N^{*}\cap\overline{N^{*}}
and is dual to
K
.
In addition,

\displaystyle{}^{\mathbf{C}}H_{K,L}

\displaystyle=H^{(1,0)}_{K,L}\oplus H^{(0,1)}_{K,L}\,,

\displaystyle{}^{\mathbf{C}}H^{*}_{K,L}

\displaystyle=(H^{(1,0)}_{K,L})^{*}\oplus(H^{(0,1)}_{K,L})^{*}\,.

where

\displaystyle H_{K,L}^{(1,0)}

\displaystyle=\overline{N}\cap N^{*}\,,

\displaystyle H_{K,L}^{(0,1)}

\displaystyle=N\cap\overline{N^{*}}\,.

We also have isomorphisms of vector bundles
H_{K,L}\cong H_{K}
,
H^{(1,0)}_{K,L}=H^{(1,0)}_{K}
, and so on, which depend on the choice of
N^{*}
.

The splitting operators and their duals, introduced in Section
3
, that is,

\displaystyle(\delta^{a}_{A},\delta^{aA})\,,

\displaystyle(\ell^{a},\delta^{a}_{i},k^{a})\,,

\displaystyle(\delta^{i}_{\alpha},\delta^{i}_{\bar{\alpha}})\,,

\displaystyle(\ell^{a},\delta^{a}_{\alpha},\delta^{a}_{\bar{\alpha}},k^{a})\,,

\displaystyle(\delta_{a}^{A},\delta_{aA})\,,

\displaystyle(\kappa_{a},\delta_{a}^{i},\lambda_{a})\,,

\displaystyle(\delta_{i}^{\alpha},\delta_{i}^{\bar{\alpha}})\,,

\displaystyle(\kappa_{a},\delta_{a}^{\alpha},\delta_{a}^{\bar{\alpha}},\lambda_{a})\,,

will be used throughout the article to convert index types, with the convention that
k^{a}\alpha_{a}=\kappa_{a}\alpha^{a}=\alpha^{0}
and
\ell^{a}\alpha_{a}=\lambda_{a}\alpha^{a}=\alpha_{0}
for any
1
-form
\alpha_{a}
.

In order to avoid ambiguity when taking components of the covariant derivative of some tensor
\alpha_{b\ldots d}
, we shall often write

\displaystyle(\nabla\alpha)_{ab\ldots d}

\displaystyle:=\nabla_{a}\alpha_{b\ldots d}\,.

For instance,

\displaystyle(\nabla\alpha)^{0}{}_{\alpha 0i}

\displaystyle=k^{a}\delta^{b}_{\alpha}\ell^{c}\delta^{d}_{i}\left(\nabla_{a}\alpha_{bcd}\right)\,.

The splitting operators will also be used as injectors. Thus, if
\omega_{ij}
is the Hermitian
2
-form on
H_{K}
, we can set
\omega_{ab}=\omega_{ij}\delta^{i}_{a}\delta^{j}_{b}
for some chosen splitting operators, and construct the
Robinson
3
-form

\rho_{abc}:=3\kappa_{[a}\omega_{bc]}
associated to the optical
1
-form
\kappa_{a}
.

4.2.
Pure spinors

Whenever
(\mathcal{M},g)
is assumed to be spin, we introduce a spin bundle
S
and translate the theory of spinors summarised in Section
3.2.6
to the language of bundles. We shall denote the spinor bundle by
S
and its irreducible parts by
S_{+}
and
S_{-}
. We shall not distinguish notationally between the Levi-Civita connection
\nabla
and the induced spin connection. We now view the van der Waerden symbols
\gamma_{a\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}{}^{\mathbf{B}}
and
\gamma_{a\mathbf{A}}{}^{\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}
, the bilinear forms (
3.20
) as fields on
\mathcal{M}
compatible with
\nabla
. There is also an antilinear map on each fiber of
S
, induced from the reality structure on
({}^{\mathbf{C}}T\mathcal{M},{}^{\mathbf{C}}g)
, and thus, a notion of charge conjugate of a spinor field.

An almost Robinson structure
(N,K)
, where we assume for specificity that
N
is self-dual, can therefore be expressed by a non-vanishing section
\nu^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}
of
S_{+}
that is pure at every point, i.e. the kernel of the map

\displaystyle\nu_{a}^{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle:=\gamma_{a\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}{}^{\mathbf{A}}\nu^{\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}:\Gamma(T\mathcal{M})\rightarrow\Gamma(S^{-})

is precisely
N
. Since the kernel
N
of the map
\nu_{a}^{\mathbf{A}}
is invariant under rescaling of the spinor
\nu^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}
, the almost Robinson structure is in fact equivalent to the existence of a complex line subbundle
S_{+}^{N}
of
S_{+}
, which is spanned by
\nu^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}
. The bundle
S_{+}^{N}
can be viewed as a square root of the line bundle
\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{m+1}\mathrm{Ann}(N)
. Indeed, we have an isomorphism of bundles

(4.3)

\displaystyle\scalebox{1.2}{$\wedge$}^{m+1}\mathrm{Ann}(N)\cong S_{+}^{N}\otimes S_{+}^{N}\,.

Any spinor
\nu^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}
annihilating the almost null structure
N
will be referred to as a
Robinson spinor
, and any section of
\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{m+1}\mathrm{Ann}(N)
a
complex Robinson
(m+1)
-form
.

A completely parallel analysis can be carried out starting with the charge conjugate of
\nu^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}
, which spans the complex conjugate bundle
\overline{S_{+}^{N}}
. The invariants
1
-form
\kappa_{a}
,
3
-form
\rho_{abc}
,
(m+1)
-forms
\nu_{a_{1}\ldots a_{m+1}}
and
\overline{\nu}_{a_{1}\ldots a_{m+1}}
of the almost Robinson structure can then be recovered from
\nu^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}
and its charge conjugate using (
3.2.6
).

We are now in the position of stating the direct translation of Proposition
3.11
into the language of manifolds:

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g)
be an oriented and time-oriented smooth Lorentzian manifold of dimension
2m+2
. The following statements are equivalent.

(1)

(\mathcal{M},g)
is endowed with an almost Robinson structure
(N,K)
.

(2)

(\mathcal{M},g)
admits a simple totally null complex
(m+1)
-form.

(3)

(\mathcal{M},g)
is endowed with an optical structure
K
whose screen bundle
(H_{K},h)
is equipped with a bundle complex structure compatible with the bundle metric
h
.

(4)

(\mathcal{M},g)
admits a null
1
-form
\kappa_{a}
and a
3
-form
\rho_{abc}
such that

(4.4)

\displaystyle\rho_{ab}\,{}^{e}\rho_{cde}

\displaystyle=-4\kappa_{[a}g_{b][c}\kappa_{d]}\,.

(5)

when
(\mathcal{M},g)
is spin, it admits a pure spinor (of real index one).

Remark 4.5
.

In general, if one assumes that
(\mathcal{M},g)
is spin, any geometric statement on a complex Robinson
(m+1)
-form can equivalently be expressed in terms of a Robinson spinor.

4.3.
Almost Robinson structures as
G
-structures

From the discussion of
[
25
]
, we shall also view an almost Robinson structure as a reduction of the frame bundle to the
Q
-bundle
\mathcal{F}^{Q}
where
Q=(\mathbf{R}_{>0}\times\mathbf{U}(m))\ltimes(\mathbf{R}^{2m})^{*}
, and given any
Q
-module
\mathbb{A}
, we will construct associated vector bundles
\mathcal{F}^{Q}(\mathbb{A}):=\mathcal{F}^{Q}\times_{Q}\mathbb{A}
. Similarly, a choice of splitting gives rise to the
Q_{0}
-invariant vector bundles where
Q_{0}=\mathbf{R}_{>0}\times\mathbf{U}(m)
. It will often be more convenient to deal with the reduced coframe bundle
{\mathcal{F}^{*}}^{Q}
. A section of
{\mathcal{F}^{*}}^{Q}
will then consist of a null complex coframe
(\kappa,\theta^{\alpha},\overline{\theta}{}^{\bar{\alpha}},\lambda)
such that

(1)

\kappa
annihilates
K^{\perp}
, or equivalently,
\kappa=g(k,\cdot)
for some section
k
of
K
;

(2)

(\kappa,\theta^{\alpha})
annililate
N
, or equivalently,
(\kappa,\overline{\theta}{}^{\bar{\alpha}})
annililate
\overline{N}
;

(3)

(\theta^{\alpha})
are unitary with respect to the screen bundle metric;

(4)

the metric takes the form

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle=2\kappa\lambda+2h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\theta^{\alpha}\overline{\theta}{}^{\bar{\beta}}\,.

We shall refer to
(\kappa,\theta^{\alpha},\overline{\theta}{}^{\bar{\alpha}},\lambda)
as a
Robinson coframe
.

Any two Robinson coframes
(\kappa,\theta^{\alpha},\overline{\theta}{}^{\bar{\alpha}},\lambda)
and
(\widehat{\kappa},\widehat{\theta}^{\alpha},\overline{\widehat{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}},\widehat{\lambda})
are related by a transformation of the form

(4.5)

\displaystyle\widehat{\kappa}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{\varphi}\kappa\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\psi_{\beta}{}^{\alpha}\theta^{\beta}+\phi^{\alpha}\kappa\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{-\varphi}\left(\lambda-\psi_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\phi_{\beta}\theta^{\alpha}-\psi_{\bar{\alpha}}{}^{\bar{\beta}}\phi_{\bar{\beta}}\theta^{\bar{\alpha}}-\phi_{\alpha}\phi^{\alpha}\kappa\right)\,,

where
\varphi
is a smooth real-valued function, and
\phi^{\alpha}
,
\psi_{\beta}{}^{\alpha}
are smooth complex-valued functions on
\mathcal{M}
with
\psi_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}
being a
\mathbf{U}(m)
-transformation at any point, i.e.
h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=h_{\gamma\bar{\delta}}\psi_{\alpha}{}^{\gamma}\psi_{\bar{\beta}}{}^{\bar{\delta}}
, and
\phi_{\alpha}\phi^{\alpha}=h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\phi^{\alpha}\phi^{\bar{\beta}}
.

Associated to the representations
\mathbf{R}(w)
and
\mathbf{C}(w,w^{\prime})
defined in Section
3.4
, where
w
and
w^{\prime}
are real, we define the bundle
\mathcal{E}(w)
of
boost densities
of weight
w
, already introduced in
[
25
]
, and the bundle
\mathcal{E}(w,w^{\prime})
of
boost-spin densities
of weight
(w,w^{\prime})
. In particular, we have the identifications

\displaystyle K

\displaystyle\cong\mathcal{E}(-1)\,,

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle\cong\mathcal{E}(1)\,,

(4.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(-1,0)

\displaystyle:=\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{m+1}\mathrm{Ann}(N)\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(0,-1)

\displaystyle:=\overline{\mathcal{E}(-1,0)}=\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{m+1}\mathrm{Ann}(\overline{N})\,.

If
(\mathcal{M},g)
is assumed to be spin, we define the smooth complex line bundles

(4.7)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{E}(\tfrac{1}{2},0)&:=\left(S_{+}^{N}\right)^{-1}\,,&\mathcal{E}(0,\tfrac{1}{2})&:=\left(\overline{S_{+}^{N}}\right)^{-1}\,,\\
\mathcal{E}(-\tfrac{1}{2},0)&:=\left(\mathcal{E}(\tfrac{1}{2},0)\right)^{*}\,,&\mathcal{E}(0,-\tfrac{1}{2})&:=\left(\mathcal{E}(0,\tfrac{1}{2})\right)^{*}\,.\end{aligned}

In particular, we recover (
4.6
) by virtue of (
4.3
).
Our definitions are consistent with those of the real line bundles
\mathcal{E}(w)
in the sense that

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(w)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{E}(\tfrac{w}{2},\tfrac{w}{2})/S^{1}\,,

for any real
w
.

This can be readily be checked using (
3.2.6
).

4.4.
Intrinsic torsion

As explained in Section 2 of
[
25
]
, the intrinsic torsion of an almost Robinson structure is given by a section

T^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{Q}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{q})
, which we identify with the
Q
-invariant subbundle
\mathcal{G}:=\mathcal{F}^{Q}\times_{Q}\mathbb{G}
where
\mathbb{G}:={\mathbb{V}}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{q}
. We shall accordingly call
\mathcal{G}
the bundle of intrinsic torsions of
(N,K)
. Its
Q_{0}
-invariant subbundles and
Q
-invariant subbundles will presently be defined with reference to Section
3
, and in particular Theorem
3.15
.

The filtration (
3.33
) on
\mathbb{G}
induces a filtration of
Q
-invariant subbundles

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}

\displaystyle=:\mathcal{G}^{-2}\supset\mathcal{G}^{-1}\supset\mathcal{G}^{0}\supset\mathcal{G}^{-1}\,,

where
\mathcal{G}^{i}:=\mathcal{F}^{Q}\times_{Q}\mathbb{G}^{i}
. Correspondingly, the associated graded vector bundle

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}(\mathcal{G})=\mathrm{gr}_{-2}(\mathcal{G})\oplus\mathrm{gr}_{-1}(\mathcal{G})\oplus\mathrm{gr}_{0}(\mathcal{G})\oplus\mathrm{gr}_{1}(\mathcal{G})\,,

where
\mathrm{gr}_{i}(\mathcal{G}):=\mathcal{F}^{Q}\times_{Q}\mathrm{gr}_{i}(\mathbb{G})
, splits into irreducible
Q
-invariant subbundles
\mathrm{gr}_{i}^{j,k}(\mathcal{G}):=\mathcal{F}^{Q}\times_{Q_{0}}\mathrm{gr}_{i}^{j,k}(\mathbb{G})
. For each choice of splitting, these are isomorphic to the
Q_{0}
-invariant subbundles

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{i}^{j,k}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{F}^{Q}\times_{Q_{0}}\mathbb{G}_{i}^{j,k}\,,

and we introduce further

\displaystyle(\mathcal{G}_{-1}^{0\times 1})_{[x:y]}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{F}^{Q}\times_{Q_{0}}(\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{0\times 1})_{[x:y]}\,,

for each
[x:y]\in\mathbf{R}\mathbb{P}^{1}
,

\displaystyle(\mathcal{G}_{-1}^{1\times 2})_{[x:y]}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{F}^{Q}\times_{Q_{0}}(\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1\times 2})_{[x:y]}\,,

for each
[x:y]\in\mathbf{R}\mathbb{P}^{1}
,

\displaystyle(\mathcal{G}_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[z:w]}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{F}^{Q}\times_{Q_{0}}(\mathbb{G}_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[z:w]}\,,

for each
[z:w]\in\mathbf{C}\mathbb{P}^{1}
,

\displaystyle(\mathcal{G}_{0}^{0\times 1})_{[z:w]}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{F}^{Q}\times_{Q_{0}}(\mathbb{G}_{0}^{0\times 1})_{[z:w]}\,,

for each
[z:w]\in\mathbf{C}\mathbb{P}^{1}
,

with reference to Table
1
and (
3.35
).

We correspondingly define the
Q
-invariant subbundles

\displaystyle\not{\mathcal{G}}_{i}^{j,k}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{F}^{Q}\times_{Q}\not{\mathbb{G}}_{i}^{j,k}\,,

\displaystyle(\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{0\times 1})_{[x:y]}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{F}^{Q}\times_{Q}(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{0\times 1})_{[x:y]}\,,

for each
[x:y]\in\mathbf{R}\mathbb{P}^{1}
,

\displaystyle(\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1\times 2})_{[x:y]}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{F}^{Q}\times_{Q}(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1\times 2})_{[x:y]}\,,

for each
[x:y]\in\mathbf{R}\mathbb{P}^{1}
,

\displaystyle(\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[z:w]}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{F}^{Q}\times_{Q}(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[z:w]}\,,

for each
[z:w]\in\mathbf{C}\mathbb{P}^{1}
,

\displaystyle(\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{0\times 1})_{[z:w]}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{F}^{Q}\times_{Q}(\not{\mathbb{G}}_{0}^{0\times 1})_{[z:w]}\,,

for each
[z:w]\in\mathbf{C}\mathbb{P}^{1}
,

where the
Q
-modules are all defined in Theorem
3.18
.

Finally, in order to make contact with the intrinsic torsion of an optical geometry described in
[
25
]
, we define, for each
i,j
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{i}^{j}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{F}^{P}\times_{P_{0}}\mathbb{G}_{i}^{j}\,,

\displaystyle\not{\mathcal{G}}_{i}^{j}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{F}^{P}\times_{P}\not{\mathbb{G}}_{i}^{j}\,,

where
\mathbb{G}_{i}^{j}
are defined in Proposition
3.17
, and
\not{\mathbb{G}}_{i}^{j}
are defined by (
3.37
).

To determine the algebraic properties of the intrinsic torsion of an almost Robinson structure, we choose an optical
1
-form
\kappa_{a}
and its associated Robinson
3
-form
\rho_{abc}
, compute their respective covariant derivatives
\nabla_{a}\kappa_{b}
and
\nabla_{a}\rho_{bcd}
, and project these tensors onto the various
Q_{0}
-invariant subbundles of
\mathcal{G}
by means of splitting operators
(\ell^{a},\delta^{a}_{\alpha},\delta^{a}_{\bar{\alpha}},k^{a})
. This is achieved by extending the projections
\Pi_{i}^{j,k}
defined in Appendix
A
to bundle projections, where we identify the tensor
\Gamma_{ab}{}^{c}
as a connection
1
-form of
\nabla_{a}
adapted to the splitting operators. We shall make use of the notation already introduced in Section
3
, mirroring the definition (
3.36
). Let us set

(4.8)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &&\gamma_{i}&:=(\nabla\kappa)^{0}{}_{i}\,,\\
\epsilon&:=(\nabla\kappa)_{ij}h^{ij}\,,&\tau_{ij}&:=(\nabla\kappa)_{[ij]}\,,&\sigma_{ij}&:=(\nabla\kappa)_{(ij)_{\circ}}\,,\\
&&E_{i}&:=(\nabla\kappa)_{0i}\,,\end{aligned}

These components split further into irreducible
Q_{0}
-components. In particular, the component
\tau_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
splits as

\displaystyle\tau_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2m}\tau^{\omega}h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}+\tau^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\,,

where

\displaystyle\tau^{\omega}

\displaystyle:=\omega^{ij}\tau_{ij}=-2\mathrm{i}\tau_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}h^{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\,,

\displaystyle\tau^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}

\displaystyle:=\left(\tau_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\right)_{\circ}\,,

In addition, the components of the covariant derivative of the Robinson
3
-form
\rho_{abc}
of interest are

(4.9)

\displaystyle\zeta_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle:=(\nabla\rho)^{0}{}_{\alpha\beta 0}\,,

(4.10)

\displaystyle G_{i\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle:=(\nabla\rho)_{i\beta\gamma 0}\,,

\displaystyle B_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle:=(\nabla\rho)_{0\alpha\beta 0}\,.

the second of which splits further into the irreducible components

\displaystyle G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle:=G_{[\alpha\beta\gamma]}\,,

\displaystyle G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle:=\frac{2}{3}\left(G_{(\alpha\beta)\gamma}-G_{(\alpha\gamma)\beta}\right)\,,

\displaystyle G^{\circ}_{\bar{\gamma}\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle:=\left(G_{\bar{\gamma}\alpha\beta}\right)_{\circ}\,,

\displaystyle G_{\alpha}

\displaystyle:=h^{\beta\bar{\gamma}}G_{\bar{\gamma}\beta\alpha}\,,

so that

\displaystyle G_{\alpha\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle=G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}+G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\,,

\displaystyle G_{\bar{\alpha}\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle=G^{\circ}_{\bar{\alpha}\beta\gamma}-\frac{2}{m-1}G_{[\beta}h_{\gamma]\bar{\alpha}}\,,

\displaystyle G_{i\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle=\delta^{\alpha}_{i}G_{\alpha\beta\gamma}+\delta^{\bar{\alpha}}_{i}G_{\bar{\alpha}\beta\gamma}\,.

The other relevant components of
\nabla_{a}\rho_{bcd}
can be found below:

\displaystyle(\nabla\rho)_{a}{}^{0}{}_{jk}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle(\nabla\rho)_{a\alpha\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle(\nabla\rho)^{00}{}_{0\alpha}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}\gamma_{\alpha}\,,

\displaystyle(\nabla\rho)^{0}{}_{\alpha\beta\bar{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{i}\gamma_{[\alpha}h_{\beta]\bar{\gamma}}\,,

\displaystyle(\nabla\rho)_{\alpha\beta}{}^{0}{}_{0}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}\tau_{\alpha\beta}-\mathrm{i}\sigma_{\alpha\beta}\,,

\displaystyle(\nabla\rho)_{\alpha\beta\gamma\bar{\delta}}

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{i}\left(\tau_{\alpha[\beta}+\sigma_{\alpha[\beta}\right)h_{\gamma]\bar{\delta}}\,,

\displaystyle(\nabla\rho)_{\bar{\alpha}\beta}{}^{0}{}_{0}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}\frac{1}{2m}\epsilon h_{\beta\bar{\alpha}}+\mathrm{i}\tau_{\beta\bar{\alpha}}-\mathrm{i}\sigma_{\beta\bar{\alpha}}\,,

\displaystyle(\nabla\rho)_{\bar{\alpha}\beta\gamma\bar{\delta}}

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{i}\left(\frac{1}{2m}\epsilon h_{[\beta|\bar{\alpha}}-\tau_{[\beta|\bar{\alpha}}+\sigma_{[\beta|\bar{\alpha}}\right)h_{|\gamma]\bar{\delta}}\,,

\displaystyle(\nabla\rho)_{0\alpha}{}^{0}{}_{0}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}E_{\alpha}\,,

\displaystyle(\nabla\rho)_{0\alpha\beta\bar{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{i}E_{[\alpha}h_{\beta]\bar{\gamma}}\,,

\displaystyle(\nabla\rho)^{0}{}_{0\beta\bar{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}(\nabla\kappa)^{0}{}_{0}h_{\beta\bar{\gamma}}\,,

\displaystyle(\nabla\rho)_{i0\beta\bar{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}(\nabla\kappa)_{i0}h_{\beta\bar{\gamma}}\,,

\displaystyle(\nabla\rho)_{00\beta\bar{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}(\nabla\kappa)_{00}h_{\beta\bar{\gamma}}\,.

The elements defined above should be regarded as trivialisations of sections of
\mathcal{G}_{i}^{j,k}
. Such sections generally carry a boost weight. These will be adorned with a breve accent. To be clear, we have collected them in Tables
3
and
4
— the bracket notation
[\![\cdot]\!]
and
[\cdot]
therein should be understood as taking the real span of the quantities enclosed therebetween. For instance,

\displaystyle[\![\breve{G}^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}]\!]

\displaystyle\sim\breve{G}^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\delta^{\alpha}_{i}\delta^{\beta}_{j}\delta^{\gamma}_{k}+\breve{G}^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\bar{\alpha}\bar{\beta}\bar{\gamma}}\delta^{\bar{\alpha}}_{i}\delta^{\bar{\beta}}_{j}\delta^{\bar{\gamma}}_{k}\,,

and

\displaystyle[\breve{\tau}^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}]

\displaystyle\sim 2\breve{\tau}^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\delta^{\alpha}_{[i}\delta^{\bar{\beta}}_{j]}\,,

and so on.

{\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr\text{$P_{0}$-bundle}&\text{Description}&\text{Tensor}\\

We shall also introduce the quantity

\displaystyle\breve{\tau}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2m}\breve{\tau}^{\omega}h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}+\breve{\tau}^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\,,

which we identify as a section of
\mathcal{G}_{-1}^{1,0}\oplus\mathcal{G}_{-1}^{1,2}
.

Finally, to characterise sections of the bundles
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{i}^{j,k}
, we simply apply the results of Theorem
3.18
. For instance, the intrinsic torsion is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,2}
if and only if its weighted components in any splitting satisfy

\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\sigma}_{\alpha\beta}=2\mathrm{i}\breve{\tau}^{\circ}_{\alpha\beta}+\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{G}^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=0\,.

Remark 4.6
.

We can also express some of the properties of the almost Robinson structure in terms of a Robinson spinor — this will be done in Section
4.10
.

4.5.
Congruences of null geodesics

Since an almost Robinson manifold
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
defines in particular an optical geometry, there is a congruence of null curves
\mathcal{K}
associated to it. The algebraic properties of the intrinsic torsion of the optical geometry
(\mathcal{M},g,K)
can be related to the geometric properties of
\mathcal{K}
as reviewed in
[
25
]
. We summarise the correspondence in Table
5
.

{\begin{array}[]{|c|c|}\hline\cr\text{Intrinsic torsion}&\text{Congruence}\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-2}^{0}=\mathcal{G}^{-1}&\text{geodesic}\\
\hline\cr\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{0}&\text{non-expanding}\\
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1}&\text{non-twisting}\\
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{2}&\text{non-shearing}\\
\hline\cr\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{0}=\{0\}&\text{parallel}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}}

Table 5.
Geometric properties of
\mathcal{K}
and intrinsic torsion
\mathring{T}

We also record the following lemma, whose proof is straightforward.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be an almost Robinson manifold with congruence of null curves
\mathcal{K}
. Denote by
J_{i}{}^{j}
the complex structure on the screen bundle
H_{K}
. Suppose that the intrinsic torsion
\mathring{T}
of
(N,K)
is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-2}^{0}
so that
\mathcal{K}
is geodesic with twist
\breve{\tau}_{ij}
and shear
\breve{\sigma}_{ij}
. Then
\mathring{T}
is a section of

•

\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,1}
, i.e.
\breve{\tau}_{\alpha\beta}=0
, if and only if
\breve{\tau}_{ij}
and
J_{i}{}^{j}
commute, i.e.
J_{[i}{}^{k}\breve{\tau}_{j]k}=0
;

•

\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{2,1}
, i.e.
\breve{\sigma}_{\alpha\beta}=0
, if and only if
\breve{\sigma}_{ij}
and
J_{i}{}^{j}
commute, i.e.
J_{(i}{}^{k}\breve{\sigma}_{j)k}=0
;

•

\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,0}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,2}
, i.e.
\breve{\tau}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=0
, if and only if
\breve{\tau}_{ij}
and
J_{i}{}^{j}
anti-commute, i.e.
J_{(i}{}^{k}\breve{\tau}_{j)k}=0
;

•

\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{2,0}
, i.e.
\breve{\sigma}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=0
, if and only if
\breve{\sigma}_{ij}
and
J_{i}{}^{j}
anti-commute, i.e.
J_{[i}{}^{k}\breve{\sigma}_{j]k}=0
.

Locally, we shall identify an almost Robinson manifold
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
of dimension
2m+2
as a surjective submersion over a
(2m+1)
-dimensional smooth manifold
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
, namely, the local leaf space of
\mathcal{K}
. This leaf space will be endowed with various geometric structures depending on the geometric properties of
\mathcal{K}
. In the next sections, we shall examine the relation between the intrinsic torsion of
(N,K)
with the induced geometric structures on the leaf space. As a notational rule followed in this article, tensor fields on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
will be underlined to distinguish them from tensor fields on
\mathcal{M}
.

Remark 4.8
.

We briefly recall the results of
[
25
]
(see also
[
89
]
) that pertain to the optical structure
K
associated to an almost Robinson manifold
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
. In the following, we shall denote the conformal class of
g
by
[g]
. If the congruence of null curves
\mathcal{K}
tangent to
K
is geodesic, there is a subclass
[g]_{n.e.}
of metrics in
[g]
for which
\mathcal{K}
is also non-expanding. By extension,
[g]
and
[g]_{n.e.}
determine conformal subclasses of bundle metrics
[h]
and
[h]_{n.e.}
respectively on the screen bundle
H_{K}
. We then have the following relations between the geometric properties of
\mathcal{K}
and its leaf space:

•

If
\mathcal{K}
is geodesic,
H_{K}
descends to a rank-
2m
distribution
\underline{H}
on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
. In this case, there is a one-to-one correspondence between optical vector fields
k
such that
\mathsterling_{k}\kappa=0
, where
\kappa=g(k,\cdot)
, and sections of
\mathrm{Ann}(\underline{H})
.

•

If
\mathcal{K}
is geodesic and non-twisting, the induced distribution
\underline{H}
on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
is involutive.

•

If
\mathcal{K}
is geodesic and non-shearing, the screen bundle metric
H_{K}
induces a bundle conformal structure
\underline{\mathbf{c}}_{\underline{H}}
on
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H})
. There is a one-to-one correspondence between metrics in
[g]_{n.e.}
(or equivalently, screen bundle metrics in
[h]_{n.e.}
) and metrics in
\underline{\mathbf{c}}_{\underline{H}}
. It follows that if
\mathcal{K}
is already non-expanding for
g
, then
h
descends to a distinguished bundle metric
\underline{h}
on
\underline{H}
.

Dually, we thus have that the bracket condition
[K,K^{\perp}]\subset K^{\perp}
is equivalent to
\mathcal{K}
being geodesic, and the condition
[K^{\perp},K^{\perp}]\subset K^{\perp}
to
\mathcal{K}
being geodesic and non-twisting. The latter can split into the two following obvious subcases.

Proposition 4.9
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be a
(2m+2)
-dimensional almost Robinson manifold with congruence of null curves
\mathcal{K}
with leaf space
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
. The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

[N,\overline{N}]\subset{}^{\mathbf{C}}K^{\perp}
;

(2)

the intrinsic torsion is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,0}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,2}
, i.e.
