\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\tau}^{\omega}=\breve{\tau}^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=0\,;

(3)

\mathcal{K}
is geodesic and its twist anti-commutes with the screen bundle complex structure.

Proposition 4.10
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be a
(2m+2)
-dimensional almost Robinson manifold with congruence of null curves
\mathcal{K}
with leaf space
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
. The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

[N,N]\subset{}^{\mathbf{C}}K^{\perp}
;

(2)

the intrinsic torsion is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,1}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\tau}_{\alpha\beta}=0\,;

(3)

\mathcal{K}
is geodesic and its twist commutes with the screen bundle complex structure.

We shall deal with the remaining bracket conditions, namely
[K,N]\subset N
and
[N,N]\subset N
in Propositions
4.16
and
4.45
respectively.

Remark 4.11
.

In the context of almost Robinson geometry, there are many candidates generalising the notion of non-shearing congruence of null geodesics from four to higher even dimensions. All of them should be almost Robinson structures whose intrinsic torsion is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{2,1}
, i.e.
\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\ \breve{\sigma}_{\alpha\beta}=0
,

Remark 4.12
.

It is clear from Theorem
3.18
that the only proper subbundles of
\mathcal{G}
that are not contained in
\mathcal{G}^{-1}
are

(4.11)

\displaystyle\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,1}\subset(\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[4\mathrm{i}:-1]}\,,

i.e.
-4\,\mathrm{i}\tau_{\alpha\beta}+\zeta_{\alpha\beta}=0
and
G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=-4\,\mathrm{i}\tau_{\alpha\beta}+\zeta_{\alpha\beta}=0
. This means that the congruence of null curves associated to an almost Robinson structure whose intrinsic torsion lies in any proper subbundles of
\mathcal{G}
except for (
4.11
) must be geodesic. We leave it as a conjecture whether one can construct almost Robinson manifolds whose intrinsic torsion lies in (
4.11
) but whose congruence is not geodesic.

4.6.
Almost CR geometry

The geometric structure that an almost Robinson structure may induce on the leaf space of its associated congruence of null curves is an almost CR structure, of which we now recall some notions. For a friendly introduction, see
[
42
]
.

4.6.1.
General definitions

An
almost CR structure
on a
(2m+1)
-dimensional smooth manifold
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
consists of a pair
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
, where
\underline{H}
is a rank-
2m
distribution equipped with a bundle complex structure
\underline{J}
. A
CR structure
is an almost CR structure for which the
-\mathrm{i}
-eigenbundle
\underline{H}^{(0,1)}
of
\underline{J}
, or equivalently its
\mathrm{i}
-eigenbundle
\underline{H}^{(1,0)}
, is in involution. We call an almost CR structure
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
together with a choice of
1
-form
\underline{\theta}{}^{0}
annihilating
\underline{H}
an
almost pseudo-Hermitian structure
.

There are two notions of
Levi forms
that we can associate to
(\underline{H},\underline{J}
):

•

Following
[
18
]
, we define the
Levi form of the distribution
\underline{H}
to be the bundle homomorphism
\underline{\bm{\mathsf{L}}}:\mathrm{Ann}(\underline{H})\rightarrow\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{2}\left(T^{*}\underline{\mathcal{M}}/\mathrm{Ann}(\underline{H})\right)
given by the composition

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ann}(\underline{H})\longrightarrow T^{*}\underline{\mathcal{M}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{d}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{2}T^{*}\underline{\mathcal{M}}\longrightarrow\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{2}\left(T^{*}\underline{\mathcal{M}}/\mathrm{Ann}(\underline{H})\right)\,.

Concretely, for any section
\underline{\theta}{}^{0}
of
\mathrm{Ann}(\underline{H})
,
\underline{\theta}{}^{0}\circ\underline{\bm{\mathsf{L}}}(v,w)=\mathrm{d}\underline{\theta}{}^{0}(v,w)
for any sections
v
and
w
of
\underline{H}
. We shall refer to
\underline{\mathsf{L}}:=\underline{\theta}{}^{0}\circ\underline{\bm{\mathsf{L}}}
as the
Levi form of
(\underline{H},\underline{\theta}^{0})
.

•

Given a
1
-form
\underline{\theta}^{0}
annihilating
\underline{H}
, the
Levi form of the almost pseudo-Hermitian structure

(\underline{H},\underline{J},\underline{\theta}^{0})
is the Hermitian form
\underline{\mathsf{h}}
on
\underline{H}^{(1,0)}
defined by

\displaystyle\underline{\mathsf{h}}(v,w)

\displaystyle:=-2\mathrm{i}\mathrm{d}\underline{\theta}^{0}(v,\overline{w})\,,

for any
v,w\in\Gamma(\underline{H}^{(1,0)})
.

By extension, the
Levi form

\underline{\bm{\mathsf{h}}}
of
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
is the Hermitian form taking values in
\mathbf{C}\otimes\left(T\underline{\mathcal{M}}/\underline{H}\right)
defined by
\underline{\mathsf{h}}:=\underline{\theta}{}^{0}\circ\underline{\bm{\mathsf{h}}}
.

Note that the definition of
\underline{\bm{\mathsf{h}}}
depends on both
\underline{H}
and
\underline{J}
, but that of
\underline{\bm{\mathsf{L}}}
depends on
\underline{H}
alone. One can identify
\underline{\bm{\mathsf{h}}}
in a suitable way with the
(1,1)
-part of
\underline{\bm{\mathsf{L}}}
with respect to
\underline{J}
— see below.

A coframe
(\underline{\theta}^{0},\underline{\theta}^{\alpha},\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}})
adapted to the almost CR structure
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
consists of a real
1
-form
\underline{\theta}{}^{0}
and
m
complex
1
-forms
\underline{\theta}{}^{\alpha}
with
\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}}=\overline{\underline{\theta}{}^{\alpha}}
, such that
\underline{H}=\mathrm{Ann}(\underline{\theta}^{0})
, and
\underline{H}^{(0,1)}=\mathrm{Ann}(\underline{\theta}{}^{0},\underline{\theta}{}^{\alpha})
. For simplicity, we shall assume that
\mathrm{Ann}(\underline{H})
is oriented, although this assumption can easily be dropped. Any other coframe
(\widehat{\underline{\theta}}{}^{0},\widehat{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha},\overline{\widehat{\underline{\theta}}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}})
adapted to
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
is related to
(\underline{\theta}{}^{0},\underline{\theta}{}^{\alpha},\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}})
by

(4.12)

\displaystyle\widehat{\underline{\theta}}{}^{0}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{\underline{\varphi}}\underline{\theta}{}^{0}\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\underline{\psi}_{\beta}{}^{\alpha}\underline{\theta}{}^{\beta}+\underline{\phi}^{\alpha}\underline{\theta}{}^{0}\,,

where
\underline{\varphi}
,
\underline{\psi}_{\beta}{}^{\alpha}
, and
\underline{\phi}^{\alpha}
are smooth functions on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
, with the requirement that the determinant of
\underline{\psi}_{\beta}{}^{\alpha}
be non-vanishing.
4
4

4

One could also include negative rescalings of
\underline{\theta}^{0}
if one drops the assumption of orientability of
\mathrm{Ann}(\underline{H})
.
Even with a choice of an almost pseudo-Hermitian structure
\underline{\theta}^{0}
on
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{J})
, there is no canonical choice for
(\underline{\theta}{}^{\alpha})
in general. Any choice of vector field
\underline{e}_{0}
dual to
\underline{\theta}^{0}
splits
T\underline{\mathcal{M}}
as

(4.13)

\displaystyle T\underline{\mathcal{M}}=\underline{H}\oplus\mathrm{span}(\underline{e}_{0})\,.

The structure equations for a given CR coframe
(\underline{\theta}^{0},\underline{\theta}^{\alpha},\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}})
can be expressed as

(4.14)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathrm{d}\underline{\theta}^{0}&=\mathrm{i}\underline{\mathsf{h}}{}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\,\underline{\theta}^{\alpha}\wedge\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\beta}}+\underline{\mathsf{L}}{}_{\alpha\beta}\,\underline{\theta}^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{\theta}^{\beta}+\underline{\mathsf{L}}{}_{\bar{\alpha}\bar{\beta}}\,\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}}\wedge\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\beta}}+\underline{\alpha}\wedge\underline{\theta}^{0}\,,\\
\mathrm{d}\underline{\theta}^{\alpha}&=\underline{\theta}{}^{\beta}\wedge\underline{\Gamma}{}_{\beta}{}^{\alpha}+\underline{A}{}^{\alpha}{}_{\bar{\beta}}\,\underline{\theta}^{0}\wedge\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\beta}}-\frac{1}{2}\underline{\mathsf{N}}_{\bar{\beta}\bar{\gamma}}{}^{\alpha}\,\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\beta}}\wedge\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\gamma}}\,,\\
\mathrm{d}\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}}&=\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\beta}}\wedge\overline{\underline{\Gamma}}{}_{\bar{\beta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}}+\underline{A}{}^{\bar{\alpha}}{}_{\beta}\,\underline{\theta}^{0}\wedge\underline{\theta}{}^{\beta}-\frac{1}{2}\underline{\mathsf{N}}_{\beta\gamma}{}^{\bar{\alpha}}\,\underline{\theta}{}^{\beta}\wedge\underline{\theta}{}^{\gamma}\,,\end{aligned}

for some complex functions
\underline{\mathsf{h}}{}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
,
\underline{\mathsf{L}}{}_{\alpha\beta}
,
\underline{A}{}^{\bar{\alpha}}{}_{\beta}
,
\underline{\mathsf{N}}_{\beta\gamma}{}^{\bar{\alpha}}
and
1
-forms
\underline{\Gamma}{}_{\beta}{}^{\alpha}
and
\underline{\alpha}
on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
, where
\underline{\mathsf{h}}{}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
is Hermitian,
\underline{\alpha}
is real, and the remaining quantities are defined by complex conjugation. Here, we identify
\underline{\mathsf{h}}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
as the components of the Levi form of the pseudo-Hermitian structure
(\underline{H},\underline{J},\underline{\theta}^{0})
.

There are two important invariants of
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
at a point of
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
, namely

•

the
rank
of
\underline{\bm{\mathsf{L}}}
, that is, the largest integer
r
for which
\underline{\theta}^{0}\wedge\left(\mathrm{d}\underline{\theta}^{0}\right)^{r}
does not vanish;

•

the
signature
of
\underline{\bm{\mathsf{h}}}
, i.e. the signature of the Hermitian matrix
\underline{\mathsf{h}}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
.

Clearly, these do not depend on the choice of CR coframe. We shall assume regularity of the rank and signature throughout the article, i.e. these will be constant everywhere. We say that the almost CR structure
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
is

•

contact
or
non-degenerate
if
\underline{\bm{\mathsf{L}}}
has maximal rank, i.e.
\underline{\theta}^{0}\wedge\left(\mathrm{d}\underline{\theta}^{0}\right)^{m}
does not vanish at any point, i.e.
\underline{H}
is a contact distribution;

•

totally degenerate
if
\underline{\bm{\mathsf{L}}}
vanishes identically, i.e.
\underline{\mathsf{L}}{}_{\alpha\beta}=\underline{\mathsf{h}}{}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=0
, i.e.
\underline{\theta}^{0}\wedge\mathrm{d}\underline{\theta}^{0}=0
everywhere, i.e.
\underline{H}
is involutive;

•

partially integrable
if
\underline{\bm{\mathsf{L}}}
is of type
(1,1)
, i.e.
\underline{\mathsf{L}}{}_{\alpha\beta}=0
— in this case,
\underline{\bm{\mathsf{L}}}
can be identified with the imaginary part of
\underline{\bm{\mathsf{h}}}
;

•

integrable
or
involutive
if
\underline{H}^{(0,1)}
is involutive, i.e.
\underline{\mathsf{L}}{}_{\bar{\alpha}\bar{\beta}}=\underline{\mathsf{N}}_{\bar{\beta}\bar{\gamma}}{}^{\alpha}=0
.

The first two properties pertain to
\underline{H}
alone while the latter two depend on the pair
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
.

Example 4.13
.

The model for a contact CR manifold is the CR sphere
S^{2m+1}
viewed as a hypersurface in
\mathbf{C}^{2m+2}
. More generally, any real hypersurface in
\mathbf{C}^{2m+2}
is a CR manifold.

Remark 4.14
.

As a special case of almost CR manifold, consider an almost complex manifold
(\undertilde{\mathcal{M}},\undertilde{J})
. Then, one can take
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
to be a bundle over
\undertilde{\mathcal{M}}
with one-dimensional fibers such as
\mathbf{R}\times\undertilde{\mathcal{M}}
,
\mathbf{R}_{>0}\times\undertilde{\mathcal{M}}
and
S^{1}\times\undertilde{\mathcal{M}}
, and choose
(\undertilde{\theta}^{\alpha})
to be a frame of
(1,0)
-forms for
\undertilde{\mathcal{M}}
, extend it to a coframe
(\underline{\theta}^{0},\underline{\theta}^{\alpha},\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}})
on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}\rightarrow\undertilde{\mathcal{M}}
by adjoining a vertical
1
-form
\underline{\theta}^{0}
. Then clearly
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{J})
is an almost CR manifold, where
\underline{H}=\mathrm{Ann}(\underline{\theta}^{0})
, and the
-\mathrm{i}
-eigenbundle of
\underline{J}
is
\underline{H}^{(0,1)}=\mathrm{Ann}(\underline{\theta}^{0},\underline{\theta}^{\alpha})
.

4.6.2.
Partially integrable contact almost CR manifolds

Suppose that
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
is both contact and partially integrable. Then
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
is equipped with a subconformal contact structure
\underline{\mathbf{c}}_{\underline{H},\underline{J}}
compatible with the bundle complex structure
\underline{J}
. Indeed,
\underline{\bm{\mathsf{L}}}
is now a
(1,1)
-form, which we may identify with
\underline{\bm{\mathsf{h}}}
, and with a slight abuse of notation,
\underline{\bm{\mathsf{h}}}=2\,\underline{\bm{\mathsf{L}}}\circ\underline{J}
is a
subconformal metric
on
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{J})
. Note that
\underline{H}^{(1,0)}
and
\underline{H}^{(0,1)}
are totally null with respect to
\underline{\mathbf{c}}_{\underline{H},\underline{J}}
. In particular, we have a one-to-one correspondence between contact forms in
\mathrm{Ann}(\underline{H})
and metrics in
\underline{\mathbf{c}}_{\underline{H},\underline{J}}
, each metric being given by
\underline{\mathsf{h}}=2\,\underline{\mathsf{L}}\circ\underline{J}
where
\underline{\mathsf{L}}=\underline{\theta}^{0}\circ\underline{\bm{\mathsf{L}}}
for some contact form
\underline{\theta}^{0}
.

Furthermore, each choice of contact
1
-form
\underline{\theta}^{0}
determines a unique vector field
\underline{e}_{0}
, the
Reeb
vector field, satisfying
\underline{\theta}^{0}(\underline{e}_{0})=1
and
\mathrm{d}\underline{\theta}^{0}(\underline{e}_{0},\cdot)=0
, which induces a canonical splitting (
4.13
), and one can choose an
adapted coframe

(\underline{\theta}^{\alpha},\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}})
for
\underline{H}
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\underline{\theta}^{0}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\underline{\mathsf{h}}{}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\underline{\theta}^{\alpha}\wedge\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\beta}}\,.

Any two such coframes must be related by a change (
4.12
) where
\underline{\phi}^{\alpha}=\mathrm{i}\underline{\mathsf{h}}^{\alpha\bar{\beta}}(\mathrm{d}\underline{\varphi})_{\bar{\beta}}
. With no loss, one can always choose
(\underline{\theta}^{\alpha})
to be unitary with respect to
\underline{\mathsf{h}}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
.

Finally, to each choice of contact
1
-form
\underline{\theta}^{0}
, there exists a unique connection
\underline{\nabla}
, namely the
Webster–Tanaka connection
, that preserves
\underline{\theta}^{0}
and
\mathrm{d}\underline{\theta}^{0}
, with prescribed torsion
[
108
,
116
]
: with reference to the structure equations (
4.14
), we identity
\underline{\Gamma}_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}
with the connection
1
-form of
\underline{\nabla}
,
\underline{A}_{\alpha\beta}=\underline{h}_{\alpha\bar{\gamma}}\underline{A}^{\bar{\gamma}}{}_{\beta}
with the so-called
pseudo-Hermitian torsion tensor
, and
\underline{\mathsf{N}}_{\beta\gamma\alpha}=\underline{\mathsf{N}}_{\beta\gamma}{}^{\bar{\delta}}\underline{h}_{\alpha\bar{\delta}}
with the so-called
Nijenhuis torsion tensor
. By virtue of the Bianchi identities, these satisfy
\underline{A}_{[\alpha\beta]}=\underline{\mathsf{N}}_{[\beta\gamma\alpha]}=0
. While
\underline{\mathsf{N}}_{\beta\gamma\alpha}
is a CR invariant – it is the obstruction to the involutivity of
\underline{H}^{(1,0)}
– the torsion tensor
\underline{A}_{\alpha\beta}
depends on the choice of contact form, and is an invariant of the almost pseudo-Hermitian structure only. It may be interpreted as the obstruction to the Reeb vector field being a transverse symmetry of the CR structure. If
\underline{A}_{\alpha\beta}=0
vanishes, we call
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{J},\underline{\theta}^{0})
a
Sasaki
almost pseudo-Hermitian contact manifold.

Further invariants of the partially integrable contact structure
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
can be obtained from the curvature of
\underline{\nabla}
. Of interest are the
Chern–Moser tensor
when
m>1
, and the
Cartan tensor
when
m=1
. If
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
is integrable, then the vanishing of these tensors is equivalent to the CR manifold being locally diffeomorphic to the CR sphere – see e.g.
[
14
]
.

Remark 4.15
.

There are close analogies between partially integrable contact almost CR geometry and conformal geometry by virtue of the existence of the subconformal structure
\underline{\mathbf{c}}_{\underline{H},\underline{J}}
: here, an almost pseudo-Hermitian structure can be seen as a choice of a bundle metric in
\underline{\mathbf{c}}_{\underline{H},\underline{J}}
, and one may define an almost pseudo-Hermitian analogue of the Einstein condition in terms of the
\underline{\theta}^{0}
-dependent
Webster–Ricci tensor
and the pseudo-Hermitian torsion tensor
\underline{A}_{\alpha\beta}
. This condition was introduced in the integrable case in
[
51
,
12
]
, and generalised to the non-integrable case in
[
106
]
where it is referred to as an
almost CR–Einstein structure
.
5
5

5

It is rather unfortunate that the terminology “almost CR–Einstein structure” is used in different ways in
[
12
]
and
[
106
]
: in the former reference, “almost” refers to the Einstein condition, meaning that the manifold is a CR manifold that is CR–Einstein off the zero set of some density. In the latter reference, “almost” refers to an almost CR structure that is not necessarily integrable.
As shown in
[
51
,
106
]
, such a structure can be constructed on the anti-canonical bundle of an almost Kähler–Einstein manifold. Conversely, any almost CR–Einstein manifold locally arises in this way.

We shall leave aside analytical questions related to CR manifolds, especially in connection with embeddability, and we refer the reader to
[
42
,
113
]
and references therein for further details.

4.7.
Nearly Robinson structures and almost CR structures

In this section, we restrict our attention to nearly Robinson structures. These lie at the junction between Lorentzian geometry and almost CR geometry, as the next proposition makes clear.

Proposition 4.16
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be a
(2m+2)
-dimensional almost Robinson manifold with congruence of null curves
\mathcal{K}
with leaf space
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
. The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

(N,K)
is a nearly Robinson structure, i.e
[K,N]\subset N
, i.e. for any optical vector field
k
, and any
v\in\Gamma(N)
,
\mathsterling_{k}v\in\Gamma(N)
;

(2)

any complex Robinson
(m+1)
-form
\nu
is preserved along
K
, i.e. for any optical vector field
k
,
\mathsterling_{k}\nu=f\nu
for some smooth function
f
;

(3)

the intrinsic torsion is a section of
(\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[-2\mathrm{i}:1]}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{2,0}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\sigma}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{i}\breve{\tau}_{\alpha\beta}\,;

(4)

(N,K)
induces an almost CR structure
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
.

If any of these conditions holds,
\mathcal{K}
is geodesic and its shear commutes with the screen bundle complex structure.

Remark 4.17
.

Condition (
1
) tells us that the splitting of
{}^{\mathbf{C}}H_{K}
into
H^{(1,0)}_{K}
and
H^{(0,1)}_{K}
is preserved along the integral curves of
\mathcal{K}
. In particular, the distribution
\underline{H}
on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
inherits this splitting, which is equivalent to an almost CR structure, as claimed by (
4
).

Proof.

The equivalence between (
1
) and (
2
) is clear. For the almost Robinson structure to be preserved along
K
, i.e.
\mathsterling_{k}v\in\Gamma(\overline{N})
for any
k\in\Gamma(K)
,
v\in\Gamma(\overline{N})
, we must have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\kappa\left(\mathsterling_{k}e_{\alpha}\right)=-\nabla\kappa\left(k,e_{\alpha}\right)=-\gamma_{\alpha}\,,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=g\left(\mathsterling_{k}e_{\alpha},e_{\beta}\right)=g\left(\nabla_{k}e_{\alpha},e_{\beta}\right)-\nabla\kappa\left(e_{\alpha},e_{\beta}\right)=\frac{1}{2\mathrm{i}}\zeta_{\alpha\beta}-\tau_{\alpha\beta}-\sigma_{\alpha\beta}\,.

for any adapted frame
(\ell,e_{\alpha},e_{\bar{\alpha}},k)
and
\kappa=g(k,\cdot)
. It now follows that
\gamma_{\alpha}=0
, and taking the symmetric and skew-symmetric parts yields
-2\mathrm{i}\tau_{\alpha\beta}+\zeta_{\alpha\beta}=\sigma_{\alpha\beta}=0
. This computation shows that conditions (
1
) and (
3
) are equivalent.

The equivalence between (
2
) and (
4
) can be proved following
[
63
]
. One can always find a complex Robinson
(m+1)
-form
\nu
such that
\mathsterling_{k}\nu=0
for some optical vector field
k
. So
\nu
is the pullback of a complex
(m+1)
-form
\underline{\nu}
on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
. This
\underline{\nu}
clearly shares the same algebraic properties of
\nu
. In particular, it is simple, and
\underline{\theta}^{0}\wedge\underline{\nu}=0
, where
\underline{\theta}^{0}
is a real
1
-form that pulls back to an optical
1
-form on
\mathcal{M}
, and annihilates a rank-
2m
distribution
\underline{H}
on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
. This means that
\mathrm{span}(\underline{\nu})=\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{m+1}\mathrm{Ann}(\underline{H}^{(0,1)})
for some complex rank-
m
vector subbundle
\underline{H}^{(0,1)}
of
{}^{\mathbf{C}}\underline{H}
. The story for the complex conjugate of
\nu
is entirely analogous, and yields
a complex rank-
m
vector subbundle
\underline{H}^{(1,0)}
of
{}^{\mathbf{C}}\underline{H}
. It is then straightforward to check
\underline{H}^{(1,0)}\cap\underline{H}^{(0,1)}=\{0\}
at any point, i.e.
{}^{\mathbf{C}}\underline{H}=\underline{H}^{(1,0)}\oplus\underline{H}^{(0,1)}
, which defines the almost CR structure on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
as required.

The last claim of the proposition follows from Lemma
4.7
.
∎

In
[
63
]
, the authors show how to construct Robinson manifolds as trivial lines bundles over CR manifolds. Here, we generalise the construction to nearly Robinson manifolds. The proof of the following result is self-evident.

Proposition 4.18
.

Let
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{J})
be a
(2m+1)
-dimensional oriented almost CR manifold, and
\mathcal{M}:=\mathbf{R}\times\underline{\mathcal{M}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varpi}}{{\longrightarrow}}\underline{\mathcal{M}}
be a trivial line bundle over
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
. Fix a triplet
\left((\underline{\theta}^{0},\underline{\theta}^{\alpha},\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}}),h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}},\lambda\right)
where

•

(\underline{\theta}^{0},\underline{\theta}^{\alpha},\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}})
is a CR-coframe on
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{J})
,

•

h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
is a positive-definite Hermitian matrix depending smoothly on
\mathcal{M}
, and

•

\lambda
is a
1
-form on
\mathcal{M}
such that
\lambda\wedge\varpi^{*}\underline{\varepsilon}
does not vanish at any point for any non-vanishing
(2m+1)
-form
\underline{\varepsilon}
on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
.

Then
(\mathcal{M},g)
is an oriented and time-oriented Lorentzian manifold with metric

(4.15)

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle=4\,\varpi^{*}\underline{\theta}^{0}\,\lambda+2\,h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\varpi^{*}\underline{\theta}^{\alpha}\,\varpi^{*}\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\beta}}\,,

and
(N,K)
, where
N=\mathrm{Ann}(\varpi^{*}\underline{\theta}^{0},\varpi^{*}\underline{\theta}^{\alpha})
and
K=\mathrm{Ann}(\varpi^{*}\underline{\theta}^{0})^{\perp}
, defines a nearly Robinson structure on
(\mathcal{M},g)
. In particular,
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
is the leaf space of the congruence of null geodesics tangent to
K
.

Any two such triplets
\left((\underline{\theta}^{0},\underline{\theta}^{\alpha},\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}}),h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}},\lambda\right)
and
\left((\widehat{\underline{\theta}}{}^{0},\widehat{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha},\overline{\widehat{\underline{\theta}}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}}),\widehat{h}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}},\widehat{\lambda}\right)
where
(\underline{\theta}^{0},\underline{\theta}{}^{\alpha},\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}})
and
(\widehat{\underline{\theta}}{}^{0},\widehat{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha},\overline{\widehat{\underline{\theta}}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}})
are related by (
4.12
) define the same metric (
4.15
) if and only if
\underline{\psi}{}_{\alpha}{}^{\gamma}
is an element of
\mathbf{U}(m)
at every point, i.e.

\displaystyle\widehat{h}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}

\displaystyle=h_{\gamma\bar{\delta}}(\underline{\psi}^{-1}){}_{\alpha}{}^{\gamma}(\underline{\psi}^{-1}){}_{\bar{\beta}}{}^{\bar{\delta}}\,,

and
\lambda
transforms as

\displaystyle\widehat{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{-\underline{\varphi}}\lambda-\frac{1}{2}\underline{\psi}{}_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\underline{\phi}{}_{\beta}\underline{\theta}^{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\underline{\psi}{}_{\bar{\alpha}}{}^{\bar{\beta}}\underline{\phi}{}_{\bar{\beta}}\overline{\underline{\theta}}^{\bar{\alpha}}-\frac{1}{2}\underline{\phi}{}_{\beta}\underline{\phi}{}^{\beta}\underline{\theta}^{0}\,.

Remark 4.19
.

Variations of the above construction are possible by replacing the
\mathbf{R}
-factor of
\mathcal{M}
by
\mathbf{R}_{>0}
or
S^{1}
for instance.

Definition 4.20
.

We shall refer to any nearly Robinson structure constructed on
\mathcal{M}:=\mathbf{R}\times\underline{\mathcal{M}}\longrightarrow\underline{\mathcal{M}}
as in Proposition
4.18
, as a
lift
of the almost CR manifold
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{J})
. The pullbacks of the
1
-forms
(\underline{\theta}^{0},\underline{\theta}^{\alpha},\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}})
will be referred to as
horizontal
, and the
1
-form
\lambda
as
vertical
(with respect to the fibration
\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\underline{\mathcal{M}}
).

Remark 4.21
.

We emphasise that the metric constructed in Proposition
4.18
is
not
canonical in general. To do away with the choice of CR coframe and
1
-form
\lambda
, while fixing the conformal class
[h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}]
, one needs to introduce the notion of
generalised almost Robinson geometry
, which is dealt with in Section
6
– see Proposition
6.3
.

Before we proceed, we give the converse of Proposition
4.18
– see
[
63
]
for the involutive case.

Proposition 4.22
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be a nearly Robinson manifold with congruence of null geodesics
\mathcal{K}
. Then
\mathcal{M}
is locally diffeomorphic to the trivial line bundle
\mathbf{R}\times\underline{\mathcal{M}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varpi}}{{\longrightarrow}}\underline{\mathcal{M}}
, where
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
is the local leaf space of
\mathcal{K}
and is equipped with an almost CR structure
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
. Further, locally,
g
takes the form (
4.15
) for some CR coframe
(\underline{\theta}^{0},\underline{\theta}^{\alpha},\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}})
, Hermitian matrix
h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
depending smoothly on
\mathcal{M}
, and
1
-form
\lambda
on
\mathcal{M}
that never vanishes on
K
.

Proof.

Note that
\mathcal{M}
is locally diffeomorphic to the line bundle
\mathbf{R}\times\underline{\mathcal{M}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varpi}}{{\longrightarrow}}\underline{\mathcal{M}}
, where each fiber is a null curve of
\mathcal{K}
. By Proposition
4.16
,
(N,K)
descends to an almost CR structure
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
on the local leaf space
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
of
\mathcal{K}
: following our convention,
N/{}^{\mathbf{C}}K
and
\overline{N}/{}^{\mathbf{C}}K
descend to the eigenbundles
\underline{H}^{(0,1)}
and
\underline{H}^{(1,0)}
of
\underline{J}
respectively. Let
(\underline{\theta}^{0},\underline{\theta}^{\alpha},\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}})
be a coframe on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
where
\mathrm{span}(\underline{\theta}^{0})=\mathrm{Ann}(\underline{H})
and
\mathrm{span}(\underline{\theta}^{0},\underline{\theta}^{\alpha})=\mathrm{Ann}(\underline{H}^{(0,1)})
. Then
\mathrm{span}(\varpi^{*}\underline{\theta}^{0})=\mathrm{Ann}(\underline{H})
and
\mathrm{span}(\varpi^{*}\underline{\theta}^{0},\varpi^{*}\underline{\theta}^{\alpha})=\mathrm{Ann}(N)
, and it follows immediate that
g
must be of the form (
4.15
) where
h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
and
\lambda
have the required properties.
∎

Propositions
4.18
and
4.22
thus allow us to relate the geometric properties of a nearly Robinson manifold
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
with those of the almost CR leaf space
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{J})
of its associated congruence of null geodesics
\mathcal{K}
. This can be seen at four levels:

•

the involutivity of
N
is equivalent to that of
\underline{H}^{(0,1)}
— see Section
4.10
;

•

the twist
\breve{\tau}_{ij}
of
\mathcal{K}
encodes the geometric and algebraic properties of the Levi form
\underline{\bm{\mathsf{L}}}
of
\underline{H}
;

•

what remains of the shear, that is, its
(1,1)
-part
\breve{\sigma}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
, and the expansion
\breve{\epsilon}
of
\mathcal{K}
obstruct the existence of a conformal or metric structure on
\underline{H}
;

•

further denegeracy conditions of the intrinsic torsion of
(N,K)
will also depend on the choice of the
1
-form
\lambda
— see Lemma
4.55
for instance.

Unlike
N
and
\breve{\tau}_{ij}
, which are tied to the properties of
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
, the shear and expansion depend only on the screen bundle metric of (
4.15
) — see also Remark
4.30
below.

To delve into this matter further, we must bear in mind that the coframe
(\underline{\theta}^{0},\underline{\theta}^{\alpha},\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}})
on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
does
not
in general pull back to a Robinson coframe on
\mathcal{M}
by simply adjoining the
1
-form
\lambda
, in the sense that
(\underline{\theta}^{\alpha})
do
not
form a unitary coframe with respect to
h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
. This essentially depends on the choice of
h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
. To be precise, a Robinson coframe
(\kappa,\theta^{\alpha},\overline{\theta}{}^{\bar{\alpha}},\lambda)
for the metric (
4.15
), so that
(\theta^{\alpha})
is unitary for
h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
, is related to
(\underline{\theta}^{0},\underline{\theta}^{\alpha},\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}})
via

(4.16)

\displaystyle\kappa

\displaystyle=2\varpi^{*}\underline{\theta}^{0}\,,

\displaystyle\theta^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\psi{}_{\beta}{}^{\alpha}\left(\varpi^{*}\underline{\theta}^{\beta}\right)+\phi^{\alpha}\underline{\theta}^{0}\,,

for some smooth functions
\psi^{\alpha}
and
\psi{}_{\beta}{}^{\alpha}
on
\mathcal{M}
, where
\psi{}_{\beta}{}^{\alpha}
takes values in
\mathbf{GL}(m,\mathbf{C})
. Note that one can always choose our Robinson coframe such that
\psi^{\alpha}=0
in (
4.16
). When
m=1
, we have the decomposition
\mathbf{GL}(1,\mathbf{C})\cong\mathbf{C}^{*}\cong\mathbf{R}_{>0}\cdot\mathbf{U}(1)
, in which case we always have
\psi{}_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}=r\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\phi}\delta_{\alpha}^{\beta}
where
r,\phi
are real with
r>0
. Now, the space of all Hermitian forms on
\mathbf{C}^{m}
is isomorphic to the homogeneous space
\mathbf{GL}(m,\mathbf{C})/\mathbf{U}(m)
of real dimension
m^{2}
. Thus, the failure of
(\underline{\theta}^{\alpha})
to be unitary with respect to some scalar multiple of
h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
at any point is measured by an element of
\mathbf{GL}(m,\mathbf{C})\mod\mathbf{U}(m)
.

At present, let us relate the twist of
\mathcal{K}
to the Levi forms of
\underline{H}
and
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
. Using (
4.16
), we find

\displaystyle\underline{\mathsf{h}}{}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}

\displaystyle=\tau{}_{\gamma\bar{\delta}}\psi{}_{\alpha}{}^{\gamma}\overline{\psi}{}_{\bar{\beta}}{}^{\bar{\delta}}\,,

\displaystyle 2\,\underline{\mathsf{L}}{}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=\tau{}_{\gamma\delta}\psi{}_{\alpha}{}^{\gamma}\psi{}_{\beta}{}^{\delta}\,.

From the first of these equations, we conclude that the
\mathcal{G}_{-1}^{1,0}\oplus\mathcal{G}_{-1}^{1,2}
-component
\breve{\tau}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
of the intrinsic torsion encodes the signature of the Levi form of
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
, while the
\mathcal{G}_{-1}^{1,1}
-component
\breve{\tau}_{\alpha\beta}
of the intrinsic torsion encodes the partial integrability of
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
. As a direct consequence, we obtain the following three propositions:

Proposition 4.23
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be a nearly Robinson manifold with congruence of null geodesics
\mathcal{K}
and almost CR leaf space
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{J})
. The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

the intrinsic torsion has non-degenerate
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1}
-component
\breve{\tau}_{ij}
;

(2)

\mathcal{K}
is maximally twisting;

(3)

(\underline{H},\underline{J})
is contact.

Proposition 4.24
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be a nearly Robinson manifold with congruence of null geodesics
\mathcal{K}
and almost CR leaf space
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{J})
. The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

the intrinsic torsion is also a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,1}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\tau}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{\sigma}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=0\,;

(2)

the twist of
\mathcal{K}
commutes with the screen bundle complex structure;

(3)

(\underline{H},\underline{J})
is partially integrable.

Proposition 4.25
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be a nearly Robinson manifold with congruence of null geodesics
\mathcal{K}
and almost CR leaf space
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{J})
. The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

the intrinsic torsion is also a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{3,0}
with non-degenerate
\mathcal{G}_{-1}^{1,0}\oplus\mathcal{G}_{-1}^{1,2}
-component, i.e.

\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\tau}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{\sigma}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\mbox{with non-degenerate $\breve{\tau}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}$}\,;

(2)

\mathcal{K}
is maximally twisting, and the twist commutes with the screen bundle complex structure;

(3)

(\underline{H},\underline{J})
is partially integrable and contact, and is thus endowed with a subconformal contact structure
\mathbf{c}_{\underline{H},\underline{J}}
.

Remark 4.26
.

The metrics in the subconformal structure
\underline{\mathbf{c}}_{\underline{H},\underline{J}}
of Proposition
4.25
are in one-to-one correspondence with optical vector fields
k
such that
\mathsterling_{k}\kappa=0
where
\kappa=g(k,\cdot)
.

Remark 4.27
.

Special cases of the nearly Robinson manifolds given in Propositions
4.23
,
4.24
and
4.25
are those for which
\breve{\tau}^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=0
, i.e. the twist determines the almost Robinson structure — see Section
4.9
.

As has already been treated in
[
25
]
, the abscence of shear induces a subconformal structure
\mathbf{c}_{\underline{H}}
on
\underline{H}
, in which metrics are in one-to-one correspondence with metrics in
[g]_{n.e.}
— see Remark
4.8
. This subconformal structure is however
not
compatible with the complex structure
\underline{J}
in general. Combining this fact with Proposition
4.25
yields the following result:

Proposition 4.28
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be a nearly Robinson manifold with congruence of null geodesics
\mathcal{K}
and almost CR leaf space
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{J})
. The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

the intrinsic torsion is also a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{2,0}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{3,0}
with non-degenerate
\mathcal{G}_{-1}^{1,0}\oplus\mathcal{G}_{-1}^{1,2}
-component, i.e.

\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\tau}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{\sigma}_{ij}=\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\mbox{with non-degenerate $\breve{\tau}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}$}\,;

(2)

\mathcal{K}
is maximally twisting and non-shearing, and the twist commutes with the screen bundle complex structure;

(3)

(\underline{H},\underline{J})
is partially integrable and contact, and thus equipped with a subconformal structure
\underline{\mathbf{c}}_{\underline{H},\underline{J}}
, and also inherits a subconformal structure
\underline{\mathbf{c}}_{\underline{H}}
from
[g]_{n.e.}
.

Remark 4.29
.

The subconformal structures
\underline{\mathbf{c}}_{\underline{H},\underline{J}}
and
\underline{\mathbf{c}}_{\underline{H}}
are distinct in general. They can however be related in the following way. Since
\mathcal{K}
is maximally twisting and non-shearing, we know from
[
25
]
that there exists a unique optical vector field
k^{a}
such that the twist of
k^{a}
is normalised to
\tau_{ij}\tau^{ij}=2m
for any metric
g
in
[g]_{n.e.}
. With this normalisation, for each choice of metric
g
in
[g]_{n.e.}
, the Levi form of
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
is related to the twist of
k
by

(4.17)

\displaystyle\underline{\mathsf{h}}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=\underline{h}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}+\underline{\tau}^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\,.

We thus see that
\underline{\mathsf{h}}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
is a deformation of the metric
\underline{h}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
by the tracefree part of the twist
\underline{\tau}^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
. In particular,
\underline{\mathbf{c}}_{\underline{H},\underline{J}}
and
\underline{\mathbf{c}}_{\underline{H}}
coincide if and only if
\underline{\tau}^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=0
, i.e. the nearly Robinson structure is twist-induced. In Section
4.9
, we shall focus on a special case of the aforementioned results where the only non-vanishing of the twist lies in
\mathcal{G}_{-1}^{1,0}
.

Remark 4.30
.

Proposition
4.18
allows us to construct an almost Robinson manifold
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
with prescribed intrinsic torsion from a chosen almost CR manifold
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{J})
. While
(N,K)
, including the twist of the congruence
\mathcal{K}
, is determined by
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
, there is more freedom as to the choice of Hermitian matrix
h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
, which will impact the
(1,1)
-part of the shear
\breve{\sigma}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
and the expansion
\breve{\epsilon}
of
\mathcal{K}
. In fact, using (
4.16
), we compute

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon}{m}h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}+\sigma_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=h_{\gamma\bar{\beta}}\dot{\psi}_{\delta}{}^{\gamma}(\psi^{-1})_{\alpha}{}^{\delta}+h_{\alpha\bar{\delta}}\dot{\overline{\psi}}_{\bar{\gamma}}{}^{\bar{\delta}}(\overline{\psi}{}^{-1})_{\bar{\beta}}{}^{\bar{\gamma}}\,,

where
\dot{\psi}_{\delta}{}^{\gamma}:=\mathsterling_{k}\psi_{\delta}{}^{\gamma}
for the optical vector field
k=g^{-1}(\kappa,\cdot)
. In particular, we interpret the
\mathcal{G}_{-1}^{2,0}
-component
\breve{\sigma}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
of the intrinsic torsion as the infinitesimal obstruction to
(\underline{\theta}^{\alpha})
being unitary with respect to
h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
.

Various considerations may dictate the choice of screen bundle Hermitian form
h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
. For
\mathcal{K}
to be non-shearing, we may set
h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=\mathrm{e}^{2\varphi}\underline{h}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
for some Hermitian form
\underline{h}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
on
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{J})
and smooth function
\varphi
on
\mathcal{M}
. If
\varphi
is a function on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
,
\mathcal{K}
is non-expanding too. There are two extreme cases to consider:

•

If
\underline{\mathsf{h}}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
is positive-definite, we
may
take
\underline{h}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=\underline{\mathsf{h}}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
. This is the case if
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
is partially integrable and contact: the resulting
(N,K)
is then said to be
twist-induced
, of which we shall say more in Section
4.9
.

•

If the almost CR structure is totally degenerate,
\mathcal{K}
is also non-twisting, and the resulting nearly Robinson manifold
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
is either of
Kundt type
or of
Robinson–Trautman type
— see Sections
4.12
and
4.13
.

There are further, intermediate, situations where the Levi form of
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
is degenerate but not identically zero. This allows for screen bundle metrics to be constructed partly from the Levi form.

4.8.
Conditions on the Robinson
3
-forms

The purpose of this section is to highlight the fact that for a given almost Robinson manifold
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
, conditions on Robinson
3
-forms do not necessarily entail that
(N,K)
is nearly Robinson. The next two propositions illustrate the point.

Proposition 4.31
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be an almost Robinson manifold with congruence of null curves
\mathcal{K}
with leaf space
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
. The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

any Robinson
3
-form
\rho_{abc}
is preserved along
K
, i.e.
\mathsterling_{k}\rho_{abc}=f\,\rho_{abc}
for some smooth function
f
, and any optical vector field
k
.

(2)

the intrinsic torsion is a section of

(\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[2\mathrm{i}:1]}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{2,1}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\sigma}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=-2\mathrm{i}\breve{\tau}_{\alpha\beta}\,.

(3)

any Robinson
3
-form induces a
3
-form on the leaf space
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H})
.

If any of these conditions holds,
\mathcal{K}
is geodesic and its shear anticommutes with the screen bundle complex structure.

Proof.

Choose splitting operators
(\ell^{a},\delta^{a}_{\alpha},\delta^{a}_{\bar{\alpha}},k^{a})
and let
\rho_{abc}
be the Robinson
3
-form associated to the optical
1
-form
\kappa_{a}=g_{ab}k^{b}
. The condition that
\rho_{abc}
be preserved along
K
gives

\displaystyle k^{d}\nabla_{d}\rho_{abc}-3k^{d}\nabla_{[a}\rho_{bc]d}

\displaystyle=f\,\rho_{abc}\,.

Now, contracting with
\delta^{a}_{\alpha}\delta^{b}_{\beta}\delta^{c}_{\bar{\gamma}}
,
\delta^{a}_{\alpha}\delta^{b}_{\beta}\ell^{c}
and
\delta^{a}_{\alpha}\delta^{b}_{\bar{\beta}}\ell^{c}
yields
\gamma_{\alpha}=0
,
\zeta_{\alpha\beta}=-2\mathrm{i}\tau_{\alpha\beta}
and
\sigma_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=0
respectively. This proves the equivalence between conditions (
1
) and (
2
). The equivalence with condition (
3
) follows from the geometric interpretation of condition (
1
). An application of Lemma
4.7
completes the proof.
∎

Proposition 4.32
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be an almost Robinson manifold with congruence of null curves
\mathcal{K}
. The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

the intrinsic torsion is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{3,0}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}=0\,.

(2)

any Robinson
3
-form is recurrent along
K
.

(3)

N
is parallel along
K
.

If any of these conditions holds,
\mathcal{K}
is geodesic.

Proof.

The equivalence between (
1
) and (
2
) is tautological, while the equivalence between (
2
) and (
3
) follows from the definition of
\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}
. That
\mathcal{K}
is geodesic in this case follows from
\breve{\gamma}_{i}=0
.
∎

Returning to nearly Robinson manifolds, we have the following lemma:

Lemma 4.33
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be a nearly Robinson manifold with congruence of null geodesics
\mathcal{K}
and almost CR leaf space
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{J})
. The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

any Robinson
3
-form is parallel along
K
;

(2)

N
is parallel along
K
;

(3)

the twist of
\mathcal{K}
commutes with the screen bundle complex structure;

(4)

(\underline{H},\underline{J})
is partially integrable.

Proof.

For a nearly Robinson manifold, the intrinsic torsion is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{2}
, and so in particular,
\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}=2\mathrm{i}\breve{\tau}_{\alpha\beta}
. Hence
\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}
and
\breve{\tau}_{\alpha\beta}=0
, and the result follows immediately by Lemma
4.7
, Proposition
4.24
and Proposition
4.32
.
∎

It can readily be checked that the nearly Robinson manifolds of Propositions
4.24
,
4.25
and
4.28
all satisfy this property.

4.9.
Twist-induced almost Robinson structures

We shall now present a special case of almost Robinson structures, which arise from the twist of an optical geometry.

Proposition 4.34
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,K)
be an optical geometry of dimension
2m+2
with congruence of null curves
\mathcal{K}
. Let
\kappa_{a}
be an optical
1
-form and set
\tau_{abc}:=3\,\kappa_{[a}\nabla_{b}\kappa_{c]}
. The following two conditions are equivalent:

(1)

the
3
-form
\tau_{abc}
satisfies

(4.18)

\displaystyle\tau_{ab}{}^{e}\tau_{ecd}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{m}\tau_{[a}{}^{ef}g_{b][c}\tau_{d]ef}\neq 0\,;

(2)

\mathcal{K}
is geodesic and twisting, and there exists a unique optical vector field
k
whose twist endormorphism
h^{-1}\circ\tau
is a bundle complex structure
J
compatible with
h
on the screen bundle
(H_{K},h)
, i.e.
J=h^{-1}\circ\tau
. In particular, the twist of
\mathcal{K}
induces an almost Robinson structure
(N,K)
on
(\mathcal{M},g,K)
, and
\kappa=g(k,\cdot)
determines a unique Robinson
3
-form given by

(4.19)

\displaystyle\rho_{abc}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{[a}\nabla_{b}\kappa_{c]}\,.

Proof.

Choose splitting operators
(\ell^{a},\delta^{a}_{i},k^{a})
. Then we can write
\nabla_{[a}\kappa_{b]}=\lambda_{[a}\gamma_{b]}+\tau_{ab}+\alpha_{[a}\kappa_{b]}
for some
\gamma_{a}
,
\tau_{ab}
and
\alpha_{a}
, where
\ell^{a}\gamma_{a}=\ell^{a}\tau_{ab}=k^{a}\gamma_{a}=k^{a}\tau_{ab}=0
. Contracting (
4.18
) with
k^{a}\ell^{b}k^{c}\ell^{d}
and
\delta^{a}_{i}\ell^{b}\delta^{c}_{j}\ell^{d}
yields

(4.20)

\displaystyle\gamma_{i}\gamma^{i}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(4.21)

\displaystyle\tau_{i}{}^{k}\tau_{k}{}^{j}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2m}\tau_{k\ell}\tau^{k\ell}\delta_{i}^{j}\neq 0\,,

respectively, where
\gamma_{i}=\gamma_{a}\delta^{a}_{i}
and
\tau_{ij}=\tau_{ab}\delta^{a}_{i}\delta^{b}_{j}
. Since
h_{ij}
is positive-definite, equation (
4.20
) tells us that
\gamma_{i}=0
, i.e.
\mathcal{K}
is geodesic. Equation (
4.21
) tells us that we can rescale
k
by
\tfrac{\sqrt{2m}}{\|\tau\|}
so that the twist of the rescaled optical vector field satisfies

\displaystyle\tau_{i}{}^{k}\tau_{k}{}^{j}

\displaystyle=-\delta_{i}^{j}\,,

i.e.
h^{-1}\circ\tau
is a bundle complex structure on
H_{K}
. The uniqueness of
k
follows from the assumption that
K
is oriented.
∎

Remark 4.35
.

That equation (
4.19
) singles out an optical
1
-form also follows from the fact that LHS has boost weight
2
and the RHS boost weight
1
.

Definition 4.36
.

We shall refer to the almost Robinson structure given in Proposition
4.34
as a
twist-induced almost Robinson structure
.

Remark 4.37
.

Let us re-emphasise that by Proposition
4.34
, the congruence associated to a twist-induced almost Robinson structure is always geodesic and maximally twisting.

It is clear that an almost Robinson structure
(N,K)
is twist-induced if and only if its intrinsic torsion is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,2}
with non-vanishing
\mathcal{G}_{-1}^{1,0}
-component, i.e.

\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\tau}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{\tau}^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\breve{\tau}^{\omega}

\displaystyle\neq 0\,.

However, the following proposition tells us that the intrinsic torsion must in fact be a section of a subbundle thereof.

Proposition 4.38
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be an almost Robinson manifold with congruence of null curves
\mathcal{K}
. The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

(N,K)
is a twist-induced almost Robinson structure;

(2)

the intrinsic torsion of
(N,K)
is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,2}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{3,0}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,1}
with non-vanishing
\mathcal{G}_{-1}^{1,0}
-component, i.e.

\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\tau}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{\tau}^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{G}^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\breve{\tau}^{\omega}

\displaystyle\neq 0\,.

If any of these conditions holds,
N
is parallel along
K
.

Proof.

That (
2
) implies (
1
) is immediate since
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,2}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{3,0}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,1}
is a subbundle of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,2}
. For the converse, we note that a twist-induced almost Robinson structure singles out a preferred optical
1
-form
\kappa
such that
(\mathrm{d}\kappa)_{ab}=\omega_{ab}+\kappa_{[a}\alpha_{b]}
for some
1
-form
\alpha_{a}
, where
\omega_{ab}
is a representative of the screen bundle Hermitian structure. Note that the associated Robinson
3
-form is given by
\rho_{abc}=3\kappa_{[a}\omega_{bc]}
. Taking the exterior derivative of
\mathrm{d}\kappa
yields

(4.22)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{d}\omega)_{abc}+\omega_{[ab}\alpha_{c]}-\kappa_{[a}(\mathrm{d}\alpha)_{bc]}\,.

Choose a splitting
(\ell^{a},\delta^{a}_{\alpha},\delta^{a}_{\bar{\alpha}},k^{a})
. Contracting (
4.22
) with
k^{a}\delta^{b}_{\alpha}\delta^{c}_{\beta}
leads to

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=3(\mathrm{d}\omega)^{0}{}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=(\nabla\omega)^{0}{}_{\alpha\beta}-2\mathrm{i}\omega_{ab}\delta^{a}_{\alpha}\delta^{b}_{\beta}

\displaystyle=(\nabla\rho)^{0}{}_{\alpha\beta 0}=\zeta_{\alpha\beta}\,,

i.e.
\mathring{T}
is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{3,0}
. Now, contract (
4.22
) with
\delta^{a}_{\alpha}\delta^{b}_{\beta}\delta^{c}_{\gamma}
yields

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{d}\omega)_{\alpha\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle=(\nabla\rho)_{[\alpha\beta\gamma]0}=G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\,,

i.e.
\mathring{T}
is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,1}
, which completes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.39
.

Proposition
4.38
tells us that if the intrinsic torsion of a given almost Robinson manifold is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,2}
but not of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,0}
, then it must be a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,2}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{3,0}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,1}
. This should be contrasted with the situation regarding the Gray–Hervella classification of almost Hermitian manifolds
[
30
]
, which is briefly reviewed in Section
4.11
: the sixteen classes of almost Hermitian manifolds can be naturally arranged in terms of inclusions, which are shown to be strict in the sense that each class contains an almost Hermitian metric that does not belong to any of the other fifteen classes.

The next result is a direct consequence of Propositions
4.25
and
4.38
.

Proposition 4.40
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be an almost Robinson manifold with congruence of null curves
\mathcal{K}
. The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

(N,K)
is a twist-induced almost Robinson structure and the shear of
\mathcal{K}
commutes with the screen bundle complex structure;

(2)

the intrinsic torsion of
(N,K)
is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,2}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{2,1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{3,0}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,1}
with non-vanishing
\mathcal{G}_{-1}^{1,0}
-component, i.e.

\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\tau}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{\tau}^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=\breve{\sigma}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{G}^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\breve{\tau}^{\omega}

\displaystyle\neq 0\,.

If any of these conditions holds,
(N,K)
is a nearly Robinson structure and the induced almost CR leaf space
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{J})
of
\mathcal{K}
is partially integrable and contact, and is thus equipped with a subconformal structure
\underline{\mathbf{c}}_{\underline{H},\underline{J}}
compatible with
\underline{J}
.

The next proposition, which collects some of the results found in
[
106
]
, is a special case of Proposition
4.28
applied to a twist-induced almost Robinson structure — see also Remark
4.29
where equation (
4.17
) reduces to
\underline{\mathsf{h}}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=\underline{h}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
.

Proposition 4.41

(
[
106
]
)
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be an almost Robinson manifold with congruence of null curves
\mathcal{K}
. The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

(N,K)
is a twist-induced almost Robinson structure and
\mathcal{K}
is a non-shearing congruence of null geodesics;

(2)

the intrinsic torsion of
(N,K)
is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,2}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{2}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{3,0}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,3}
with non-zero
\mathcal{G}_{-1}^{1,0}
-component, i.e.

\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\tau}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{\tau}^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=\breve{\sigma}_{ij}=\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{G}^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=\breve{G}^{\circ}_{\bar{\alpha}\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\breve{\tau}^{\omega}

\displaystyle\neq 0\,.

If any of these conditions holds,
(N,K)
is a nearly Robinson structure and the almost CR leaf space
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{J})
of
\mathcal{K}
is partially integrable and contact.

In particular,
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{J})
is equipped with a positive-definite subconformal structure
\underline{\mathbf{c}}_{\underline{H},\underline{J}}
compatible with
\underline{J}
, which is also induced from
[g]_{n.e.}
.

Remark 4.42
.

In both Propositions
4.40
and
4.41
, the
\mathcal{G}_{0}^{1,2}
-component
\breve{G}^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}
of the intrinsic torsion of
(N,K)
can be identified with the Nijenhuis tensor of
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
.

Remark 4.43
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be a twist-induced almost Robinson manifold with non-shearing congruence of null geodesics
\mathcal{K}
. Applying Proposition 4.35 of
[
25
]
, one can show that for each metric
\widehat{g}
in
[g]_{n.e}
, there exists a unique generator
k
of
\mathcal{K}
and a null vector field
\ell
such that
\widehat{g}(k,\ell)=1
and
\kappa=\widehat{g}(k,\cdot)
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\kappa(k,\cdot)

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\kappa(\ell,\cdot)

\displaystyle=0\,.

We shall elaborate on this result in the conformal setting in Section
5
.

4.10.
Almost Robinson structures as almost null structures

To obtain further geometric interpretations of the subbundles of
\mathcal{G}
, we shall presently regard the almost Robinson structure
(N,K)
as an almost null structure
N
on
(\mathcal{M},{}^{\mathbf{C}}g)
in its own right, i.e. without any reference to the complex conjugate
\overline{N}
. This perspective is in part motivated by the potential involutivity of
N
. The structure group of
N
is the stabiliser
R
of an MTN vector subspace of
\mathbf{C}^{2m+2}
in
\mathbf{SO}(2m+2,\mathbf{C})
. As we shall be using a spinorial approach, we shall assume with no loss of generality, at least locally, that
R
is a subgroup of
\mathbf{Spin}(2m+2,\mathbf{C})
. We shall then identify
N
as the kernel of the map
\nu_{a}^{\mathbf{A}}:=\gamma_{a\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}{}^{\mathbf{A}}\nu^{\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}:\Gamma(T\mathcal{M})\rightarrow\Gamma(S_{-})
for some Robinson spinor
\nu^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}
. In particular, with a choice of dual
N^{*}
, the image of
\nu_{a}^{\mathbf{A}}
is isomorphic to
N^{*}\cong T\mathcal{M}/N
— see the discussion in Section
3.2.6
. The intrinsic torsion of such a structure is already investigated in
[
102
]
, and we shall appeal to the results contained therein for the subsequent analysis.

Theorem 4.44
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,K,N)
be a
(2m+2)
-dimensional almost Robinson manifold with intrinsic torsion
\mathring{T}
. Let
\nu^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}
be a Robinson spinor and set
\nu_{a}^{\mathbf{A}}:=\gamma_{a\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}{}^{\mathbf{A}}\nu^{\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}
. Then, for
m>2
,

(4.23)

\displaystyle\mathring{T}

\displaystyle\in\Gamma(\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,1})

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\left(\nu^{a[\mathbf{A}}\nabla_{a}\nu^{b\mathbf{B}}\right)\nu_{b}^{\mathbf{C}]}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(4.24)

\displaystyle\mathring{T}

\displaystyle\in\Gamma(\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,2})

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\left(\nu^{a(\mathbf{A}}\nabla_{a}\nu^{b\mathbf{B})}\right)\nu_{b}^{\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(4.25)

\displaystyle\mathring{T}

\displaystyle\in\Gamma(\not{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{0,0})

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\left(\nabla_{a}\nu^{b\mathbf{B}}\right)\nu_{b}^{\mathbf{C}}+\frac{2}{m}\left(\nu_{a}^{[\mathbf{B}}\nabla_{b}\nu^{b\mathbf{C}]}+\nu^{b[\mathbf{B}}\nabla_{b}\nu_{a}^{\mathbf{C}]}\right)

\displaystyle=0\,,

(4.26)

\displaystyle\mathring{T}

\displaystyle\in\Gamma\left(({\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{0\times 1}})_{[2\mathrm{i}:1]}\right)

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\nu^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\nabla_{a}\nu^{a\mathbf{B}}-\nu^{a\mathbf{B}}\nabla_{b}\nu^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=0\,.

In dimension six, i.e.
m=2
, equivalences (
4.24
) and (
4.26
) hold, but equivalences (
4.23
) and (
4.25
) are replaced by

(4.27)

\displaystyle\mathring{T}

\displaystyle\in\Gamma((\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[4\mathrm{i}:-1]})

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\left(\nu^{a[\mathbf{A}}\nabla_{a}\nu^{b\mathbf{B}}\right)\nu_{b}^{\mathbf{C}]}=0\,,

and

(4.28)

\mathring{T}\in\Gamma(\not{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{0,0})\Longleftrightarrow\\
\left(\nabla_{a}\nu^{b\mathbf{B}}\right)\nu_{b}^{\mathbf{C}}+\left(\nu_{a}^{[\mathbf{B}}\nabla_{b}\nu^{b\mathbf{C}]}+\nu^{b[\mathbf{B}}\nabla_{b}\nu_{a}^{\mathbf{C}]}\right)-\frac{2}{3}\nu^{b\mathbf{A}}\nabla_{b}\nu_{\mathbf{A}}\gamma_{a}{}^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}}=0\,,

respectively. For the last equation, we have used the bundle isomorphisms
S_{\pm}\cong S_{\mp}^{*}
and
{}^{\mathbf{C}}T\mathcal{M}\cong\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{2}S_{+}\cong\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{2}S_{-}
.

Proof.

We choose a splitting
{}^{C}T\mathcal{M}=N^{*}\oplus N
. With reference to the notation of Section
3.2.6
, we may choose a connection
1
-form
\Gamma_{ab}{}^{c}
with values in
\mathfrak{so}(2m+2,\mathbf{C})
for
\nabla
, so that
\left(\nabla_{a}\nu^{b\mathbf{B}}\right)\nu_{b}^{\mathbf{C}}=\Gamma_{a}{}^{BC}\delta_{B}^{\mathbf{B}}\delta_{C}^{\mathbf{C}}
and in particular,
\left(\nu^{a\mathbf{A}}\nabla_{a}\nu^{b\mathbf{B}}\right)\nu_{b}^{\mathbf{C}}=\Gamma^{ABC}\delta_{A}^{\mathbf{A}}\delta_{B}^{\mathbf{B}}\delta_{C}^{\mathbf{C}}
.
Then, one can show
[
102
]

RHS of (
4.23
) and (
4.27
)

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\Gamma^{[ABC]}

\displaystyle=0\,,

RHS of (
4.24
)

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(AB)C}

\displaystyle=0\,,

RHS of (
4.25
)

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\left(\Gamma_{A}{}^{BC}\right)_{\circ}=\Gamma^{ABC}

\displaystyle=0\,,

RHS of (
4.26
)

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\Gamma_{B}{}^{BA}=\Gamma^{ABC}

\displaystyle=0\,,

and in dimension six

RHS of (
4.28
)

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\left(\Gamma_{A}{}^{BC}\right)_{\circ}=\Gamma^{(AB)C}

\displaystyle=0\,.

Here
\left(\Gamma_{A}{}^{BC}\right)_{\circ}=\Gamma_{A}{}^{BC}-\frac{2}{m}\delta_{A}^{[B|}\Gamma_{D}{}^{D|C]}
. We can immediately deduce that

\displaystyle\Gamma^{[ABC]}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\bar{\alpha}\bar{\beta}\bar{\gamma}}=\zeta_{\bar{\alpha}\bar{\beta}}+4\mathrm{i}\tau_{\bar{\alpha}\bar{\beta}}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(AB)C}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\bar{\alpha}\bar{\beta}\bar{\gamma}}=\sigma_{\bar{\alpha}\bar{\beta}}=2\mathrm{i}\tau_{\bar{\alpha}\bar{\beta}}-\zeta_{\bar{\alpha}\bar{\beta}}=\gamma_{\bar{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\left(\Gamma_{A}{}^{BC}\right)_{\circ}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle B_{\bar{\alpha}\bar{\beta}}=G^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}\bar{\gamma}}=2\mathrm{i}(m-1)E_{\bar{\alpha}}+G_{\bar{\alpha}}=\tau^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=\sigma_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{B}{}^{BA}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle-2\mathrm{i}E_{\bar{\alpha}}+G_{\bar{\alpha}}=\tau^{\omega}=\epsilon

\displaystyle=0\,,

where
B_{\bar{\alpha}\bar{\beta}}
,
G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\bar{\alpha}\bar{\beta}\bar{\gamma}}
,
G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\bar{\alpha}\bar{\beta}\bar{\gamma}}
,
G^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}\bar{\gamma}}
,
\zeta_{\bar{\alpha}\bar{\beta}}
,
\tau_{\bar{\alpha}\bar{\beta}}
,
E_{\bar{\alpha}}
and
\gamma_{\bar{\alpha}}
are the complex conjugates of the tensors defined in Section
4.4
. The result now follows immediately from the definition of the
Q
-invariant bundles and Theorem
3.18
— this is clearly independent from the choice of dual
N^{*}
.
∎

As a consequence, we obtain:

Proposition 4.45
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be an almost Robinson manifold with congruence of null curves
\mathcal{K}
. The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

any Robinson spinor
\nu^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}
satisfies

(4.29)

\displaystyle\left(\nu^{a\mathbf{A}}\nabla_{a}\nu^{b\mathbf{B}}\right)\nu_{b}^{\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle=0\,;

(2)

any Robinson spinor
\nu^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}
is recurrent along
N
, i.e.

(4.30)

\displaystyle\left(\nu^{a\mathbf{A}}\nabla_{a}\nu^{[\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}\right)\nu^{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}]}

\displaystyle=0\,;

(3)

the intrinsic torsion of
(N,K)
is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,2}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\tau}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{\sigma}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{G}^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=\breve{G}^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=0\,;

(4)

N
is in involution, i.e. for any
v,w\in\Gamma(N)
,
[v,w]\in\Gamma(N)
;

(5)

N
is parallel along itself, i.e. for any
v,w\in\Gamma(N)
,
\nabla_{v}w\in\Gamma(N)
;

(6)

(N,K)
induces a CR structure on the leaf space of
\mathcal{K}
.

If any of these conditions holds,
\mathcal{K}
is geodesic, its twist and shear commute with the screen bundle complex structure, and
N
is parallel along
K
.

Proof.

One can show
[
102
]
that equations (
4.29
) and (
4.30
) are equivalent, from which follows the equivalence (
1
) and (
2
). The equivalence between (
1
) and (
3
) is a direct consequence of Theorem
4.44
. The equivalence between (
1
) and (
6
) is already given in
[
41
,
102
]
, while the equivalence between (
4
) and (
5
) is established in
[
104
]
, and that between (
4
) and (
6
) in
[
63
]
.
∎

Remark 4.46
.

It is important to note that the involutivity of the almost Robinson structure
(N,K)
does
not
imply that the Robinson
3
-form is preserved along
K
, and thus, may not descend to the leaf space
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
— see Proposition
4.31
. If it did, it would imply that the congruence of null geodesics is non-shearing, which is not true in general except in dimension four.

Remark 4.47
.

In the analytic category, one may complexify
(\mathcal{M},g)
to a complex Riemanian manifold
(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}},\widetilde{g})
, and extend
N
analytically to
(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}},\widetilde{g})

[
117
,
119
,
20
]
. By the Frobenius theorem, condition (
4
) of Proposition
4.45
is equivalent to the local existence of a complex foliation of
(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}},\widetilde{g})
by
(m+1)
-dimensional complex submanifolds on which
\widetilde{g}
is totally degenerate. Condition (
5
) then tells us that these leaves are totally geodesic with respect to the Levi-Civita connection of
\widetilde{g}
. See also
[
77
,
41
,
107
]
for further details.

The following proposition gives a characterisation of a certain class of almost Robinson manifolds, not necessarily nearly Robinson.

Proposition 4.48
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be an almost Robinson manifold with congruence of null curves
\mathcal{K}
. The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

any Robinson
3
-form
\rho_{abc}
satisfies

(4.31)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\rho

\displaystyle=\alpha\wedge\rho\,,

\displaystyle\mbox{for some $1$-form $\alpha$}\,;

(2)

the intrinsic torsion is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,3}
(or
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,3}
in dimension six), i.e.

\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\tau}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{\tau}^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=\breve{\sigma}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{G}^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=\breve{G}^{\circ}_{\bar{\alpha}\beta\gamma}=0\,.

Further, assuming that any of these conditions holds:

•

\mathcal{K}
is geodesic, its shear anti-commutes with the screen bundle complex structure.

•

(N,K)
is twist-induced if and only if
\mathcal{K}
is twisting.

•

(N,K)
is in addition nearly Robinson if and only if
\mathcal{K}
is also non-shearing.

Proof.

Choose an optical
1
-form
\kappa_{a}
with Robinson
3
-form
\rho_{abc}
. Then, using
(\mathrm{d}\rho)_{abcd}=\nabla_{[a}\rho_{bcd]}
, we compute the various components of (
4.31
) to find

\displaystyle\left(\mathrm{d}\rho-\alpha\wedge\rho\right)_{\alpha\beta\bar{\gamma}}{}^{0}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\gamma_{\alpha}=0\,,

\displaystyle\left(\mathrm{d}\rho-\alpha\wedge\rho\right)_{\alpha\beta 0}{}^{0}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{i}\tau_{\alpha\beta}+\zeta_{\alpha\beta}=0\,,

\displaystyle\left(\mathrm{d}\rho-\alpha\wedge\rho\right)_{\alpha\bar{\beta}0}{}^{0}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\sigma_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=0\,,

\displaystyle\left(\mathrm{d}\rho-\alpha\wedge\rho\right)_{\alpha\beta\gamma 0}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=0\,,

\displaystyle\left(\mathrm{d}\rho-\alpha\wedge\rho\right)_{\bar{\alpha}\beta\gamma 0}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle G^{\circ}_{\bar{\alpha}\beta\gamma}=0\,,

\displaystyle\left(\mathrm{d}\rho-\alpha\wedge\rho\right)_{\alpha\beta\gamma\bar{\delta}}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\tau_{\alpha\beta}=0\,,

\displaystyle\left(\mathrm{d}\rho-\alpha\wedge\rho\right)_{\alpha\bar{\beta}0}{}^{0}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\tau^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=0\,.

The remaining contractions are vacuous. The equivalence between (
1
) and (
2
) now follows.

The properties of
\mathcal{K}
and its leaf space
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
follow from
\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\tau}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{\tau}^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=\breve{\sigma}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}=0
. In particular, the only non-vanishing component of the twist is
\breve{\tau}^{\omega}
, which tells us that
(N,K)
is induced from the twist if and only if
\breve{\tau}^{\omega}\neq 0
. Finally, with reference to Proposition
4.16
, we see that the only obstruction to
(N,K)
being nearly Robinson is given by the
(2,0)
-part of the shear
\breve{\sigma}_{\alpha\beta}
.
∎

Remark 4.49
.

Proposition
4.48
reduces to Proposition
4.41
when
\mathcal{K}
is (maximally) twisting and non-shearing.

We now present a couple of examples illustrating notably the results of this section and that of Section
4.7
.

Example 4.50

(The Kerr–NUT–(A)dS metric)
.

In
[
16
]
, the authors present the
Kerr–NUT–(A)dS metric
in arbitrary dimensions, which partly generalises the Plebański–Demiański metric
[
79
]
, and admits a Euclidean analogue under a Wick rotation. In dimension
2m+2
, in coordinates
(r,x_{\alpha},t,\psi_{i})
, where
\alpha,i=1,\ldots m
, this Einstein metric takes the form

g=\frac{U}{X}\mathrm{d}r^{2}-\frac{X}{U}\left(\mathrm{d}t+\sum_{k=1}^{m}A^{(k)}\mathrm{d}\psi_{k}\right)^{2}\\
+\sum_{\alpha=1}^{m}\left(\frac{U_{\alpha}}{X_{\alpha}}\mathrm{d}x_{\alpha}^{2}+\frac{X_{\alpha}}{U_{\alpha}}\left(\mathrm{d}t+\sum_{k=1}^{m}A^{(k)}_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}\psi_{k}\right)^{2}\right)\,,

where

\displaystyle U

\displaystyle=\prod_{\beta=1}^{m}(r^{2}+x_{\beta}^{2})\,,\qquad X=\sum_{k=1}^{m+1}(-1)^{k}c_{k}r^{2k}+Mr\,,

\displaystyle A^{(k)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathclap{\nu_{1}<\ldots<\nu_{k}}}x_{\nu_{1}}^{2}\ldots x_{\nu_{k}}^{2}\,,

and, for
\alpha=1,\ldots,m
,

\displaystyle U_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=(r^{2}+x_{\alpha}^{2})\prod_{\alpha\neq\beta}(x_{\beta}^{2}-x_{\alpha}^{2})\,,

\displaystyle X_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{m+1}c_{k}x_{\alpha}^{2k}+L_{\alpha}r\,,

\displaystyle A^{(k)}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1}<\ldots<\beta_{k}\\
\beta_{i}\neq\alpha\end{subarray}}}x_{\beta_{1}}^{2}\ldots x_{\beta_{k}}^{2}-r^{2}\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1}<\ldots<\beta_{k-1}\\
\beta_{i}\neq\alpha\end{subarray}}}x_{\beta_{1}}^{2}\ldots x_{\beta_{k-1}}^{2}\,.

Here,
M
,
L_{\alpha}
,
\alpha=1,\ldots m
and
c_{\alpha}
are constants related to the mass, NUT parameters, the cosmological constant and rotation parameters of the black hole.

Define

\displaystyle\kappa

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{U}{2X}}\left(\mathrm{d}r+\frac{X}{U}\left(\mathrm{d}t+\sum_{k=1}^{m}A^{(k)}\mathrm{d}\psi_{k}\right)\right)\,,

\displaystyle\lambda

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{U}{2X}}\left(\mathrm{d}r-\frac{X}{U}\left(\mathrm{d}t+\sum_{k=1}^{m}A^{(k)}\mathrm{d}\psi_{k}\right)\right)\,,

\displaystyle\theta^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{U_{\alpha}}{2X_{\alpha}}}\left(\mathrm{d}x_{\alpha}+\mathrm{i}\frac{X_{\alpha}}{U_{\alpha}}\left(\mathrm{d}t+\sum_{k=1}^{m}A^{(k)}_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}\psi_{k}\right)\right)\,,\quad(\alpha=1,\ldots,m)\,.

The set of null
1
-forms
(\kappa,\theta^{\alpha})
, where
\kappa
is real and
\theta^{\alpha}
are complex, defines an almost Robinson structure
(N,K)
. We compute the intrinsic torsion of
(N,K)
, and find, suspending the summation convention,

\displaystyle\gamma_{i}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\alpha\beta}=\tau_{\alpha\beta}=\zeta_{\alpha\beta}=G_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=0\,,

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon}{2m}h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}+\sigma_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}+\tau_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{X}{2U}}\frac{r-\mathrm{i}x_{\alpha}}{r^{2}+x_{\alpha}^{2}}\delta_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\,,

\displaystyle E_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\sqrt{\frac{X_{\alpha}}{2U_{\alpha}}}\frac{r+\mathrm{i}x_{\alpha}}{r^{2}+x_{\alpha}^{2}}\delta_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\,,
