\displaystyle G_{\bar{\gamma}\alpha\beta}=-\mathrm{i}\sqrt{\frac{2X_{\beta}}{U_{\beta}}}\frac{1}{x_{\alpha}+x_{\beta}}\delta_{\alpha\bar{\gamma}}\,,

\displaystyle B_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=0\,.

We see at once that the conditions of Proposition
4.45
are met. In particular,
(N,K)
is involutive, i.e. any of the associated spinors satisfies (
4.29
), but none of the stronger conditions (
4.25
), (
4.28
) and (
4.26
). Thus, the intrinsic torsion of
(N,K)
is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,2}
, and does not degenerate further.
6
6

6

This can be seen by inspection of the above computation bearing in mind that here, the vanishing of
B_{\alpha\beta}
is not invariant under the structure group of
(N,K)
.
In particular, when
m>1
, the Robinson structure is not twist-induced.

In addition, the congruence
\mathcal{K}
tangent to
K
is geodesic, expanding, maximally twisting and shearing, when
m>1
— see also
[
85
]
— and non-shearing when
m=1
as is well-known.

Hence, by Propositions
4.23
and
4.45
, the Robinson structure
(N,K)
descends to a contact CR structure on the leaf space of
\mathcal{K}
.

Similarly, the set of null
1
-forms
(\lambda,\theta^{\alpha})
also defines an almost Robinson structure whose intrinsic torsion share the same properties as
(N,K)
. More generally, it is shown in
[
59
]
that this metric admits
2^{m+1}
almost null structures, which yield
2^{m-1}
Robinson structures associated to each of the optical structures
K
and
L
, all sharing the same properties.

These findings also apply to other related metrics such as the Myers–Perry metric
[
61
]
that may be viewed as a special case of the Kerr–NUT–(A)dS metric in the limit where the NUT parameters and cosmological constant tend to zero.

Example 4.51

(Taub–NUT–(A)dS and Fefferman–Einstein metrics)
.

It is shown in
[
106
]
that any conformal optical geometry
(\mathcal{M},\mathbf{c},K)
of dimension
2m+2
greater than four, whose associated congruence is geodesic, twisting and non-shearing, and whose Weyl tensor satisfies

(4.32)

\displaystyle W(k,v,k,v)

\displaystyle=0\,,

for any sections
k
of
K
,
v
of
K^{\perp}
,

admits a twist-induced almost Robinson structure
(N,K)
. Here, we recall that
\mathbf{c}
denotes an equivalence class of conformally related Lorentzian metrics. The description of the intrinsic torsion of
(N,K)
is then described by Proposition
4.41
. In particular, the leaf space
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
of
\mathcal{K}
is endowed with a partially integrable contact almost CR structure
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
whose associated subconformal structure coincides with that induced by
\accentset{n.e.}{\mathbf{c}}
. The involutivity of
(N,K)
(or equivalently, the vanishing of the Nijenhuis tensor
\underline{\mathsf{N}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}
of
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
) is equivalent to the Weyl tensor satisfying

\displaystyle W(k,u,v,w)

\displaystyle=0\,,

for any sections
k
of
K
,
u,v,w
of
N
.

Let us now assume further that the Weyl tensor satisfies

\displaystyle W(k,v,k,\cdot)

\displaystyle=0\,,

for any sections
k
of
K
,
v
of
K^{\perp}
.

Then locally any Einstein metric
\widehat{g}
in
\mathbf{c}
determines a contact form
\underline{\theta}^{0}
on
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
such that its corresponding almost pseudo-Hermitian structure is almost CR–Einstein. Such a structure is briefly discussed in Remark
4.15
— see references therein for more precise definitions. Suffices to say that locally
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{J},\underline{\theta}^{0})
is fibered over an almost Kähler–Einstein manifold
(\undertilde{\mathcal{M}},\undertilde{h},\undertilde{J})
of dimension
2m
, and the Levi form
\underline{h}
of
(\underline{H},\underline{J},\underline{\theta}^{0})
can be identified as the pullback of the almost Kähler–Einstein metric
\undertilde{h}
.

Further, one can choose a coordinate
\phi
on the fibers
\mathcal{M}\accentset{\varpi}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\mathcal{M}}
so that

\displaystyle\widehat{g}

\displaystyle=\sec^{2}\phi\,g\,,

for
-\frac{\pi}{2}<\phi<\frac{\pi}{2}
,

where

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle=4\,\varpi^{*}\underline{\theta}^{0}\left(\mathrm{d}\phi+\lambda_{0}\,\varpi^{*}\underline{\theta}^{0}\right)+\varpi^{*}\underline{h}\,,

with

\lambda_{0}=\frac{\underline{\Lambda}}{2m+2}+\left(\frac{\Lambda}{2m+1}-\frac{\underline{\Lambda}}{2m+2}\right)\left(\sum_{j=0}^{m}a_{j}\cos^{2j}\phi-2a_{m}\cos^{2m+2}\phi\right)\\
+\underline{c}\cos^{2m+1}\phi\sin\phi\,,

\displaystyle a_{0}=1\,,

\displaystyle a_{j}=\frac{2m-2j+4}{2m-2j+1}a_{j-1}\,,

\displaystyle j=1,\ldots,m\,,

and
\Lambda
,
\underline{\Lambda}
and
\underline{c}
are constant. Here, the Ricci scalars of
(\mathcal{M},\widehat{g},K)
and
(\undertilde{\mathcal{M}},\undertilde{h},\undertilde{J})
are proportional to
\Lambda
and
\underline{\Lambda}
respectively.

We can compute the intrinsic torsion of
(\mathcal{M},\widehat{g},N,K)
explicitely. We find

\displaystyle\gamma_{i}=\sigma_{ij}=\tau_{\alpha\beta}=\tau^{\circ}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\tau^{\omega}

\displaystyle=\sec^{2}\phi\,,

\displaystyle\epsilon

\displaystyle=2m\tan\phi\,,

\displaystyle E_{\alpha}=G_{\alpha}=G^{\circ}_{\bar{\alpha}\beta\gamma}=G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle=-2\mathrm{i}\sec^{4}\phi\,\underline{\mathsf{N}}_{\beta\gamma\alpha}\,,

\displaystyle B_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=0\,.

Therefore, the intrinsic torsion of
(N,K)
is a section of

•

(\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{0\times 1})_{[2(m-1)\mathrm{i}:-1]}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,3}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,1}
, and

•

\not{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{0,0}
when
(N,K)
is involutive.

In particular, any of the Robinson spinors associated to
(N,K)
satisfies (
4.23
), and if
(N,K)
is involutive, (
4.25
). We shall see in Section
5
that these bundles do not depend on the metric
\widehat{g}
but solely on the conformal structure
\mathbf{c}
. In fact, the intrinsic torsion of
(N,K)
with respect to the metric
g
is similar except that
\tau^{\omega}=1
,
\epsilon=0
and
G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=-2\mathrm{i}\underline{\mathsf{N}}_{\beta\gamma\alpha}
.

Furthermore we remark that for certain values of
\Lambda
,
\underline{\Lambda}
and
\underline{c}
, and when
(N,K)
is involutive, the metric
\widehat{g}
is locally isometric to the Taub–NUT–(A)dS metric of
[
8
,
6
,
4
]
generalising the four-dimensional one of
[
109
,
62
]
, or the Fefferman–Einstein metric given in
[
51
]
.

Finally, there is a secondary almost Robinson structure
(N^{*},L)
, dual to
(N,K)
, with non-shearing congruence of null geodesics
\mathcal{L}
. If the function
\lambda_{0}
is non-vanishing, then
\mathcal{L}
is twisting and
(N^{*},L)
is twist-induced. Otherwise,
\mathcal{L}
is non-twisting.

Details can be found in
[
106
]
.

Remark 4.52
.

One reason why non-shearing congruences of null geodesics in higher dimensions are not as common as in dimension four has to do with curvature. Let us review the various geometric interpretations of the algebraic condition on the Weyl tensor (
4.32
). These are as follows:

•

In dimension
four
, if
\mathcal{K}
is geodesic and non-shearing
regardless of whether
\mathcal{K}
is non-twisting or not
, then condition (
4.32
) holds — see e.g.
[
96
,
77
]
.

•

In dimensions
greater
than four, if
\mathcal{K}
is non-shearing
and non-twisting
, then condition (
4.32
) holds — in fact, the Weyl tensor satisfies an even stronger condition
[
70
]
.

•

In
odd
dimensions greater than four, if
\mathcal{K}
is twisting and condition (
4.32
) holds, then
\mathcal{K}
must also be shearing
[
70
]
.

•

In
even
dimensions greater than four, if
\mathcal{K}
is twisting and non-shearing, and condition (
4.32
) holds, then the twist induces an almost Robinson structure on
(\mathcal{M},g)

[
106
]
.

4.11.
Analogies between almost Robinson geometry and almost Hermitian geometry

Recall that an almost Hermitian manifold consists of a triple
(\mathcal{M},g,J)
, where
(\mathcal{M},g)
is a
(2m+2)
-dimensional smooth Riemannian manifold, and
J
is an almost complex structure compatible with
g
, i.e.
J\circ g=-g\circ J
. An equivalent definition of an almost Hermitian structure on
(\mathcal{M},g)
is as an almost null structure
N
of real index zero, i.e. the complexified tangent bundle splits as
{}^{\mathbf{C}}T\mathcal{M}=N\oplus\overline{N}

[
63
]
– since in Riemannian signature, an almost null structure always has real index zero, we may dispense of this attribute. The equivalence between the two definitions is established by identifying
N
and
\overline{N}
as the eigenbundles of
J
. Dually, one can express the almost Hermitian structure in terms of a non-vanishing section of
\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{m+1}\mathrm{Ann}(N)
, which can be normalised up to a phase against its complex conjugate. Locally, or globally if
(\mathcal{M},g)
is spin, this section is the ‘square’ of a pure spinor field (of real index 0). It annihilates
N
, while its charged conjugate annihilates
\overline{N}
. Their pairing yields the almost Hermitian
2
-form of
(\mathcal{M},g,J)
and powers thereof
[
48
]
.

As emphazised in
[
63
]
, the point of contact between almost Robinson geometry and almost Hermitian geometry is their underlying almost null structure, and the only distinguishing feature between them is its real index, which is itself determined by the metric signature.

The relation between almost Robinson structures and almost null structures was already investigated in the previous section, especially in Theorem
4.44
, using the results of
[
102
]
. One can play the same game by studying the geometry of an almost Hermitian manifold
(\mathcal{M},g,J)
in the light of its underlying almost null structure
(\mathcal{M},{}^{\mathbf{C}}g,N)
. To this end, we recall the Gray–Hervella classification of almost Hermitian manifolds given in
[
30
]
. Following the notation of that reference, the bundle
\mathcal{W}
of intrinsic torsions of
(\mathcal{M},g,J)
splits into irreducible
\mathbf{U}(m+1)
-invariant subbundles as

(4.33)

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{W}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{2}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{3}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{4}\,,

where at any point

(4.34)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{W}_{1}&\cong[\![\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(3,0)}(\mathbf{R}^{2m+2})^{*}]\!]\,,&\mathcal{W}_{2}&\cong[\![\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=8.74669pt,width=0.3pt,depth=2.18663pt\hbox to10.93333pt{\hfil}\vrule height=8.74669pt,width=0.3pt,depth=2.18663pt\hbox to10.93333pt{\hfil}\vrule height=8.74669pt,width=0.3pt,depth=2.18663pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=8.74669pt,width=0.3pt,depth=2.18663pt\hbox to10.93333pt{\hfil}\vrule height=8.74669pt,width=0.3pt,depth=2.18663pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}(\mathbf{R}^{2m+2})^{*}]\!]\,,\\
\mathcal{W}_{3}&\cong[\![\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(1,2)}_{\circ}(\mathbf{R}^{2m+2})^{*}]\!]\,,&\mathcal{W}_{4}&\cong[\![\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}^{(1,0)}(\mathbf{R}^{2m+2})^{*}]\!]\ \,.\end{aligned}

There are various ways to characterise the intrinsic torsion
\mathring{T}
of
(\mathcal{M},g,J)
. For instance,
(\mathcal{M},g,J)
is
almost Kähler
, i.e.
\mathring{T}
is a section of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
, if and only if the almost Hermitian
2
-form
\omega=g\circ J
is closed. It is Hermitian, i.e.
\mathring{T}
is a section of
\mathcal{W}_{3}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{4}
, if and only if the Nijenhuis tensor of the complex structure vanishes.
But only a subset of the Gray–Hervella classes will be relevant to the present discussion, namely those that reflect the geometric properties of the underlying almost null structure. For instance, one can characterise a Hermitian manifold in terms of a pure spinor field that is recurrent along the totally null distribution it defines
[
41
,
49
,
102
]
: this is equivalent to the eigenbundles of
J
being in involution — see Proposition
4.45
for the Robinson analogue. Proceeding as in the proof of Theorem
4.44
, one can easily prove the equivalence between the first and last columns of Table
6
, which summarizes the correspondences between the various classes of almost null structures, almost Robinson structures and almost Hermitian structures. The equivalence between the first and second columns follows directly from Theorem
4.44
. We leave the details for the reader.

{\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|}\hline\cr\text{Almost null structures}&\text{Almost Robinson structures}&\text{Almost Hermitian structures}\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\mbox{Eq. \eqref{eq:pure_spinor1}}&\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,1}&\mathcal{W}_{2}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{3}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{4}\\
\mbox{Eq. \eqref{eq:pure_spinor2}}&\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,2}&\mathcal{W}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{3}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{4}\\
\mbox{Eqs. \eqref{eq:pure_spinor1} and \eqref{eq:pure_spinor2}}&\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,2}&\mathcal{W}_{3}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{4}\\
\mbox{Eq. \eqref{eq:pure_spinor3} in dim$>6$}&\not{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{0,0}&\mathcal{W}_{4}\\
\mbox{Eq. \eqref{eq:pure_spinor3d6} in dim $6$}&\not{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{0,0}&\mathcal{W}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{4}\\
\mbox{Eq. \eqref{eq:pure_spinor0}}&({\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{0\times 1}})_{[2\mathrm{i}:1]}&\mathcal{W}_{3}\\
\mbox{Eq. \eqref{eq:pure_spinor3} and \eqref{eq:pure_spinor0}}&\{0\}&\{0\}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}}

Table 6.
Comparison of the intrinsic torsion of almost Robinson structures and the Gray–Hervella classification of almost Hermitian manifolds on the basis of the properties of their underlying null structure.

4.12.
Almost Robinson manifolds of Kundt type

Definition 4.53
.

An almost Robinson manifold
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
is said to be
of Kundt type
if
(\mathcal{M},g,K)
is a Kundt spacetime, i.e.
K
is tangent to a non-expanding, non-shearing, non-twisting congruence of null geodesics.

Equivalently, the intrinsic torsion of such manifolds is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{0}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{2}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\epsilon}=\breve{\tau}_{ij}=\breve{\sigma}_{ij}

\displaystyle=0\,.

For most of this section, however, we shall restrict ourselves to nearly Robinson manifolds of Kundt type, in which case the intrinsic torsion is a section of
\mathcal{G}^{0}=\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{0}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{2}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{3,0}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\epsilon}=\breve{\tau}_{ij}=\breve{\sigma}_{ij}=\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=0\,.

Note that this implies that
N
is parallel along
K
. From Section
4.7
,
(N,K)
induces an almost CR structure with totally degenerate Levi form on the leaf space
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H})
, and the screen bundle metric
h
on
H_{K}
descends to a bundle metric
\underline{h}
on
\underline{H}
. This tells us that
(\underline{H},\underline{h},\underline{J})
is a Hermitian vector bundle. In addition, since
K^{\perp}
is in involution, so is
\underline{H}
. Thus,
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{h})
admits a local Riemannian foliation. Putting these two facts together allows us to characterise nearly Robinson manifolds of Kundt type in the following terms.

Proposition 4.54
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be a
(2m+2)
-dimensional nearly Robinson manifold of Kundt type with congruence of null geodesics
\mathcal{K}
. Then the local leaf space
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{h},\underline{J})
of
\mathcal{K}
is foliated by a smooth one-parameter family of
2m
-dimensional almost Hermitian manifolds, each tangent to
\underline{H}
.

In the neighbourhood of every point of
\mathcal{M}
, we can apply Proposition
4.22
to express the metric
g
in terms of a Robinson coframe adapted to the Kundt geometry in terms of the leaf space
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
as follows. Locally, we shall refer to a Robinson coframe
(\kappa,\theta^{\alpha},\overline{\theta}{}^{\bar{\alpha}},\lambda)
as a
(complex) Kundt coframe
if

\displaystyle\kappa

\displaystyle:=2\varpi^{*}\underline{\theta}^{0}\,,

\displaystyle\theta^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\varpi^{*}\underline{\theta}^{\alpha}\,,

for some exact
1
-form
\underline{\theta}^{0}
annihilating
\underline{H}
, and coframe
(\underline{\theta}^{\alpha},\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}})
on
\underline{H}
unitary with respect to
\underline{h}
. As before,
\varpi
is the local projection from
\mathcal{M}
to
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
. The
1
-form
\lambda
must be vertical with respect to
\varpi
.

In addition, one can find local coordinates
u
,
v
on
\mathcal{M}:=\mathbf{R}\times\underline{\mathcal{M}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varpi}}{{\longrightarrow}}\underline{\mathcal{M}}
such that

\displaystyle\kappa

\displaystyle=2\varpi^{*}\underline{\theta}^{0}=\mathrm{d}u\,,

and

\displaystyle k

\displaystyle=g^{-1}(\kappa,\cdot)=\frac{\partial}{\partial v}\,,

i.e.
u
is a smooth function on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
parametrising the leaves of the almost Hermitian foliation, and
v
is an affine parametre along the geodesics of
\mathcal{K}
. This allows us to write

\lambda
in the form

(4.35)

\displaystyle\lambda

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}v+\lambda_{\alpha}\varpi^{*}\underline{\theta}^{\alpha}+\lambda_{\bar{\alpha}}\varpi^{*}\underline{\theta}^{\bar{\alpha}}+\lambda_{0}\varpi^{*}\underline{\theta}^{0}\,,

where
\lambda_{\alpha}
,
\lambda_{\bar{\alpha}}
and
\lambda_{0}
are smooth functions on
\mathcal{M}
.

Any two Kundt coframes
(\kappa,\theta^{\alpha},\overline{\theta}{}^{\bar{\alpha}},\lambda)
and
(\widehat{\kappa},\widehat{\theta}{}^{\alpha},\overline{\widehat{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}},\widehat{\lambda})
are related by the transformations (
4.5
) where
\phi^{\alpha}
and
\psi{}_{\beta}{}^{\alpha}
are now required to be constant along
K
, and
\varphi
constant along
K^{\perp}
. Such a transformation can be induced from a transformation of adapted coframes on
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{h})
, a change of parameter for the almost Hermitian foliation thereon, or a change of affine parameter along
\mathcal{K}
.

We shall streamline the notation by setting
(\kappa,\theta^{i},\lambda)=(\kappa,\theta^{\alpha},\overline{\theta}{}^{\bar{\alpha}},\lambda)
. In the following, we fix a
1
-form
\underline{\theta}^{0}
annihilating
\underline{H}
and a splitting
T\mathcal{M}=\mathrm{span}(\underline{e}_{0})\oplus\underline{H}
where
\underline{e}_{0}
is dual to
\underline{\theta}^{\alpha}(\underline{e}_{0})=0
. Note that this fixes the freedom in choosing
(\underline{\theta}{}^{\alpha},\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}})
, up to unitary transformations. We introduce a connection
\underline{\nabla}
on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
that preserves
\underline{h}_{ij}
,
\underline{\theta}^{0}
and
\underline{e}_{0}
with torsion tensor
\underline{A}_{ij}=\underline{A}_{(ij)}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\left(\underline{\nabla}_{0}\underline{\nabla}_{i}-\underline{\nabla}_{i}\underline{\nabla}_{0}\right)\underline{f}

\displaystyle=-\underline{A}_{i}{}^{j}\underline{\nabla}_{j}\underline{f}\,,

for any smooth function
\underline{f}
on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
.

Note this connection depends on the choice of
\underline{\theta}^{i}
up to orthogonal transformations, and thus on the choice of
\underline{e}_{0}
. Dropping the pullback maps, we can express the Levi-Civita connection
\nabla
of
g
in the following way:

(4.36)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \nabla\kappa&=2\,\kappa\odot E\,,\\
\nabla\theta^{i}&=\underline{\nabla}\underline{\theta}^{i}-2\,B^{i}{}_{j}\underline{\theta}^{j}\odot\kappa-C^{i}\kappa\otimes\kappa-\frac{1}{2}\underline{A}^{i}{}_{j}\underline{\theta}^{j}\otimes\kappa-2\,E^{i}\lambda\odot\kappa\,,\\
\nabla\lambda&=-2E\wedge\lambda-E_{0}\kappa\odot\lambda+\kappa\otimes C+\frac{1}{2}\underline{A}-B\,,\end{aligned}

where

\displaystyle\underline{A}

\displaystyle=\underline{A}_{ij}\underline{\theta}^{i}\otimes\underline{\theta}^{j}\,,

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=B_{ij}\underline{\theta}^{i}\otimes\underline{\theta}^{j}\,,

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle=E_{i}\underline{\theta}^{i}+E_{0}\underline{\theta}^{0}\,,

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle=C_{i}\underline{\theta}^{i}\,,

with
B_{ij}=B_{[ij]}
,
E_{i}
,
E_{0}
and
C_{i}
being functions on
\mathcal{M}
satisfying

\displaystyle B_{ij}

\displaystyle=-\underline{\nabla}_{[i}\lambda_{j]}+\lambda_{[i}\dot{\lambda}_{j]}\,,

\displaystyle C_{i}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\underline{\nabla}_{i}\lambda_{0}-\underline{\nabla}_{0}\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{i}\dot{\lambda}_{0}+\lambda_{0}\dot{\lambda}_{i}-\underline{A}_{ij}\lambda^{j}\right)\,,

\displaystyle E_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\dot{\lambda}_{i}\,,

\displaystyle E_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\dot{\lambda}_{0}\,.

This choice of notation is of course not fortuitous since we can then identify the components
E_{\alpha}
and
B_{\alpha\beta}
of the intrinsic torsion.

Lemma 4.55
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be a nearly Robinson manifold of Kundt type with congruence of null geodesics
\mathcal{K}
and leaf space
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{h},\underline{J})
. Then, for any Kundt coframe
(\kappa,\theta^{\alpha},\overline{\theta}{}^{\bar{\alpha}},\lambda)
, the
1
-form
\lambda
satisfies

\displaystyle E_{i}

\displaystyle=-(\nabla\lambda)_{i}{}^{0}=-(\mathrm{d}\lambda)_{i}{}^{0}\,,

and
\breve{E}_{i}
is an invariant of
(N,K)
.

If the intrinsic torsion is in addition a section of
(\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{0\times 1})_{[-2(m-1)\mathrm{i}:1]}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,2}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,3}
, then

\displaystyle B_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=(\nabla\lambda)_{\alpha\beta}=-(\mathrm{d}\lambda)_{\alpha\beta}\,.

In particular,
\breve{B}_{\alpha\beta}
is an invariant of
(N,K)
.

Proof.

The expressions for
E_{i}
and
B_{\alpha\beta}
follow from reading off the components of the Levi-Civita connections from equations (
4.36
). The statements on the invariance of the corresponding weighted quantities
\breve{E}_{i}
and
\breve{B}_{\alpha\beta}
can be checked from the transformations in the proof of Theorem
3.18
.
∎

Since both
\kappa_{a}
and
\rho_{abc}
are the pull-backs of some
1
-form and
3
-form from
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
to
\mathcal{M}
, we can relate the present classification of almost Robinson structures with the Gray–Hervella classification
[
30
]
of almost Hermitian manifolds. To this end, we simply note that for any splitting
(\ell^{a},\delta^{a}_{i},k^{a})
, using (
4.36
), we have

(4.37)

\displaystyle(\nabla\rho)_{ijk0}

\displaystyle=\underline{\nabla}_{i}\underline{\omega}_{jk}\,,

where
\underline{\omega}_{ij}=\underline{J}_{i}{}^{k}\underline{h}_{kj}
is the smooth family of Hermitian
2
-form associated to the almost Hermitian foliation on
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{h},\underline{J})
. One can readily check that this does not depend on the choice of coframe — this essentially follows from equations (
A.1
) and the fact that the optical invariants
\breve{\gamma}_{i}
,
\breve{\epsilon}
,
\breve{\sigma}_{ij}
and
\breve{\tau}_{ij}
all vanish. The Gray–Hervella classes can be easily obtained by comparing the LHS and the RHS of equation (
4.37
) with references to (
4.33
) and (
4.34
). We have collected the findings in Table
7
.

{\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr\text{Type of almost Hermitian structure}&\lx@intercol\hfil\text{Intrinsic torsion $\underline{\mathring{T}}$}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@intercol&\lx@intercol\hfil\text{Intrinsic torsion $\mathring{T}$}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@intercol\\
\text{on $\underline{\mathcal{M}}$}&\mathcal{W}_{1}&\mathcal{W}_{2}&\mathcal{W}_{3}&\mathcal{W}_{4}&\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,0}&\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,1}&\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,2}&\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,3}\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\text{almost Hermitian}&\checkmark&\checkmark&\checkmark&\checkmark&&&&\\
\mathcal{G}_{2}&&\checkmark&\checkmark&\checkmark&&\checkmark&&\\
\mathcal{G}_{1}&\checkmark&&\checkmark&\checkmark&&&\checkmark&\\
&\checkmark&\checkmark&&\checkmark&&&&\checkmark\\
\text{semi-K\"{a}hler}&\checkmark&\checkmark&\checkmark&&\checkmark&&&\\
\text{Hermitian}&&&\checkmark&\checkmark&&\checkmark&\checkmark&\\
\text{incl. locally conformally almost K\"{a}hler}&&\checkmark&&\checkmark&&\checkmark&&\checkmark\\
&&\checkmark&\checkmark&&\checkmark&\checkmark&&\\
&\checkmark&&&\checkmark&&&\checkmark&\checkmark\\
&\checkmark&&\checkmark&&\checkmark&&\checkmark&\\
\text{quasi-K\"{a}hler}&\checkmark&\checkmark&&&\checkmark&&&\checkmark\\
\text{nearly K\"{a}hler}&\checkmark&&&&\checkmark&&\checkmark&\checkmark\\
\text{almost K\"{a}hler}&&\checkmark&&&\checkmark&\checkmark&&\checkmark\\
\text{special Hermitian}&&&\checkmark&&\checkmark&\checkmark&\checkmark&\\
\text{incl. locally conformally K\"{a}hler}&&&&\checkmark&&\checkmark&\checkmark&\checkmark\\
\text{K\"{a}hler}&&&&&\checkmark&\checkmark&\checkmark&\checkmark\\
\hline\cr\end{array}}

Table 7.
Relation between the intrinsic torsions
\mathring{T}
and
\underline{\mathring{T}}
for nearly Robinson manifolds of Kundt type.

The following proposition gives a characterisation of an almost Robinson manifold of Kundt type in the case where the leaves of the Riemannian foliation on the leaf space are Kähler manifolds. We leave the proof to the reader.

Proposition 4.56
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be a nearly Robinson manifold of Kundt type with congruence of null geodesics
\mathcal{K}
and leaf space
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{h},\underline{J})
. The following statements are equivalent.

(1)

the intrinsic torsion is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,0}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,2}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,3}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\epsilon}=\breve{\tau}_{ij}=\breve{\sigma}_{ij}=\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{G}_{\alpha}=\breve{G}^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=\breve{G}^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=\breve{G}^{\circ}_{\bar{\alpha}\beta\gamma}=0\,.

(2)

any Robinson
3
-form
\rho_{abc}
is recurrent along
K^{\perp}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\kappa_{[a}\nabla_{b]}\rho_{cde}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{[a}\alpha_{b]}\rho_{cde}\,,

for some
1
-form
\alpha_{a}
;

(3)

N
is parallel along
K^{\perp}
.

(4)

Each leaf of the almost Hermitian foliation on
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{h},\underline{J})
is a Kähler manifold.

Besides the classes of nearly Robinson manifolds of Kundt type enumerated in Table
7
, other interesting degeneracy conditions on the intrinsic torsion are also possible. In fact, these can be partly characterised by the covariant derivative of the
1
-form
\lambda
using Lemma
4.55
.

Proposition 4.57
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be a nearly Robinson manifolds of Kundt type with congruence of null geodesics
\mathcal{K}
and leaf space
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{h},\underline{J})
. Let
[z:w]\in\mathbf{C}\mathbb{P}^{1}
such that
z\neq 0
. The following two statements are equivalent.

(1)

the intrinsic torsion of
(N,K)
is a section of
(\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{0\times 1})_{[z:w]}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\epsilon}=\breve{\tau}_{ij}=\breve{\sigma}_{ij}=\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}=0\,,

\displaystyle z\breve{E}_{\alpha}+w\,\breve{G}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=0\,.

(2)

for any choice of Kundt coframe, the components
\lambda_{\alpha}
of the vertical
1
-form
\lambda
given by (
4.35
) are determined by

(4.38)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\underline{\lambda}_{\alpha}^{(0)}-2\frac{w}{z}v\underline{\lambda}^{(1)}_{\alpha}\,,

for some smooth functions
\underline{\lambda}_{\alpha}^{(0)}
and
\underline{\lambda}_{\alpha}^{(1)}
on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
such that

(4.39)

\displaystyle\underline{\lambda}_{\alpha}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=G_{\alpha}=\underline{h}^{ij}(\underline{\nabla}\underline{\omega})_{ij\alpha}\,,

where
\underline{\omega}_{ij}=\underline{J}_{i}{}^{k}\underline{h}_{kj}
.

Finally, for any optical vector field
k
, with
\kappa=g(k,\cdot)
, the Weyl tensor satisfies

(4.40)

\displaystyle k^{a}W_{abc[d}\kappa_{e]}k^{c}

\displaystyle=0\,.

Proof.

This is a straightforward computation. By definition, the condition
zE_{\alpha}+w\,G_{\alpha}=0
can be rewritten as
z\dot{\lambda}_{\alpha}+2w\,G_{\alpha}=0
, which has solution given precisely by (
4.38
) and (
4.39
).

For the last part, it is shown in
[
82
]
that condition (
4.40
) on Weyl tensor is equivalent to
\lambda_{i}
being linear in
v
.
∎

Remark 4.58
.

Weaker conditions, where one takes
z
and
w
to be non-constant complex-valued smooth functions in Proposition
4.57
, are possible. In this case, it is no longer true that
\lambda_{i}
is linear in
v
.

The next proposition follows from the interpretation of the vanishing of the intrinsic torsion as the reduction of the holonomy of the Levi-Civita connection to
Q
, or equivalently, to the parallelism of the distribution
N
. The last item follows from Lemma
4.55
.

Proposition 4.59
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be an almost Robinson manifold with congruence of null curves
\mathcal{K}
. The following statements are equivalent.

(1)

the intrinsic torsion vanishes identically, i.e.

\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\epsilon}=\breve{\tau}_{ij}=\breve{\sigma}_{ij}=\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{E}_{i}=\breve{G}_{\alpha}=\breve{G}^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=\breve{G}^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=\breve{G}^{\circ}_{\bar{\alpha}\beta\gamma}=\breve{B}_{\alpha\beta}=0\,.

(2)

the holonomy of the Levi-Civita connection is reduced to
Q=(\mathbf{R}_{>0}\times\mathbf{U}(m))\ltimes(\mathbf{R}^{2m})^{*}
, the structure group of
(N,K)
.

(3)

any Robinson
3
-form
\rho_{bcd}
is recurrent, i.e.

\displaystyle\nabla_{a}\rho_{bcd}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{a}\rho_{bcd}\,,

for some
1
-form
\alpha_{a}
.

(4)

N
is parallel, i.e. for any
v\in\Gamma(N)
,
\nabla v\in\Gamma(N)
.

(5)

any Robinson spinor
\nu
is recurrent, i.e.

\displaystyle\left(\nabla_{a}\nu^{b\mathbf{A}}\right)\nu_{b}{}^{\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle=0\,,

i.e.

\displaystyle\left(\nabla_{a}\nu^{[\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\right)\nu^{\mathbf{B}^{\prime}]}

\displaystyle=0\,.

(6)

(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
is of Kundt type with leaf space
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{h},\underline{J})
where
\underline{h}
is a smooth one-parameter family of Kähler metrics, and for any choice of Kundt coframe, the components
\lambda_{i}
of the vertical
1
-form
\lambda
given by (
4.35
) are smooth functions
\underline{\lambda}_{i}^{(0)}
on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
, and, at any point,
\underline{\nabla}_{[i}\underline{\lambda}_{j]}^{(0)}
is an element of
\mathfrak{u}(m)

Remark 4.60
.

We note that if a Robinson
3
-form is recurrent, so is any optical
1
-form.

Remark 4.61
.

One can also weaken the assumptions of Proposition
4.59
, by supposing that on each leaf tangent to
\underline{H}
, the metric
\underline{h}_{ij}
is almost Kähler, i.e. the Hermitian
2
-form is closed, rather than Kähler. Then locally one may take
\underline{\lambda}_{i}^{(0)}
to be any potential
1
-form for the Hermitian
2
-form. Then
\underline{\nabla}_{[i}\underline{\lambda}_{j]}^{(0)}
is an element of
\mathfrak{u}(m)
. This implies that
B_{\alpha\beta}=0
, i.e. the
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{0,0}
-component of the intrinsic torsion vanishes.

Finally, the next two results, stated without proofs, are concerned with further holonomy reduction.

Proposition 4.62
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be an almost Robinson manifold with congruence of null curves
\mathcal{K}
. The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

the holonomy of the Levi-Civita connection is reduced to a subgroup of
\mathbf{U}(m)\ltimes(\mathbf{R}^{2m})^{*}
;

(2)

(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
admits a parallel Robinson
3
-form
\rho_{bcd}
, i.e.
\nabla_{a}\rho_{bcd}=0
;

(3)

(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
is of Kundt type with leaf space
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{h})
where
\underline{h}
is a smooth one-parameter family of Kähler metrics, and for any choice of Kundt coframe, the components
\lambda_{i}
and
\lambda_{0}
of the vertical
1
-form
\lambda
given by (
4.35
) are smooth functions
\underline{\lambda}_{i}^{(0)}
and
\underline{\lambda}_{0}^{(0)}
on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
, and, at any point,
\underline{\nabla}_{[i}\underline{\lambda}_{j]}^{(0)}
is an element of
\mathfrak{u}(m)
.

If any of these conditions holds,
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
admits a parallel optical vector field.

Proposition 4.63
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be an almost Robinson spin manifold with congruence of null curves
\mathcal{K}
. The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

the holonomy of the Levi-Civita connection is reduced to a subgroup of
\mathbf{SU}(m)\ltimes(\mathbf{R}^{2m})^{*}
;

(2)

(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
admits a parallel Robinson spinor
\nu
, i.e.
\nabla_{a}\nu=0
;

(3)

(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
admits a parallel optical vector field and a parallel Robinson
3
-form;

(4)

(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
is of Kundt type with leaf space
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{h})
where
\underline{h}
is a smooth one-parameter family of Ricci-flat Kähler metrics, and for any choice of Kundt coframe, the components
\lambda_{i}
and
\lambda_{0}
of the vertical
1
-form
\lambda
given by (
4.35
) are smooth functions
\underline{\lambda}_{i}^{(0)}
and
\underline{\lambda}_{0}^{(0)}
on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
, and, at any point,
\underline{\nabla}_{[i}\underline{\lambda}_{j]}^{(0)}
is an element of
\mathfrak{su}(m)
.

Example 4.64

(Metrics of supergravity)
.

Parallel Robinson structures are relevant to the study of solutions to the supergravity equations. These equations are rather restrictive. For instance, it is shown in
[
15
]
(see also
[
32
]
) that solutions known as

(1,0)
-vacua (up to local isometry) are six-dimensional Lie groups admitting a bi-invariant Lorentzian metric and an anti-self-dual
3
-form induced from the Lie bracket. In the same reference, it is proved that these must be either Minkowski space, a one-parameter family of so-called Freud–Rubin vacua on
AdS_{3}\times S^{3}
with equal radii, or a six-dimensional Nappi–Witten vacuum. The latter is locally isometric to a certain Cahan–Wallach space. In coordinates
(u,v,x^{1},x^{2},x^{3},x^{4})
, the metric is given by

(4.41)

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}u\left(\mathrm{d}v-\frac{1}{2}\undertilde{h}_{ij}x^{i}x^{j}\mathrm{d}u\right)+\undertilde{h}_{ij}\mathrm{d}x^{i}\mathrm{d}x^{j}\,,

where
\undertilde{h}_{ij}
is the standard Euclidean metric on
\mathbf{R}^{4}
. The anti-self-dual
3
-form induced from the Lie bracket takes the form

(4.42)

\displaystyle\rho

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{d}u\wedge\left(\mathrm{d}x^{1}\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{2}+\mathrm{d}x^{3}\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{4}\right)\,.

It can be checked that
\rho
defines a Robinson
3
-form with optical
1
-form
\kappa=\mathrm{d}u
.
Both
\kappa
and
\rho
are parallel with respect to the Levi-Civita connection.

One may start with a Kundt geometry
(\mathcal{M},g,K)
with congruence of null geodesics
\mathcal{K}
whose leaf space
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
of
\mathcal{K}
is a fiber bundle over a smooth
2m
-dimensional Riemannian manifold
(\undertilde{\mathcal{M}},\undertilde{h})
. Then any almost complex structure on
\undertilde{\mathcal{M}}
compatible with
\undertilde{h}
lifts to an almost Robinson structure on
(\mathcal{M},g)
compatible with
K
. Depending on its topology,
(\undertilde{\mathcal{M}},\undertilde{h})
may admit many almost Hermitian structures, each with a specific intrinsic torsion, or even families thereof. For instance, let us take
(\undertilde{\mathcal{M}},\undertilde{h})
to be Euclidean space. Then locally there are infinitely many Hermitian structures (see e.g.
[
21
]
) that can be lifted to an almost Robinson structure
(N,K)
on
(\mathcal{M},g)
.

A less trivial example follows.

Example 4.65
.

Let
(\undertilde{\mathcal{M}},\undertilde{h})
be the Iwasawa manifold, that is, the quotient of the three-dimensional complex Heisenberg group by a discrete subgroup. In
[
2
]
, the authors construct almost Hermitian structures in the following Gray–Hervella classes

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{3}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{4}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{3}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{4}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{2}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{4}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{2}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{3}\,.

Of these, the set of all invariant Hermitian structures on
(\undertilde{\mathcal{M}},\undertilde{h})
is known to consist of the union of a point (its bi-invariant Hermitian structure) and a 2-sphere
[
1
,
45
]
. These are in fact special Hermitian, i.e. their intrinsic torsion is of class
\mathcal{W}_{3}
. For topological reasons,
\undertilde{\mathcal{M}}
cannot admit any Kähler structure. It is also conjectured that
\undertilde{\mathcal{M}}
cannot admit almost Hermitian structures in the classes
\mathcal{W}_{1}
and
\mathcal{W}_{4}
.

The Kundt geometry
(\mathcal{M},g,K)
associated to
(\undertilde{\mathcal{M}},\undertilde{h})
admits almost Robinson structures corresponding to the almost Hermitian structures on
(\undertilde{\mathcal{M}},\undertilde{h})
, and their classes of intrinsic torsion can be read off from Table
7
.

As illustrated in the following example, not every almost Robinson structure on a Kundt spacetime is a nearly Robinson structure.

Example 4.66
.

Take
\mathcal{M}=\mathbf{R}\times\mathbf{R}\times\mathbf{R}^{2m}\cong\mathbf{R}\times\mathbf{R}\times\mathbf{C}^{m}=(u,v,z^{\alpha},\overline{z}{}^{\bar{\alpha}})
, and let
g
be the metric on
\mathcal{M}
given by

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{d}u\left(\mathrm{d}v+\lambda_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}z^{\alpha}+\lambda_{\bar{\alpha}}\mathrm{d}\overline{z}^{\bar{\alpha}}+\lambda_{0}\mathrm{d}u\right)+2\,\undertilde{h}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\mathrm{d}z^{\alpha}\mathrm{d}\overline{z}^{\bar{\beta}}\,,

where
\lambda_{\alpha}
,
\lambda_{\bar{\alpha}}
and
\lambda_{0}
are arbitrary smooth functions on
\mathcal{M}
, and
\undertilde{h}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
is the standard Hermitian form on
\mathbf{C}^{m}
. Let
k=\frac{\partial}{\partial v}
and set
K:=\mathrm{span}(k)
. Then
(\mathcal{M},g,K)
is a Kundt spacetime, and any almost Robinson structure
(N,K)
on
\mathcal{M}
compatible with
K
is annihilated by the set of
1
-forms

\displaystyle\kappa

\displaystyle=g(k,\cdot)=\mathrm{d}u\,,

\displaystyle\theta^{\alpha}=\mathrm{d}z^{\alpha}+\phi^{\alpha}{}_{\bar{\beta}}\mathrm{d}\overline{z}^{\bar{\beta}}\,,

for some complex-valued functions
\phi^{\alpha\beta}
on
\mathcal{M}
with
\phi^{\alpha\beta}=\phi^{[\alpha\beta]}
– the functions
\phi^{\alpha\beta}
are essentially the components of a section of the bundle
\mathrm{Gr}_{m+1}^{+}(\mathcal{M},g,K)
of (self-dual) almost Robinson structures compatible with
K
— see Remark
3.14
. Note that
(\theta^{\alpha})
does
not
constitute a unitary coframe for
\undertilde{h}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
in general. Choosing the
\phi^{\alpha\beta}
such that
\mathsterling_{k}\phi^{\alpha\beta}\neq 0
anywhere yields a coframe that does not descend to the leaf space
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
of
\mathcal{K}
. Since the manifold is Kundt, it is clear that the remaining obstruction to
(N,K)
being nearly Robinson is the
\mathcal{G}_{-1}^{3,0}
-component
\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}
of the intrinsic torsion, which, here, can be identified with
\mathsterling_{k}\phi_{\alpha\beta}
. Take for instance,
\phi^{\alpha\beta}=f(v)\underline{\phi}^{\alpha\beta}
for some smooth function
f
of
v
and smooth functions
\underline{\phi}^{\alpha\beta}
on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
. For definiteness, let us assume
m=2
. The bundle of all almost Hermitian structures on
\mathbf{R}^{4}
has fibers isomorphic to
\mathbf{C}\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Any unitary frame
(\theta^{1},\theta^{2})
for
\underline{h}
takes the form

\displaystyle\theta^{1}

\displaystyle=\left(a\bar{a}+b\bar{b}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(a\,\mathrm{d}z^{1}+b\,\mathrm{d}\overline{z}^{2}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\theta^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(a\bar{a}+b\bar{b}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(a\,\mathrm{d}z^{2}-b\,\mathrm{d}\overline{z}^{1}\right)\,.

for some smooth complex-valued functions
a
and
b
on
\mathcal{M}
with
a,b
not both vanishing. Note that this expression is invariant under non-zero rescaling of
(a,b)
, so at any point,
[a:b]
defines an element of
\mathbf{C}\mathbb{P}^{1}
as expected. Take
a=1
and
b
any smooth complex-valued function depending on
v
, i.e.
\mathsterling_{k}b\neq 0
. Then
(N,K)
is an almost Robinson structure on
(\mathcal{M},g,K)
that does not descend to
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{h})
, i.e. it is not nearly Robinson.

4.13.
Almost Robinson manifolds of Robinson–Trautman type

In complete analogy with Definition
4.53
, we make the following definition.

Definition 4.67
.

An almost Robinson manifold
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
is said to be
of Robinson–Trautman type
if
(\mathcal{M},g,K)
is a Robinson–Trautman spacetime, i.e. the congruence of curves tangent to
K
is geodesic, expanding, non-shearing and non-twisting.

The intrinsic torsion of such a manifold is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{2}
, with non-degenerate
\mathcal{G}_{-1}^{0}
-component i.e.

\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\sigma}_{ij}=\breve{\tau}_{ij}=0\,,

\displaystyle\breve{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\neq 0\,.

Again, it is natural to consider nearly Robinson manifolds of Robinson–Trautman type. These manifolds enjoy properties similar to those of their Kundt counterparts. In particular, the leaf space
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
of the congruence
\mathcal{K}
tangent to
K
is foliated by
2m
-dimensional almost Hermitian manifolds. The intrinsic torsion of
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
can also be related to the Gray–Hervella class of the almost Hermitian foliation as in Table
7

except
in cases where
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,0}
is involved — this would contradict the fact that
\mathcal{K}
is expanding. This means that the only Gray–Hervella classes allowed would be those containing
\mathcal{W}_{4}
.

However, since a Robinson–Trautmann spacetime is conformal to a Kundt spacetime
[
84
,
25
]
, one may still consider the full Gray–Hervella classification applied to the almost Hermitian foliation on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
. For instance, a nearly Robinson manifold of Robinson–Trautman type with intrinsic torsion in
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,3}
will arise from an almost Hermitian foliation on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
of either class
\mathcal{W}_{2}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{4}
or class
\mathcal{W}_{2}
.

Example 4.68

(The Tangherlini–Schwarzschild metric)
.

The smooth manifold
\mathcal{M}=\mathbf{R}\times\mathbf{R}_{>0}\times S^{2m}
admits the Tangherlini–Schwarzschild metric – see e.g.
[
80
]
. Then
(\mathcal{M},g,K)
is a Ricci-flat Robinson–Trautman spacetime which does not admit any global Robinson structure except in the case
m=1
. This follows from the fact that the
2m
-sphere admits a Hermitian structure if and only if
m=1
. However, since
S^{2m}
is conformally flat, it locally admits infinitely many Hermitian structures – these correspond precisely to holomorphic sections of the twistor bundles over
S^{2m}
, a Riemannian articulation of the Kerr theorem – see e.g.
[
21
]
.

In the above example, there is no distinguished (almost) Robinson structure on the Robinson–Trautman manifold. However, as the following example due to
[
69
]
shows, the Einstein–Maxwell equations may single out an almost Robinson structure on a Robinson–Trautman spacetime.

Example 4.69
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,K)
be a Robinson–Trautman optical geometry of dimension
2m+2
. Suppose
g
satisfies the Einstein–Maxwell equations with an electromagnetic field
F_{ab}=F_{[ab]}
, that is,
F_{ab}
is closed and co-closed, and the Einstein field equations take the form

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ric}_{ab}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\Lambda g_{ab}+8\pi T_{ab}+\frac{m-1}{2m}g_{ab}T_{cd}T^{cd}\,,

where
\Lambda
is the cosmological constant, and the energy-momentum tensor is given by

\displaystyle T_{ab}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\left(F_{ac}F_{b}{}^{c}-\frac{1}{4}g_{ab}F_{cd}F^{cd}\right)\,.

We assume further that
F_{ab}
satisfying
k^{a}F_{a}{}^{[b}k^{c]}=0
for any section
k
of
K
. For definiteness, assume
m>2
. Then
[
69
]
, there exist coordinates
(r,u,x^{i})
such that the metric takes the form

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle=-2\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}r-2H(r)(\mathrm{d}u)^{2}+r^{2}\undertilde{h}_{ij}(x)\mathrm{d}x^{i}\mathrm{d}x^{j}\,,

where
\undertilde{h}_{ij}
is a metric on each slice of constant
(r,u)
,

\displaystyle 2H(r)

\displaystyle=K-\frac{2\Lambda}{2m(2m+1)}r^{2}-\frac{\mu}{r^{2m-1}}+\frac{2Q^{2}}{2m(2m-1)}\frac{1}{r^{2(2m-1)}}-\frac{\|\undertilde{F}\|^{2}}{2m(2m-3)}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,,

and the electromagnetic field is given by

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle=\frac{Q}{r^{2m}}\mathrm{d}r\wedge\mathrm{d}u+\frac{1}{2}\undertilde{F}_{ij}(x)\mathrm{d}x^{i}\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{j}\,.

Here,
K\in\{-1,0,1\}
,
\mu
,
Q
and
\|\undertilde{F}\|^{2}=\undertilde{F}_{ij}\undertilde{F}^{ij}
are constants, and
k=\frac{\partial}{\partial r}
is a null vector field tangent to
K
whose congruence is also geodetsic, non-twisting and non-shearing. The vector field
\ell=\frac{\partial}{\partial u}-H(r)\frac{\partial}{\partial r}
defines a optical structure
L
dual to
K
. Set
\kappa=g(k,\cdot)=\mathrm{d}u
and
\lambda=g(\ell,\cdot)=-\mathrm{d}r-H(r)\mathrm{d}u
. Assuming
\|F\|^{2}\neq 0
, the electromagnetic
2
-form
F_{ab}
determines two almost Robinson structures
(N_{K},K)
and
(N_{L},L)
: their associated
3
-forms are proportional to
\kappa\wedge F
and
\lambda\wedge F
respectively. By virtue of the Maxwell equations, it can be shown
[
69
]
that the metric
\undertilde{h}_{ij}
is almost Kähler–Einstein. It follows from Table
7
that the intrinsic torsion of each of these almost Robinson structures must be a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,3}
.

The six-dimensional case is similar, and further generalisations of these results can be found in
[
46
]
.

As for Kundt spacetimes, one can associate to any smooth
2m
-dimensional Riemannian manifold
(\undertilde{\mathcal{M}},\undertilde{h})
a Robinson–Trautman geometry
(\mathcal{M},g,K)
with congruence of null geodesics
\mathcal{K}
such that the leaf space
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
of
\mathcal{K}
is the trivial line bundle
\mathbf{R}\times\undertilde{\mathcal{M}}
. Any almost complex structure on
\undertilde{\mathcal{M}}
compatible with
\undertilde{h}
lifts to an almost Robinson structure on
(\mathcal{M},g)
compatible with
K
.

4.14.
Compatible linear connections

We end Section
4
with a brief consideration of linear connections compatible with a given almost Robinson structure.

Proposition 4.70
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be an almost Robinson manifold. Fix a splitting
(\ell^{a},\delta^{a}_{i},k^{a})
. Define a linear connection
\nabla^{\prime}
with

(4.43)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\prime}_{a}\xi_{b}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{a}\xi_{b}-Q_{ab}{}^{c}\xi_{c}\,,

for any
1
-form
\xi_{a}
,

where
Q_{abc}
is a tensor such that

\displaystyle Q^{000}=Q_{i}{}^{00}

\displaystyle=Q_{0}{}^{00}=Q^{0}{}_{0}{}^{0}=0\,,

\displaystyle Q^{0}{}_{j}{}^{0}=-Q^{00}{}_{j}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{j}\,,

\displaystyle Q_{ij}{}^{0}=-Q_{i}{}^{0}{}_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2m}\epsilon\,h_{ij}+\tau_{ij}+\sigma_{ij}\,,

\displaystyle Q^{0}{}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2m}\epsilon\,h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}-\tau_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\,,

\displaystyle Q_{i(jk)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}(m-1)}G_{i}h_{jk}\,,

\displaystyle Q_{0j}{}^{0}=Q_{j0}{}^{0}

\displaystyle=-Q_{0}{}^{0}{}_{j}=-Q^{0}{}_{0k}=E_{j}\,,

\displaystyle Q_{0(jk)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2m}f_{0}h_{jk}\,,

and

\displaystyle Q^{0}{}_{\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle=-\tau_{\beta\gamma}+\frac{1}{2\mathrm{i}}\zeta_{\beta\gamma}\,,

\displaystyle Q_{\alpha\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\mathrm{i}}G_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\,,

\displaystyle Q_{\bar{\alpha}\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\mathrm{i}}G_{\bar{\alpha}\beta\gamma}\,,

\displaystyle Q_{\alpha\beta\bar{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{\mathrm{i}(m-1)}G_{(\alpha}h_{\beta)\bar{\gamma}}\,,

\displaystyle Q_{\alpha\bar{\gamma}\beta}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}(m-1)}G_{\alpha}h_{\beta\bar{\gamma}}\,,

\displaystyle Q_{0\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\mathrm{i}}B_{\alpha\beta}\,.

Then
\nabla^{\prime}
is a connection compatible with
K
and
[h]
, i.e.

\displaystyle\nabla^{\prime}_{u}\kappa(v)

\displaystyle=0\,,

for any
u\in\Gamma(T\mathcal{M})
,
v\in\Gamma(K^{\perp})
,

\displaystyle\nabla^{\prime}_{u}g(v,w)

\displaystyle=0\,,

for any
u\in\Gamma(T\mathcal{M})
,
v,w\in\Gamma(K^{\perp})
,

with torsion tensor satisfying

\displaystyle T^{0}{}_{j}{}^{0}

\displaystyle=-\gamma_{j}\,,

\displaystyle T_{ij}{}^{0}

\displaystyle=-2\,\tau_{ij}\,,

\displaystyle T^{0}{}_{j}{}_{k}

\displaystyle=-\sigma_{jk}\,,

\displaystyle T^{0}{}_{0}{}^{0}=T^{0}{}_{0k}

\displaystyle=T_{0j}{}^{0}=0\,,

and

\displaystyle T^{0}{}_{[\beta\gamma]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\mathrm{i}}\zeta_{\beta\gamma}-\tau_{\beta\gamma}\,,

\displaystyle T^{0}{}_{(\beta\gamma)}

\displaystyle=-\sigma_{\beta\gamma}\,,

\displaystyle T_{[\alpha\beta\gamma]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\mathrm{i}}G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\,,

\displaystyle T_{\alpha(\beta\gamma)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\mathrm{i}}G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{(\beta\gamma)\alpha}\,,

\displaystyle T_{\bar{\alpha}[\beta\gamma]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\mathrm{i}}G^{\circ}_{\bar{\alpha}\beta\gamma}\,,

\displaystyle T_{\alpha\beta\bar{\gamma}}=T_{\bar{\alpha}(\beta\gamma)}

\displaystyle=T_{0[\beta\gamma]}=0\,.

Proof.

This is a straightforward computation using (
4.43
) and the fact that the torsion tensor is given by
T_{abc}=-2\,Q_{[ab]c}
.
∎

Remark 4.71
.

The linear connection defined in the proposition above depends in general on the choice of splitting. Note however that even with fixed
k
and
\lambda
,
\nabla^{\prime}
as defined in the proof of the proposition is not unique, the undefined components of
Q_{abc}
in the proposition above being entirely arbitrary and not affecting the property of the torsion.

We may set all the remaining components of
Q_{[ab]c}
to zero, and obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 4.72
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
be an almost Robinson manifold. Suppose that the intrinsic torsion of
(N,K)
is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{2}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{3}
. Then
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
admits a torsion-free connection that preserves
(N,K)
and (the conformal class of) the screen bundle metric.

5.
Conformal almost Robinson structures

As for optical geometry and almost Hermitian geometry, the notion of almost Robinson structure, or indeed almost null structure, finds a very natural setting in conformal geometry. We shall follow the conventions set up in
[
7
,
25
]
, and denote a conformal structure on a smooth manifold
\mathcal{M}
by
\mathbf{c}
. For each
w\in\mathbf{R}
, the bundle of conformal densities of weight
w
is denoted by
\mathcal{E}[w]
. In particular, a choice of ray subundle
\mathcal{E}_{+}[1]
of
\mathcal{E}[1]
is referred to as the bundle of conformal scales. The conformal structure can be encoded by means of the
conformal metric

\bm{g}_{ab}
, that is a non-degenerate global section of
\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\odot$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\odot$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\odot$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\odot$}}^{2}T^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes\mathcal{E}[2]
. For each
g
in
\mathbf{c}
, we extend the Levi-Civita connection
\nabla
of
g
to a linear connection on
\mathcal{E}[w]
for each
w\in\mathbf{R}
. The exterior covariant derivative will be denoted by
\mathrm{d}^{\nabla}
. Further details can be found in the aforementioned references.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},\mathbf{c})
be an oriented and time-oriented Lorentzian conformal manifold of dimension
2m+2
. An
almost Robinson structure
on
(\mathcal{M},\mathbf{c})
consists of a pair
(N,K)
where
N
is a complex distribution of rank
m+1
totally null with respect to
g
, and
K
a real line distribution such that
{}^{\mathbf{C}}K=N\cap\overline{N}
. We shall call
(N,K)
a
nearly Robinson structure
when
[K,N]\subset N
, and a
Robinson structure
when
[N,N]\subset N
.

We shall accordingly refer to the quadruple
(\mathcal{M},\mathbf{c},N,K)
as an
almost (conformal) Robinson manifold
, as a
nearly (conformal) Robinson manifold
or as a
(conformal) Robinson manifold
.

Remark 5.2
.

As in the metric case, one can describe an almost conformal Robinson manifold as an almost null structure (of real index one).

The conformal metric
\bm{g}_{ab}
induces a conformal bundle metric
\bm{h}_{ij}
on
H_{K}
. Proposition
4.4
can immediately be translated into the conformal setting as follows:

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},\mathbf{c})
be an oriented and time-oriented Lorentzian manifold of dimension
2m+2
. The following are equivalent:

(1)

(\mathcal{M},\mathbf{c})
is endowed with an almost Robinson structure
(N,K)
;

(2)

(\mathcal{M},\mathbf{c})
admits a totally null complex
(m+1)
-form of conformal weight
m+2
;

(3)

(\mathcal{M},\mathbf{c})
is endowed with an optical structure
K
whose screen bundle
H_{K}=K^{\perp}/K
is equipped with a bundle complex structure compatible with the induced conformal structure;

(4)

(\mathcal{M},\mathbf{c})
admits a
1
-form
\bm{\kappa}_{a}
of conformal weight
2
, and a
3
-form
\bm{\rho}_{abc}
of conformal weight
4
such that

\displaystyle\bm{\rho}_{ab}\,{}^{e}\bm{\rho}_{cde}=-4\bm{\kappa}_{[a}\bm{g}_{b][c}\bm{\kappa}_{d]}\,;

(5)

(\mathcal{M},\mathbf{c})
admits a pure spinor of real index one.

We shall follow the terminology already introduced in Section
4
: thus the
1
-form
\bm{\kappa}_{a}
and
3
-form
\bm{\rho}_{abc}
given in Proposition
5.3
will be referred to as
optical
1
-form
and
Robinson
3
-form
respectively, and so on.

With reference to the proposition above, the bundle complex structure
J_{i}{}^{j}
yields a bundle Hermitian structure
\bm{\omega}_{ij}=J_{i}{}^{k}\bm{h}_{kj}
of conformal weight
2
. For a given optical
1
-form
\bm{\kappa}_{a}
we obtain a Robinson
3
-form
\bm{\rho}_{abc}=3\bm{\kappa}_{[a}\bm{\omega}_{bc]}
of conformal weight
4
, where
\bm{\omega}_{ab}
is such that
k^{c}\bm{\omega}_{c[a}\bm{\kappa}_{b]}=0
,
\bm{\omega}_{ij}=\bm{\omega}_{ab}\delta^{a}_{i}\delta_{j}^{b}
. The complex
(m+2)
-form
\bm{\nu}_{a_{0}a_{1}\ldots a_{m}}
is required to have conformal
m+1
since

\displaystyle\bm{\nu}_{aa_{1}\ldots a_{m}}\overline{\bm{\nu}}_{b}{}^{a_{1}\ldots a_{m}}\,\propto\,\bm{\kappa}_{a}\bm{\kappa}_{b}\,.

The relation with pure spinor fields is analogous to the Lorentzian case. Here, neither a Robinson spinor nor its charge conjugate are conformally weighted, but the van der Waerden symbols carry some conformal weight. This means that for each
k=0,\ldots,m+1
, the spinor bilinear form with values in (complex)
k
-forms has conformal weight
k+1
. We omit the details, which will play no rôle in the subsequent discussion.

5.1.
Conformal invariants of an almost Robinson structure

An optical geometry with a congruence of null geodesics has two conformal invariants, the shear and the twist, which we may view as fields of conformal weight two
[
90
,
25
]
. To determine the conformal invariants of an almost Robinson manifold
(\mathcal{M},\mathbf{c},N,K)
, we examine how the covariant derivative of the Robinson
3
-form changes under a change of metrics
\widehat{g}=\mathrm{e}^{2\varphi}g
for some smooth function
\varphi
as given below:

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{a}\bm{\kappa}_{b}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{a}\bm{\kappa}_{b}+2\Upsilon_{[a}\bm{\kappa}_{b]}+\bm{g}_{ab}\Upsilon^{c}\bm{\kappa}_{c}\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{a}\bm{\rho}_{bcd}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{a}\bm{\rho}_{bcd}+3\Upsilon_{a}\bm{\rho}_{bcd}-3\Upsilon_{[b}\bm{\rho}_{cd]a}+3\,\bm{g}_{a[b}\bm{\rho}_{cd]e}\Upsilon^{e}\,,

where
\Upsilon=\mathrm{d}\varphi
. Projecting these tensors into their components with splitting operators, we find that

\displaystyle\widehat{\gamma}_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{2\varphi}\gamma_{i}\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}_{ij}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{2\varphi}\tau_{ij}\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{\sigma}_{ij}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{2\varphi}\sigma_{ij}\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\epsilon+2m\Upsilon^{c}k_{c}\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{\zeta}_{\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{4\varphi}\zeta_{\beta\gamma}\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{E}_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{2\varphi}\left(E_{i}-\Upsilon_{i}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{G}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{2\varphi}\left(G_{\gamma}-2(m-1)\mathrm{i}\Upsilon_{\gamma}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{G}^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{4\varphi}G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{G}^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{4\varphi}G^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{G}^{\circ}_{\bar{\alpha}\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{4\varphi}G^{\circ}_{\bar{\alpha}\beta\gamma}\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{B}_{\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{4\varphi}B_{\beta\gamma}\,.

Note that

\displaystyle 2(m-1)\mathrm{i}\widehat{E}_{\gamma}-\widehat{G}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{2\varphi}\left(2(m-1)\mathrm{i}E_{\gamma}-G_{\gamma}\right)\,.

We also find that for any
[z:w]\in\mathbf{C}\mathbb{P}^{1}
,

\displaystyle z\widehat{\tau}_{\alpha\beta}+w\widehat{\zeta}_{\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{4\varphi}\left(z\tau_{\alpha\beta}+w\zeta_{\beta\gamma}\right)\,.

From these computations, we immediately conclude:

Theorem 5.4
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},\mathbf{c},N,K)
be an almost conformal Robinson manifold. Let
g
be a metric in
\mathbf{c}
so that
(\mathcal{M},g,K)
is an optical geometry with bundle of intrinsic torsions
\mathcal{G}
. Any conformally invariant subbundle of
\mathcal{G}
must be an intersection of the following:

\displaystyle\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-2}^{0,0}\,,

\displaystyle\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,0}\,,

\displaystyle\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,1}\,,

\displaystyle\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1,2}\,,

\displaystyle\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{2,0}\,,

\displaystyle\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{2,1}\,,

\displaystyle\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{3,0}\,,

\displaystyle\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,1}\,,

\displaystyle\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,2}\,,

\displaystyle\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,3}\,,

\displaystyle(\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{0\times 1})_{[2(m-1)\mathrm{i}:-1]}\,,

\displaystyle\not{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{0,0}\,,

\displaystyle(\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1\times 2})_{[x:y]}\,,

\displaystyle\mbox{\scriptsize{$[x:y]\in\mathbf{R}\mathbb{P}^{1}$}}\,,

\displaystyle(\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1\times 3})_{[z:w]}\,,

\displaystyle\mbox{\scriptsize{$[z:w]\in\mathbf{C}\mathbb{P}^{1}$}}\,.

As for conformal optical geometries, there is a subclass
\accentset{n.e.}{\mathbf{c}}
of metrics in
\mathbf{c}
with the property that whenever
g
is in
\accentset{n.e.}{\mathbf{c}}
, the congruence
\mathcal{K}
is non-expanding, i.e. for any
k\in\Gamma(K)
with
\kappa=g(k,\cdot)
,
\kappa\,\mathrm{div}k-\nabla_{k}\kappa=0
.

We also know from
[
25
]
that there exists a family of optical vector fields
k
such that
\mathsterling_{k}\bm{\kappa}=0
where
\kappa=\bm{g}(k,\cdot)
. If the corresponding Robinson
3
-form
\bm{\rho}_{abc}
is preserved along
K
, then
\mathsterling_{k}\bm{\rho}_{abc}=0
, where the Lie derivative is given by

\displaystyle\mathsterling_{k}\bm{\rho}_{abc}

\displaystyle=k^{d}\nabla_{d}\bm{\rho}_{abc}+3\bm{\rho}_{d[ab}\nabla_{c]}k^{d}-\frac{4}{n+2}\bm{\rho}_{abc}\nabla_{d}k^{d}\,.

Here
\nabla
is the Levi-Civita connection of any metric in
\mathbf{c}
. The details are left to the reader.

5.2.
Conformally parallel Robinson structures

In
[
25
]
, we saw that under certain conditions, one can find metrics in
\mathbf{c}
for which the optical structure is parallel. We extend this result to the Robinson setting.

Proposition 5.5
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},\mathbf{c},N,K)
be an almost Robinson manifold with congruence of null curves
\mathcal{K}
. Suppose that the intrinsic torsion of
(N,K)
for some (and thus any) metric
g
in
\mathbf{c}
is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{0,0}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\tau}_{ij}=\breve{\sigma}_{ij}=\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{G}^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=\breve{G}^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=\breve{G}^{\circ}_{\bar{\alpha}\beta\gamma}=\breve{B}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(5.1)

\displaystyle 2(m-1)\mathrm{i}\breve{E}_{\gamma}-\breve{G}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=0\,.

Equivalently, any Robinson spinor satisfies (
4.25
) and any optical
1
-form
\bm{\kappa}
satisfies
\bm{\kappa}\wedge\mathrm{d}^{\nabla}\bm{\kappa}=0
. Suppose further that the Weyl tensor
W_{abcd}
satisfies

\displaystyle k^{a}W_{ab[cd}\bm{\kappa}_{e]}

\displaystyle=0\,.

Then locally, there is a subclass
\accentset{par.}{\mathbf{c}}
of metrics in
\mathbf{c}
with the property that whenever
g
is in
\accentset{par.}{\mathbf{c}}
, the almost Robinson structure is parallel, i.e. any Robinson spinor, and thus any optical
1
-form and Robinson
3
-form, are recurrent. In particular, the holonomy of the Levi-Civita connection of any metric in
\accentset{par.}{\mathbf{c}}
is contained in
Q=(\mathbf{R}_{>0}\times\mathbf{U}(m))\ltimes(\mathbf{R}^{2m})^{*}
. Any two metrics in
\accentset{par.}{\mathbf{c}}
differ by a factor constant along
K^{\perp}
.

Proof.

The hypothesis can be expressed by saying that the only three possibly non-vanishing components of the intrinsic torsion are
\breve{\epsilon}
,
\breve{E}_{i}
and
\breve{G}_{\alpha}
, the latter two being related by (
5.1
). It is already shown in
[
25
]
that the curvature prescription yields the existence of the subclass
\accentset{par.}{\mathbf{c}}
for which we have
\breve{E}_{i}=0
and
\breve{\epsilon}=0
. But then this implies that
\breve{G}_{\alpha}=0
. Hence, the intrinsic torsion vanishes and the result follows.
∎

As a direct consequence of Proposition
4.57
, we obtain:

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},\mathbf{c},N,K)
be an almost Robinson manifold with congruence of null curves
\mathcal{K}
. Suppose that the intrinsic torsion of
(N,K)
for some (and thus any) metric
g
in
\mathbf{c}
is a section of
\not{\mathcal{G}}_{-1}^{1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,1}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,2}\cap\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{1,3}\cap(\not{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{0\times 1})_{[2(m-1)\mathrm{i}:-1]}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\breve{\gamma}_{i}=\breve{\tau}_{ij}=\breve{\sigma}_{ij}=\breve{\zeta}_{\alpha\beta}=\breve{G}^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=\breve{G}^{\tiny{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt\hbox to5.46666pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.37334pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0933pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}}}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=\breve{G}^{\circ}_{\bar{\alpha}\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle 2(m-1)\mathrm{i}\breve{E}_{\gamma}-\breve{G}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=0\,.

Then for every metric
g
in
\accentset{n.e.}{\mathbf{c}}
,
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
is a nearly Robinson manifold of Kundt type with congruence of null geodesics
\mathcal{K}
.

Denote by
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{h},\underline{J})
denote the leaf space of
\mathcal{K}
, and set
\underline{\omega}_{ij}=\underline{J}_{i}{}^{k}\underline{h}_{kj}
. Then there exists a Kundt frame
(\kappa,\theta^{i},\lambda)
such that the vertical
1
-form
\lambda
has components
\lambda_{i}
given by

\displaystyle\lambda_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\underline{\lambda}_{\ell}^{(0)}-v\frac{1}{m-1}\underline{h}^{ij}\left(\underline{\nabla}_{i}\underline{\omega}_{jk}\right)\underline{J}_{\ell}{}^{k}\,,

where
\underline{\lambda}_{i}^{(0)}
are smooth functions on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
, and
v
an affine parameter along the geodesics of
\mathcal{K}
.

5.3.
Conformal lift of almost CR structures

In this short section, we revisit the lift of almost CR structures considered in Section
4.7
. A conformal version of Proposition
4.18
can be formulated in the following terms:

Proposition 5.7
.

Let
(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\underline{H},\underline{J})
be a
(2m+1)
-dimensional oriented almost CR manifold, and
\mathcal{M}:=\mathbf{R}\times\underline{\mathcal{M}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varpi}}{{\longrightarrow}}\underline{\mathcal{M}}
be a trivial line bundle over
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
.

Let
\left((\underline{\theta}^{0},\underline{\theta}^{\alpha},\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}}),h_{\alpha\bar{\beta}},\lambda\right)
and
\left((\widehat{\underline{\theta}}{}^{0},\widehat{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha},\overline{\widehat{\underline{\theta}}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}}),\widehat{h}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}},\widehat{\lambda}\right)
be two triplets that give rise to two almost Robinson geometries
(\mathcal{M},g,N,K)
and
(\mathcal{M},\widehat{g},N,K)
as in Proposition
4.18
. Suppose
(\underline{\theta}^{0},\underline{\theta}{}^{\alpha},\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}})
and
(\widehat{\underline{\theta}}{}^{0},\widehat{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha},\overline{\widehat{\underline{\theta}}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}})
are related by (
4.12
). Then

\displaystyle\widehat{g}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{\underline{\varphi}}g\,,

if and only if
\underline{\psi}{}_{\alpha}{}^{\gamma}
is an element of
\mathbf{U}(m)
at every point, i.e.

\displaystyle\widehat{h}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{\underline{\varphi}}h_{\gamma\bar{\delta}}(\underline{\psi}^{-1}){}_{\alpha}{}^{\gamma}(\underline{\psi}^{-1}){}_{\bar{\beta}}{}^{\bar{\delta}}\,,

and
\lambda
transforms as

\displaystyle\widehat{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\lambda-\frac{1}{2}\underline{\psi}{}_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\underline{\phi}_{\beta}\underline{\theta}^{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\underline{\psi}{}_{\bar{\alpha}}{}^{\bar{\beta}}\underline{\phi}_{\bar{\beta}}\overline{\underline{\theta}}^{\bar{\alpha}}-\frac{1}{2}\underline{\phi}_{\beta}\underline{\phi}^{\beta}\underline{\theta}^{0}\,.

Again, the proof is pretty much tautological.

5.4.
Non-shearing twist-induced almost Robinson structures

Most of the results in Sections
4.7
,
4.8
,
4.9
and
4.10
can be safely formulated in the conformal setting. In particular, we give the conformal version of Proposition
4.41
below, which is a direct consequence of Proposition 5.16 of
[
25
]
:

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},\mathbf{c},N,K)
be a
(2m+2)
-dimensional conformal twist-induced almost Robinson manifold with non-shearing congruence of null geodesics
\mathcal{K}
. We then have the following properties:

(1)

For each
g\in\accentset{n.e.}{\mathbf{c}}
, there exists a unique pair
(k,\ell)
where
k
is a generator of
\mathcal{K}
and
\ell
a null vector field such that
g(k,\ell)=1
and
\kappa=g(k,\cdot)
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\kappa(k,\cdot)

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\kappa(\ell,\cdot)

\displaystyle=0\,.

In particular, the twist
\tau
of
k
is represented by
\mathrm{d}\kappa
and determines the screen bundle Hermitian form of
(N,K)
with respect to
g
, i.e. we have
(\mathrm{d}\kappa)_{ij}=\tau_{ij}=\omega_{ij}=J_{i}{}^{k}h_{jk}
.

If
(k,\ell)
and
(\widehat{k},\widehat{\ell})
be any two such pairs corresponding to metrics
g
and
\widehat{g}
in
\accentset{n.e.}{\mathbf{c}}
, with
\widehat{g}=\mathrm{e}^{\underline{\varphi}}g
for some smooth function
\underline{\varphi}
constant along
K
then,

(5.2)

\displaystyle\widehat{k}

\displaystyle=k\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{\ell}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{-\underline{\varphi}}\left(\ell+\frac{1}{2}\omega^{-1}(\cdot,\underline{\Upsilon})-\frac{1}{4}\|\underline{\Upsilon}\|^{2}_{g}k\right)\,,

where
\underline{\Upsilon}=\mathrm{d}\underline{\varphi}
.

(2)

(N,K)
induces a partially integrable contact almost CR structure
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
on the leaf space
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
of
\mathcal{K}
. In particular,
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
is equipped with a subconformal structure
\underline{\mathbf{c}}_{\underline{H},\underline{J}}
compatible with
\underline{J}
and also induced from
\mathbf{c}
, and there is a one-to-one correspondence between metrics in
\accentset{n.e.}{\mathbf{c}}
and contact
1
-forms for
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
. More specifically, for any two adapted frames
(\underline{\theta}^{0},\underline{\theta}^{\alpha},\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}})
and
(\widehat{\underline{\theta}}{}^{0},\widehat{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha},\overline{\widehat{\underline{\theta}}}{}^{\bar{\alpha}})
for
(\underline{H},\underline{J})
related by

\displaystyle\widehat{\underline{\theta}}{}^{0}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{\underline{\varphi}}\underline{\theta}^{0}\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\underline{\theta}^{\alpha}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\Upsilon}^{\alpha}\underline{\theta}^{0}\,,

up to
\mathbf{U}(m)
-transformations, and with Levi forms related by
\widehat{\underline{\mathsf{h}}}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=\mathrm{e}^{2\varphi}\underline{\mathsf{h}}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}
, the corresponding lifts in
\accentset{n.e.}{\mathbf{c}}
are given by

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle=4\,\varpi^{*}\underline{\theta}{}^{0}\,\lambda+2\,\varpi^{*}\left(\underline{\mathsf{h}}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\underline{\theta}{}^{\alpha}\,\overline{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\bar{\beta}}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{g}

\displaystyle=4\,\varpi^{*}\widehat{\underline{\theta}}{}^{0}\,\widehat{\lambda}+2\,\varpi^{*}\left(\widehat{\underline{\mathsf{h}}}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\widehat{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha}\,\overline{\widehat{\underline{\theta}}}{}^{\bar{\beta}}\right)\,,

where
